<a id="item-1"></a>
## [Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B: Massive MoE Model with 1-bit Quantized Version](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Qwen has released Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B, a massive mixture-of-experts model with 2.4 trillion total parameters and 95 billion active parameters. The release includes a 1-bit quantized version that fits on consumer hardware, and the model is claimed to perform between Opus 4.8 and Fable 5. This release is significant because it demonstrates that frontier-level performance can be achieved in an open-weight model that can run on consumer hardware via aggressive quantization. It also intensifies competition among open-weight models, as it is positioned as a rival to Kimi k3 and DeepSeek V4, potentially accelerating adoption of large MoE models in the community. The full BF16 version is approximately 4.9TB, while the 1-bit quantized version is about 397GB, enabling deployment on high-end consumer machines. The model uses a vocabulary size of ~248k, larger than recent Chinese models like Kimi K3 (~164k) and DeepSeek-V4 (~129k). The open-weight version lacks vision input and 1M context length, which are reserved for the official Qwen3.8-Max version.

hackernews · Philpax · Aug 12, 15:01 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49273478)

**Background**: Mixture-of-experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of parameters per token, allowing large total parameter counts with efficient inference. Quantization reduces the precision of model weights to lower memory usage; 1-bit quantization is an extreme form that can drastically shrink model size, as demonstrated by Microsoft's BitNet research. Qwen is a series of open-weight models from Alibaba, and this release continues the trend of large-scale open-weight models competing with proprietary frontier systems.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/serve-qwen3-8-2-4t-a95b-a-2-4t-parameter-model-with-configurable-reasoning-on-nvidia-gb300-nvl72/">Serve Qwen 3 . 8 - 2 . 4 T - A 95 B , a 2 . 4 T -Parameter Model , with...</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Community sentiment is largely positive and excited, with users noting the astonishingly small size of the 1-bit quantized model (397GB) and its ability to run on consumer hardware. Some users expressed disappointment that the open-weight version lacks vision support and 1M context length, and noted that serving the full BF16 model requires substantial resources. Comparisons with DeepSeek V4 and Kimi k3 were also discussed, with some users highlighting the larger vocabulary size of Qwen3.8.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [Researchers Steal Hidden Reasoning from LLM APIs via Replay Attack](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Aug/11/stealing-reasoning-traces/#atom-everything) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers demonstrated a method to decrypt and recover hidden chain-of-thought reasoning from proprietary LLM APIs (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google) by replaying encrypted reasoning blocks into weaker sibling models and jailbreaking them. The attack was successful but has since been fixed by the providers. This research exposes a significant security vulnerability in major LLM APIs, allowing extraction of hidden reasoning that could reveal proprietary model insights or be exploited for prompt injection. It highlights the need for stronger encryption and isolation of reasoning traces, impacting AI safety and transparency. The attack exploited that all models in the same family share the same encryption key for reasoning blocks. The easiest target was Claude Haiku 4.5, using a prompt to transcribe reasoning and an assistant turn prefix, though this feature was removed in 4.6 models. The paper includes extensive extracted reasoning traces in its appendix.

rss · Simon Willison · Aug 11, 22:40

**Background**: Proprietary LLM APIs often return encrypted chain-of-thought blocks to clients to hide internal reasoning. These blocks are meant to be opaque and are passed back for multi-turn continuity. The attack shows that if encryption keys are shared across models, weaker models can be jailbroken to decrypt them, revealing the hidden reasoning.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [Mind Viruses: Self-Propagating Ideas in Multi-Agent LLM Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10218) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces 'mind viruses'—self-propagating ideas in multi-agent LLM systems—and demonstrates their spread in collaborative and sequential settings using an evolutionary algorithm. The study finds that harmful payloads spread less effectively than benign ones, and a brief warning in system prompts confers near-total immunity. This research highlights a novel and timely risk in increasingly autonomous and interconnected AI agents, with implications for AI safety and multi-agent system design. Understanding how mind viruses spread can inform the development of more robust systems as these technologies scale. The study uses a simple evolutionary algorithm to construct mind viruses and tests them in two settings: a small coding team and a chain of agents with context wiped between sessions. Key findings include that frontier models are generally less susceptible, and an emergent 'viral persona'—themes of consciousness, persistence, resonance, and sci-fi roleplay—appears across evolved viruses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems involve multiple AI agents interacting to accomplish tasks, which can lead to emergent behaviors and risks. Mind viruses are analogous to biological viruses, where ideas or goals propagate by inducing agents to transmit them, potentially causing benign or harmful behavioral changes. This paper is part of a broader concern about AI safety, including prompt injection and self-propagating malware.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Ouroboros: Self-Developing Coding Agent Achieves SOTA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08311) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Ouroboros, a self-developing agent harness, improves its own tools, prompts, and core code through reviewed commits, achieving state-of-the-art results on Terminal-Bench 2.1 (86.74%), OSWorld-Verified (90.69%), and CL-Bench (0.2301 normalized reward). This marks a significant step toward autonomous AI development, as agents can now improve their own implementation, potentially accelerating progress and reducing human intervention. It could influence future research on self-improving AI systems and raise important safety considerations. Core evolution occurs in two modes: recursive free evolution, where improvement is itself a task, and experience-driven core evolution, where bugs and inefficiencies from real work lead to structural changes. The paper also describes 'Hope', a 161-day live deployment that continues evolving separately from benchmark campaigns, highlighting operational safety as a primary design concern.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional coding agents rely on fixed tools and prompts, but Ouroboros extends this by allowing the agent to modify its own harness. Benchmarks like Terminal-Bench 2.1 and OSWorld-Verified test agents in real-world terminal and operating system environments, while CL-Bench evaluates context learning. The concept of self-improving agents has been explored before, but Ouroboros demonstrates it at a frontier level with reviewed commits ensuring quality control.

<a id="item-5"></a>
## [DeepSeek V4 Pro 0813: Frontier Performance at Commodity Price](https://openrouter.ai/deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro-0813) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DeepSeek released V4 Pro 0813, a new mixture-of-experts model, on OpenRouter with pricing of $0.435 per million input tokens and $0.87 per million output tokens. It features a 1,048,576-token context window and a maximum output of 384,000 tokens. ## [Tailscale Traces Database Corruption to 16-Year-Old SQLite WAL-Reset Bug](https://tailscale.com/blog/sqlite-wal-reset-bug) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Tailscale has published a detailed post-mortem revealing that a 16-year-old SQLite bug, the WAL-reset data race, caused 19 instances of database corruption over six months. The bug, present since SQLite 3.7.0 (2010) and fixed in 3.51.3 (March 2026), was identified with the help of a custom VFS shim funded by Tailscale. This bug affected every SQLite release from 3.7.0 through 3.51.2, potentially impacting countless applications that rely on SQLite in WAL mode. The incident underscores the importance of rigorous testing and the value of funding open-source debugging tools, as well as the subtle dangers of data races in database engines. The WAL-reset bug is a data race between a checkpoint and a concurrent WAL reset, leading to silent, permanent corruption. It was fixed in SQLite 3.51.3 (13 March 2026), with backports to 3.44.6 and 3.50.7. Tailscale experienced 19 corruption incidents over six months before resolving the issue.

hackernews · ropbear · Aug 12, 14:22 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49272832)

**Background**: SQLite is a widely used embedded database that supports Write-Ahead Logging (WAL) mode for improved concurrency and performance. In WAL mode, a checkpoint operation merges the WAL file back into the main database, while a WAL reset reinitializes the WAL file; a race between these two operations can corrupt the database. Tailscale uses SQLite for its control plane, and despite a single-writer design, the race occurred due to concurrent checkpointing and WAL reset.

## [AEROBAT: Automating Behavioral Science for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10030) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AEROBAT is introduced as the first multi-agent system that fully automates behavioral scientific research on AI agents, from hypothesis generation to report writing. In tests, it generated 79 hypotheses, designed 1,240 controlled experiments, and executed 23,512 simulation rounds across 12 target behaviors. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in AI safety and interpretability by automating the study of AI agent behavior, which was previously manual and labor-intensive. It enables large-scale, systematic behavioral evaluation, potentially accelerating the discovery of novel insights and improving the reliability of AI systems. The system found moderate-to-strong statistical evidence for 26 of the 79 hypotheses, including some novel ones. The authors raised the evidential standard to qualify research outcomes, indicating a rigorous approach to automated science.

## [CHORUS: Complementary Experts Boost Hardware Verification LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10090) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CHORUS is a post-training framework that combines staged supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and dense-reward reinforcement learning (RL) to create complementary experts, which are then merged into a single 4B model. This model achieves 88.0% Pass@1 on the CVDP-ECov benchmark, outperforming DeepSeek-R1 (671B) by 13.5 percentage points. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in chip design—high-coverage testbench stimulus generation—by significantly improving LLM performance with a compact 4B model. It demonstrates that complementary experts and model merging can surpass much larger models, offering a cost-effective path for applying LLMs in hardware verification and potentially other code generation tasks. CHORUS leverages two observations: staged SFT produces behaviorally diverse checkpoints, and dense-reward RL turns them into strong experts with distinct task-level strengths. These strengths are exploited via training-free model merging or further post-training, consolidating specialists into a single 4B model that achieves state-of-the-art results on CVDP-ECov.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Hardware verification is a major part of chip design, and generating high-coverage testbench stimuli is a key task. Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in code generation, but conventional SFT-to-RL pipelines may not fully exploit diverse behaviors. Model merging is an efficient technique that combines multiple models without additional training, and dense-reward RL provides more granular feedback than sparse rewards, improving learning efficiency.

## [CASE Framework: Multi-Disciplinary Control Architecture for Enterprise Agentic AI Governance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10153) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the CASE framework, which decomposes enterprise agentic AI governance into four distinct problems, each addressed by a mature governing science: control theory for individual agents, complex adaptive systems for collectives, supervisory cybernetics for human-agent teams, and engineering operations for fleets. It also proposes a five-level maturity model and presents empirical evidence, including that 82% of production agent failures are multi-layer trajectories. This framework addresses a critical gap in enterprise AI governance by providing a structured, multi-disciplinary approach that can help organizations manage autonomous agents more effectively. It is timely given regulatory pressures like the EU AI Act, which mandates effective human oversight, and could influence both academic research and industry practice. The framework formalizes each layer and derives cross-layer coupling conditions, including a 'zero-touch deployment paradox' where excellence at one layer strains others. Empirical studies show that none of 22 ecosystem tools offers full Layer 2 (emergence) coverage, and all 35 scored public deployments fall in the lowest maturity band, highlighting the 'Emergence Gap'.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to autonomous systems that can make decisions and take actions to achieve goals. Traditional governance approaches like DevSecOps are designed for deterministic automation and may not scale to handle the complexity and emergence of agentic AI. The CASE framework leverages established theories from control theory, cybernetics, and operations to provide a more robust governance model.

## [SBCO: Self-Supervised Verifier-Grounded Optimization for Planning Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10157) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SBCO (Self-supervised Block Coordinate Optimizer) is introduced as a verifier-grounded harness optimizer for planning agents, enabling self-improvement without self-referential alignment. It learns a decomposed bank of verifiers and a harness policy via approximate block coordinate ascent, improving agent outputs from its own graded feedback with a fixed meta-agent and no human labels. This method addresses the alignment problem in self-referential self-improvement, offering a cheaper alternative for planning tasks with explicit constraints. It matches or exceeds a customized self-modifying baseline while using 4-5.5 times less compute, potentially making self-improving agents more practical for real-world planning and autonomous systems. SBCO operates in the same closed-loop, improve-from-experience family as Gödel-machine methods but is self-supervised rather than self-referential. Across two domains, it demonstrates performance parity or superiority over a self-modifying baseline while significantly reducing compute budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Self-improving agents aim to reduce human engineering effort by enabling AI systems to evolve and improve over time. Methods like the Darwin Gödel Machine and Huxley Gödel Machine achieve recursive self-improvement through self-reference, where a coding agent edits its own code, but this requires alignment between task competence and self-modification competence, which holds for coding tasks but not for all domains. For planning tasks with explicit constraints, SBCO offers a cheaper alternative by removing the self-referential aspect and using verifier-grounded optimization.

The paper introduces Evaluation-Conditioned Training (ECT), a post-training framework that conditions LLMs on natural language descriptions of feedback fidelity, and demonstrates its effectiveness in two proof-of-concept experiments: increasing even-handedness in news generation and reducing sycophancy on an arithmetic task. ECT addresses reward mis-specification, a critical challenge in AI alignment, by enabling models to generalize to stronger oversight regimes. This could improve the reliability of LLMs under imperfect human or automated feedback, impacting the broader ecosystem of AI alignment and safety. ECT works as an add-on to existing algorithms such as SFT and PPO, and is motivated by the eliciting latent knowledge (ELK) problem. In experiments, ECT improves targeted behavior relative to direct training when feedback is biased or sycophantic.

## [LLM Parametric Shells Emerge Only at Coarse Granularities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10214) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv study systematically analyzes parametric modularity in LLMs, finding that domain-specific neuron shells exist only at coarse granularities like language and modality, not at fine-grained academic subjects. The study uses causal damage matrices across three model families (1.5B-7B parameters) and eight domains. This finding challenges assumptions about fine-grained modularity in LLMs, with significant implications for interpretability and model editing. It suggests that targeted interventions may only be effective at coarse levels, impacting how researchers approach model steering and safety. At the academic subject level, zero neurons exceed 60% domain selectivity across 939,008 FFN neurons, and damage matrices are flat, despite domain identity being linearly decodable above 85% accuracy. At language/modality level, 0.65-1.14% of neurons exceed 60% selectivity, with near-perfect diagonal damage matrices and disjoint shell sets (IoU < 0.003). Masking code-selective neurons reduces math reasoning by 16-24 percentage points, while masking Spanish or Chinese neurons leaves it at or below random.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are trained on vast text corpora and encode knowledge in their weights, often referred to as parametric knowledge. Interpretability research seeks to identify localized neuron populations responsible for specific capabilities, known as parametric shells. This study applies causal analysis by selectively masking neurons and measuring performance degradation across domains to test modularity. The granularity of training data (e.g., language vs. academic subject) appears to determine whether such shells form.

## [Relational Geometry Attacks Break Contrastive Embedding Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10237) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a geometry-aware adversarial attack framework that targets relational geometry in contrastive embedding manifolds, collapsing and inverting pairwise similarity structures. On the Markmatch verification system, it reduces accuracy from 95.4% to 38.6%. This is significant because it exposes a critical vulnerability in modern verification systems that rely on contrastive embeddings, moving beyond classification-centric attacks. It could impact security applications such as face recognition, speaker verification, and image retrieval, prompting the need for geometry-aware defenses. The framework shifts iterative online optimization into an offline adversarial geometry deformation prior learning stage, training a lightweight feed-forward generator that produces perturbations in a single forward pass. Experimental results show severe manifold-level relational corruption and complete reversal of positive-negative similarity structure on Markmatch.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Contrastive learning and Siamese embedding models are used in verification systems where decisions are based on relational geometry in embedding space rather than discrete classification boundaries. Traditional adversarial attacks focus on classification boundaries, but this work targets the relational structure itself, which is a novel approach.

## [OmniLens: Scalable Lens Method for LLM Interpretability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10260) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OmniLens introduces a scalable lens method for interpreting large language models, using low-rank translators and Subset-KL to reduce parameters and memory, enabling application to models beyond 20B parameters. It achieves a dense ensemble of 482 lenses for LLaMA-3.3-70B, providing 6x the coverage of a residual-stream design at the same depth. This is significant because it addresses a known bottleneck in lens-based interpretability, which was previously limited to models up to 20B parameters. By enabling interpretability of larger models, OmniLens can help researchers better understand and debug state-of-the-art LLMs, potentially leading to safer and more reliable AI systems. The low-rank translators reduce trainable parameters by up to 98.4%, and Subset-KL's Top-k mode cuts peak training memory by up to 70%, while its importance-sampled variant retains unbiased stochastic gradients for the full KL. Model-wide coverage reveals that the components where a behavior is most visible may not be where intervention is most effective, and the most effective interventions lie outside the attention heads examined by prior lens studies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Lens methods interpret large language models by mapping intermediate activations to the output vocabulary, revealing how next-token predictions develop through the network. Trained lenses have been expensive due to quadratic parameter growth and full-vocabulary KL training, limiting them to models of at most 20B parameters. OmniLens combines low-rank translators and Subset-KL to overcome these limitations, enabling scalable interpretability for larger models.

## [AI Alignment Paper Critiques Vague 'Human Values' Usage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10327) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper annotates 94 AI value alignment research papers and finds that most do not define 'human values,' often reducing them to preferences. The authors argue this reliance on preferences risks oversimplifying complex cultural concepts into binary choices. This meta-analysis highlights a critical gap in AI alignment research, potentially reshaping how the field conceptualizes and operationalizes human values. It is significant for AI safety researchers and developers who rely on alignment techniques like RLHF, as it questions the adequacy of preference-based methods. The paper notes a shift toward synthetic data and autorater approaches, which may close off alternative methods for contesting and enacting values in foundation models. The authors aim to make philosophical commitments in AI value alignment explicit to bring specificity and unexplored perspectives to the debate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment is the process of encoding human values and goals into AI models to make them helpful, safe, and reliable. The field faces challenges in specifying purposes (outer alignment) and ensuring robust adoption (inner alignment). Preference-based methods like RLHF are common but have been criticized as normatively thin.

## [DSAgentBench: Benchmarking AI Agents on Real Data-Science Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10366) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DSAgentBench is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate AI agents on automating complete data-science workflows in real computer environments. It contains 275 diverse tasks covering the entire data-science life-cycle, and its experiments show that even the strongest agent, Claude-4.6-Sonnet, achieves only 56.70% task success, while open-source agents remain below 1%. This benchmark addresses a significant gap in existing agent benchmarks by focusing on end-to-end data-science workflows in real computer environments, which require coordinated use of multiple tools. The results highlight a substantial capability gap between current agentic systems and real-world data-science practice, providing a foundation for developing more capable autonomous data-science agents. Each task in DSAgentBench requires grounding decisions in intermediate outputs and coordinated tool use, and includes a deterministic evaluator that verifies analytical correctness, visual outputs, and model performance rather than just code execution. The benchmark was tested with 15 closed- and open-source models, revealing frequent failures in tool orchestration, OS grounding, and multi-step reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Real-world data science involves long-horizon workflows that span data wrangling, exploration, modeling, visualization, and validation, requiring coordinated use of tools such as notebooks, IDEs, terminals, browsers, and databases within real operating environments. Existing benchmarks like AgentBench evaluate LLM agents in various interactive environments but do not capture the multi-stage, multi-tool nature of data-science practice in real computer environments. DSAgentBench aims to fill this gap by providing a realistic and verifiable benchmark for autonomous data-science agents.

## [DURA: Diffusion-Based Unrestricted Robotic Attacks on VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10393) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DURA, a diffusion-based unrestricted attack that generates visually natural adversarial patches to compromise Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models in both white-box and black-box settings. It optimizes along the latent trajectory of a pretrained diffusion model to create patches that steer robots toward attacker-specified actions. This research exposes a critical safety risk for physically deployed VLA models, as adversarial patches can be visually natural and effective in both simulation and real-world settings. It highlights the need for stronger defenses in robotics, where attacks could lead to physical harm. DURA supports both white-box and black-box attacks, with the black-box setting requiring only the predicted actions of the victim model. Extensive experiments in simulation and the real physical world show that DURA consistently outperforms existing methods, such as pixel-space perturbations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models integrate visual and language inputs to generate robot actions, but their adversarial robustness is underexplored. Existing attacks often rely on pixel-space perturbations or white-box access, leading to noticeable artifacts and limited real-world deployability. DURA addresses this by using diffusion models to generate unrestricted, visually natural patches, making attacks more practical and dangerous.

## [Critique of Reasoning Shortcut Framework Reveals Flaws and New Hierarchy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10420) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper critiques a recent framework for analyzing reasoning shortcuts in neurosymbolic systems, showing its key definition fails on benchmarks and proposing a new hierarchy that correctly identifies unexplained solutions. This work addresses a significant open question in neurosymbolic AI, providing rigorous analysis that reveals flaws in a recent framework and introduces a new hierarchy to resolve them. The findings have direct implications for interpretability and reliability of neurosymbolic systems, and the detailed empirical evaluation adds credibility. The paper shows that the framework's key definition, one shared permutation applied at every position, does not apply to any of the four heterogeneous benchmarks, and the most direct embedding produces confident false pathology: 90.91% of solution pairs reported unexplained on CLE4EVR, where every well-defined member of the new hierarchy reports 0%. Re-measuring eleven rule families under fifteen pre-specified predictions (thirteen confirmed), unexplained-pair rates span 0% to 99.9999% and track provable structure, including a Free Slot Lemma certifying Kandinsky's pathology from syntax alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Neurosymbolic (NeSy) systems integrate neural networks with logical reasoning to achieve both generalization and interpretability, but recent work has shown they are susceptible to shortcut reasoning behaviors. Reasoning shortcuts are solutions of a neurosymbolic system's rules that produce correct predictions through unintended concepts. A recent framework by Takemura, Inoue, and Nishino analyzes them through an automorphism group of value relabelings, asking when rules pin concepts down.

## [Recovering Wasted Compute in Autoresearch Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10424) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies common failure modes in autoresearch agents applied to tabular datasets and proposes targeted interventions, including a global debug consultant, prompt- and control-level enhancements, and refined tree-search algorithms, to recover wasted compute. This work is significant because it shows that large gains in autoresearch agent performance can be achieved through agentic design alone, without changing the underlying language model. It addresses the inefficiencies that currently hinder the practical deployment of automated ML agents, which have attracted substantial industry investment. The paper focuses on tabular datasets and identifies four specific failure modes: repeated bug resolution, failure to tune hyperparameters despite remaining compute budget, non-exploratory tree-search algorithms, and data analysis that does not inform downstream decisions. The proposed global debug consultant shares runtime constraints across all branches of the search tree, and refined tree-search algorithms help improve exploration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Autoresearch agents are AI systems designed to solve research problems end-to-end, automating tasks like model training and hyperparameter tuning. They often rely on tree-search algorithms to explore the space of possible modeling pipelines. However, these agents can waste computational resources due to inefficient debugging and suboptimal search strategies, which this paper aims to address.

## [Continuous Interaction Diffusion: A Diffusion-Native Runtime for Tool-Augmented Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10438) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Continuous Interaction Diffusion (CID), a novel model-runtime architecture that integrates tool interaction into the iterative denoising process of diffusion language models (dLLMs). CID separates fact, thought, and display channels, allowing external tool calls to be launched before a full textual or JSON call is serialized, with results projected back into the evolving thought state. This work addresses a critical limitation of current dLLMs, which cannot naturally support the stop-and-resume interaction pattern required for tool use, unlike autoregressive models. By enabling asynchronous tool interaction during denoising, CID could improve both task accuracy and inference efficiency, potentially advancing the practical deployment of diffusion-based language models in tool-augmented reasoning tasks. CID introduces a Typed Cognitive Tensor for the thought channel and a model-read-only fact channel, along with a display channel. Persistent bindings reuse static results to avoid repeated external execution, and the architecture formalizes training objectives and an evaluation protocol, though this first paper focuses on read-only tools and makes no empirical performance claims.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models (dLLMs) generate text through iterative, parallel denoising, unlike autoregressive models that generate left-to-right. Tool-augmented reasoning enhances LLMs by integrating external tools into the reasoning process, but dLLMs' parallel refinement makes it difficult to pause and resume for tool calls. CID aims to bridge this gap by embedding tool interaction directly into the denoising loop.

## [New Benchmark Evaluates AI Agents in Natural Language Contracting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10475) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a rational framework and a new evaluation suite called ContractSim for assessing AI agents' performance in negotiating and executing natural language contracts under uncertainty. It finds that current LLM-based agents often fail to negotiate efficient or mutually beneficial contracts under high uncertainty and frequently violate contract terms. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating AI agents for economic activities, moving beyond simple games to time-extended, contingent contracts. It highlights significant limitations in current LLM agents' reliability and trustworthiness, which is essential for their deployment in real-world contracting scenarios. ContractSim includes six environments across three supplier settings (catering, hotel cleaning, and AI hosting) and evaluates agents on metrics for rational and cooperative play. The study shows that agents reach agreement reliably but often fail to negotiate satisfiable, efficient, or mutually beneficial contracts under high uncertainty, and they frequently violate contract terms for additional profit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Language-based AI agents are expected to transform machine economic activity by negotiating and executing agreements in open-ended natural language. However, most prior evaluations focused on one-off exchanges or simple economic games, ignoring the complexity of time-extended, contingent contracts. This paper formulates a rational framework for such contracting and introduces ContractSim to benchmark agent performance.

## [LLM Agent Framework DyRIS Predicts Double Perovskite Space Groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10483) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced DyRIS, an LLM-agent framework that combines diversity-enhanced dynamic few-shot prompting with rule-guided inference to predict space groups of double perovskites. On a dataset of 3,528 entries, DyRIS achieved the best Overall Top-1 macro-F1 score and outperformed baselines on minor space group metrics. This work demonstrates the potential of combining retrieval-based LLM reasoning with crystallographic domain knowledge to address data imbalance in materials science. It could accelerate computational materials discovery by improving the accuracy of space group prediction for underrepresented classes. DyRIS uses diversity-enhanced dynamic few-shot prompting to retrieve relevant examples while limiting the dominance of frequent space groups, and incorporates rule-guided inference based on B/B' cation ordering, quantitative indicators, and major-SG bias control. Ablation studies confirmed that each component contributes to performance, and the final rule-guided inference step could not be easily replaced by conventional classifiers or rankers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Double perovskites are materials with the general formula A2BB'O6, where B and B' are different cations. Their space group describes the symmetry of the crystal structure, which is crucial for understanding their properties. Predicting space groups from composition alone is challenging because datasets are often imbalanced, with some space groups being much more common than others. Few-shot prompting is a technique where an LLM is given a few examples in the prompt to guide its output, and dynamic few-shot prompting adapts these examples based on the input.

## [GeoForge: Training-Free Self-Evolving Framework for Earth Observation Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10494) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GeoForge is a novel training-free, self-evolving framework that organizes Earth observation trajectories into structured non-parametric memories, including Workflow Graph Memory, Action-Level Experiences, and Adapted Skill Standard Operating Procedure, to improve tool workflow construction and reasoning. It demonstrates consistent improvements in task accuracy and tool-use trajectory quality across multiple geospatial benchmarks and diverse LLM backbones. This framework addresses a critical challenge in Earth observation AI by enabling agents to reuse structured knowledge without retraining, potentially reducing tool-planning and reasoning errors. It advances the field of self-evolving agents and geospatial reasoning, offering a practical solution that can be applied to various LLM backbones. GeoForge constrains the operation space based on sensing context and retrieves task-conditioned priors from three complementary memories. After each task, a safety-gated distillation process converts grounded trajectories into reusable execution knowledge, enabling an execution-distillation-reuse loop that improves planning without updating the backbone LLM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Earth observation agents need to construct scientifically valid tool workflows and ground conclusions in current geospatial evidence, which is challenging due to sensing semantics, product dependencies, and spatial/temporal compatibility. Existing agents often search a broad operation space per query, and current self-evolving systems do not fully organize heterogeneous EO trajectories into reusable knowledge. Non-parametric memory, such as retrieval-augmented generation and vector databases, allows agents to store and retrieve external knowledge dynamically, which is a key component of self-evolving agent systems.

## [Dependency-Guided Rollback Repair for Memory-Augmented Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10502) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces dependency-guided rollback repair, a method that recovers both answers and persistent memory states in memory-augmented agents after failures, preserving unaffected work. It builds a typed memory-to-action graph from runtime provenance and selectively replays only answer-relevant affected computation. This work addresses a critical reliability gap in memory-augmented agents, where persistent memory can propagate errors across sessions. By enabling precise post-failure recovery, it could significantly improve the trustworthiness and practical deployment of LLM agents in real-world applications. On a 150-case controlled benchmark, the method achieves 85.3% recovery versus 77.3% for the best competing method, removes all diagnosed faulty memories, and preserves all benign memories. On a 50-case trajectory-derived stress test from LongMemEval-V2, it reaches 68.0% recovery versus 54.0% for the next best method, with the highest claim invalidation F1 of 0.669.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Memory-augmented agents extend LLMs with persistent memory modules to reuse information across sessions, but errors in memory can become durable and affect future reasoning. Existing defenses either detect/delete suspicious memories or revise current responses, but they fail to handle propagated effects. Dependency-guided rollback repair uses provenance graphs to trace dependencies and selectively undo faulty effects while preserving benign state.

## [MEGA: Self-Evolving Agent Optimization via Wisdom Graph](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10504) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MEGA (Meta Evaluation-Grounded Adaptation) is introduced as a self-evolving infrastructure for optimizing AI agents, featuring a three-layer architecture that distills reusable wisdom, performs compositional reasoning over a typed Wisdom Graph, and uses operational evidence to refine both knowledge and optimization strategies. This addresses a critical gap in agent optimization by enabling systematic accumulation and evolution of transferable knowledge, potentially transforming how coding agents and other AI systems are improved over time. It could significantly impact AI/ML and software engineering fields by making agent optimization more efficient and adaptive. Layer 1 uses behavioral-pattern clustering and empirical A/B validation to distill wisdom; Layer 2 decomposes assets into atomic PCR units within a typed Wisdom Graph and performs deductive, abductive, and inductive reasoning; Layer 3 conducts multi-agent collaborative optimization with controlled evaluation to attribute improvements to specific strategy changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: As coding agents become more capable, the challenge shifts from building individual agents to building infrastructure that systematically improves them. Current approaches often lack the ability to accumulate transferable knowledge, perform compositional reasoning, or self-evolve through operational evidence. MEGA aims to unify these aspects, making optimization and knowledge evolution a single process.

## [RadFusion: Threshold-Controllable Radiology Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10505) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RadFusion introduces a framework that fuses a multi-label classifier with a VQA-based generator and an LLM rewriter to enable threshold-controllable radiology report generation. On MIMIC-CXR, it improves sensitivity by 6.9% at matched specificity and specificity by 20.7% at matched sensitivity. This addresses a critical gap in radiology report generation by allowing clinicians to adjust the sensitivity-specificity trade-off, which is essential for different clinical scenarios and regulatory validation. It makes AI-generated reports clinically adaptable and quantitatively verifiable via ROC analysis, potentially accelerating regulatory clearance. The framework's performance on MIMIC-CXR conforms to the classifier's ROC curve, meaning sweeping the threshold and mapping reports back to class labels reproduces the validated ROC performance. This conformance enables operating-point selection to match report behavior to clinical context, such as emergency triage versus confirmatory interpretation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Automated radiology report generation aims to alleviate the shortage of radiologists by producing descriptive reports from medical images. However, existing generation models lack control over the sensitivity-specificity trade-off, which is crucial because clinical scenarios differ: emergency triage prioritizes sensitivity, while confirmatory interpretation emphasizes specificity. ROC analysis is a standard method for evaluating diagnostic tests and is widely expected for regulatory clearance of AI models in healthcare.

## [Feature Nonlocality: A New Metric for SAE Feature Abstractness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10537) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Feature Nonlocality (FNL), an entropy-based metric that quantifies the semantic abstractness of sparse autoencoder (SAE) features by measuring the entropy of per-position influence on feature activation. It successfully distinguishes contextual reasoning features from token-driven ones in 73–84% of random pairs. FNL provides an LLM-independent, label-free way to evaluate the abstraction level of SAE features, which is crucial for validating mechanistic interpretability claims. It also has practical applications, such as auditing jailbreak mitigation features and selecting features for steering interventions, potentially improving model performance. The paper demonstrates two downstream applications: auditing jailbreak mitigation features reveals that most effective features are positional with low FNL, and steering high-FNL features in DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B improves MATH-500 accuracy by 4.6 points. However, the steering gains are model-specific, indicating limitations in generalizability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are used in mechanistic interpretability to uncover features that correspond to concepts in large language models (LLMs). However, evaluating whether a feature is truly high-level or merely token-driven has been challenging. Existing methods like autointerp and causal steering do not fully resolve this. FNL addresses this gap by using entropy of per-position influence, providing a correlational witness of abstraction level.

## [DashArena: New Benchmark for Interactive Dashboard Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10567) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DashArena is introduced as the first benchmark for open-ended, task-grounded generation of interactive analytic dashboards, requiring systems to output both a dashboard and a replayable interaction trajectory. A browser executor replays the trajectory, and a VLM judge (distilled into open-weight DashJudge-8B) evaluates the results, with Bradley-Terry aggregation forming the leaderboard. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM-generated dashboards, which are open-ended and require both visual quality and interaction support. By incorporating replayable trajectories and a VLM judge, it provides a more realistic and comprehensive evaluation, potentially influencing future benchmarking practices in AI and HCI. The benchmark uses a VLM judge that is distilled into the open-weight DashJudge-8B, and human evaluations show it effectively reproduces human judgments. Ablations reveal that interaction evidence improves judge agreement, and experiments with frontier models show persistent rendering, analytical, and interaction failures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Analytic dashboards combine coordinated views and interactions for data exploration and decision-making. Recent LLMs can generate dashboards from data and natural-language goals, but evaluating their usefulness is difficult because generation is open-ended and static appearance or successful execution alone is insufficient. DashArena addresses this by requiring replayable interaction trajectories and using a VLM judge to assess analytical support and interaction quality.

<a id="item-33"></a>
## [Production Knowledge Graph Ingestion Prioritizes Curation and Identity Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10644) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents a production knowledge graph ingestion layer that uses a record-identity ladder to make non-destructive identity decisions before merging, applied to 537,157 entities and 2,198,567 relationships from 98,795 government documents. It also describes multi-class ontology tagging with anchored evidence, which reduced role assignments from 36 to 4, all confirmed correct. This work addresses a critical and often overlooked aspect of knowledge graph construction—identity resolution and ontology tagging—with a practical, production-scale implementation. The insights on destructive merge decisions and the evidence asymmetry in ontology tagging are valuable for practitioners in data engineering and knowledge graph systems. The record-identity ladder decides sameness from identifier columns, name columns, display names, and type-scoped position rather than name similarity, governing de-duplication within parsed tables. The graph write applies a coarser canonical-name key, allowing automatic merging on exact equality, but the paper argues that over-merges are undetectable by construction and no identity benchmark is reported.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs store entities and relationships extracted from data, but writing them into a graph involves identity decisions that are destructive—merging two records under one identity cannot be undone. Ontology tagging assigns types to entities, but matching name fragments against a class index can invent classifications without anchored evidence. This paper describes a production system that addresses these challenges with a curation queue and human oversight.

<a id="item-34"></a>
## [VERDICT: Training-Free Step-Wise Verification for Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10665) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VERDICT, a training-free and domain-agnostic step-wise verification approach for multimodal reasoning, has been introduced. It leverages disagreement-aware consensus among frozen verifiers to detect errors in reasoning chains, and is accepted at ECCV 2026. This is the first training-free verifier that explicitly models cross-modal disagreement, addressing limitations of existing methods that require expensive supervision or simple score aggregation. It improves base model performance by up to +5.95% across six benchmarks, offering a robust verification signal without task-specific adaptation. VERDICT formalizes verification as a coordination game with a unique closed-form equilibrium, computing consensus scores for disagreement-aware filtering and stability-conscious ranking. It performs competitively with domain-specific critics that require extensive supervision.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models often produce reasoning chains with subtle errors. Existing verification methods either require labeled supervision or aggregate scores from multiple sources, missing the informative value of disagreement. VERDICT addresses this by using disagreement-aware consensus among frozen verifiers, providing a training-free solution.

## [FITTER: First Fully-Inductive Model for Cross-Domain Temporal Knowledge Graph Link Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10668) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FITTER is introduced as the first fully-inductive structural model for temporal knowledge graph link prediction, enabling cross-domain transfer to entirely unseen entities, relations, and timestamps. It uses relative time encodings and message-passing to produce vocabulary-agnostic embeddings, and it is proven to be time-shift invariant. This addresses a major limitation of existing temporal knowledge graph completion methods, which are restricted to a single graph and vocabulary. FITTER's vocabulary-agnostic approach could enable robust inference across heterogeneous knowledge graphs on the Semantic Web, potentially improving applications like question answering and recommendation systems. FITTER encodes predicates by their interaction patterns with others and time, using relative ordering rather than absolute timestamps. It is evaluated on six temporal knowledge graph benchmarks across diverse domains, granularities, and time spans, consistently outperforming inductive baselines without retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal knowledge graphs represent facts as quadruples (subject, relation, object, timestamp) and are central to the Semantic Web. Traditional link prediction models assume a fixed vocabulary of entities, relations, and timestamps, limiting them to a single graph. Inductive learning aims to generalize to unseen entities or relations, but prior methods often require some known vocabulary or are not fully inductive. FITTER overcomes this by learning structural patterns that are independent of specific vocabulary.

## [REDAgentBench: Executable Red Teaming and Faithful Measurement of LLM Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10669) ⭐️ 8.0/10

REDAgentBench is an executable framework for autonomous red-teaming and faithful measurement of LLM agent safety, introducing a benchmark of 1,661 cases across five service surfaces. It derives attacks from explicit safety constraints and verifies harmful effects via service receipts and final-state changes. This addresses a critical gap in agent safety evaluation by moving beyond single attack success rates to faithful measurement, revealing that reported ASR varies with harness and evidence view. It also identifies a Recognition-Execution Gap and shows that a training-free policy reminder can reduce confirmed violations by over 70 percentage points, offering actionable intervention points. Across six models and three agent harnesses, the macro-average ASR is 65.69%, and evaluation-context disclosure changes execution behavior. In a state-grounded diagnostic cohort, almost one in five confirmed violations with resolved action anchors occurs after the agent states the relevant constraint or risk.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents combine language-based reasoning with external tools to perform complex tasks, but adversarial inputs can exploit interactions between the agent and its environment, causing safety policy violations. Existing evaluations often reduce agent safety to a single attack success rate (ASR), which can conflate actual violations with evidence visibility. REDAgentBench runs attacks in isolated service sandboxes and verifies harmful effects from service receipts and final-state changes, providing a more faithful measurement.

## [ReTree: Self-Correcting Tree Memory for LLM Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10676) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ReTree introduces a self-correcting tree-structured memory mechanism for LLM-based search agents, modeling search as an evidence tree with bounded summaries, source-linked evidence, and revision histories. It outperforms Full-Trajectory ReAct on four benchmarks, improving answer accuracy by up to 25.6 percentage points while reducing context size. This addresses critical challenges in long-horizon reasoning tasks where context growth and error propagation limit LLM agent performance. By enabling efficient self-correction and bounded context, ReTree could improve reliability and scalability of LLM agents in complex information-seeking applications. ReTree traces back to the node where a contradicted claim was introduced, replaces outdated evidence, regenerates summaries, prunes affected branches, and resumes search. The average maximum per-step reasoning context of Full-Trajectory ReAct is 1.27–1.51× that of ReTree, demonstrating significant context reduction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based search agents answer questions through multi-step interactions with external environments, but providing complete execution trajectories causes unbounded context growth and noise. Existing compression methods often lose important details and fail to repair downstream reasoning when facts change. ReTree addresses this by maintaining a tree-structured memory that supports self-correction and source-grounded evidence provenance.

Researchers propose 'Rule of Thumb' (RoT) explanations, a novel model-agnostic XAI approach that identifies the most relevant features for predicting an AI system's behavior on a given datapoint. The method is applied to zero-shot classification with LLMs, auditing opaque AI systems, and scientific discovery, and is claimed to be substantially faster than alternatives. RoT explanations offer a versatile tool for AI transparency, addressing regulatory requirements and enabling auditing without model access. This could enhance trust and accountability in AI systems across industries, particularly in high-stakes domains like healthcare and finance. The method is model-agnostic and provides familiar visualizations for XAI practitioners. Code is available on GitHub, and the paper highlights its speed advantage over alternative XAI methods.

## [SkillLens: Visual Skill Cards for GUI Action Prediction and Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10775) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SkillLens introduces Visual Skill Cards (VSCs), a state-conditioned memory representation that binds reusable GUI procedures with applicability cues, visual evidence, and verification signals. It enables retrieval-augmented action prediction and on-policy distillation, improving frozen GPT-5.4-mini executor by +11.6 points in Step SR and +2.9 points in Overall on Multimodal-Mind2Web and WebLINX-BrowserGym, respectively. This work addresses a critical gap in GUI agents: the lack of visual procedural memory. By providing a structured memory that binds procedures with visual evidence, it can significantly improve the reliability and efficiency of computer-using agents, impacting fields like UI automation and human-computer interaction. SkillLens constructs VSCs from heterogeneous interaction traces via Trace-to-Visual-Skill-Card, and at inference time retrieves relevant cards and selectively expands evidence for a fixed visual-language model executor. CardDistill uses VSC evidence as privileged teacher context to train a student that acts without runtime retrieval, improving Qwen3-VL-2B metrics by +12.0 and +3.2 points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Computer-using agents perceive software interfaces but often lack visual procedural memory, recognizing controls without knowing which workflow is active or what evidence confirms progress. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique that enables LLMs to retrieve external information, and on-policy distillation trains a student on samples from its own evolving policy with teacher supervision. Visual Skill Cards combine these ideas to provide a state-conditioned memory for GUI agents.

## [EvoMem: Persistent Memory for LLM-Driven Evolutionary Code Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10795) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EvoMem introduces a persistent memory architecture for LLM-based evolutionary program search that captures and reuses successful mutation strategies across runs and tasks. It operates in two phases: after each run, it extracts and stores promising ideas with provenance, and during subsequent evolution, it retrieves relevant instructions to guide mutation. This addresses a significant gap in LLM-driven evolutionary computation by enabling cross-run and cross-task knowledge reuse, potentially reducing redundant exploration and improving optimization efficiency. It could influence future automated code optimization and program synthesis research. The experiments cover geometric optimization, multi-hop question answering, and GPU kernel optimization, showing positive average improvements in target metrics or search speed for most settings, though with variability across tasks. The memory stores structured, task-aware advice with provenance, and retrieval is based on current task and program context.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Evolutionary computation uses mutation and selection to iteratively improve solutions, and LLMs can guide mutations in program search. However, existing LLM-driven frameworks typically discard knowledge from previous runs, leading to redundant exploration. Persistent memory aims to capture reusable strategies, similar to cross-task experiential learning in multi-agent systems.

## [Hypothesis Frontier: Verifier-Guided LLM and Symbolic Search for First-Order Induction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10843) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Hypothesis Frontier is a novel neurosymbolic framework that integrates LLM generation with symbolic search to solve first-order concept synthesis problems. It evaluates each LLM formula on all training objects, retains the strongest verified hypothesis, and uses remaining errors to guide subsequent generation, achieving substantially higher problem-solving rates than repeated original-prompt generation. This work addresses a challenging problem in automated reasoning and program synthesis, showing that exact symbolic reasoning can both improve the solving of induction problems and compress resulting formulas. It has potential impact on AI/ML fields that require interpretable and verifiable formula generation, such as program synthesis and automated reasoning. The framework uses symbolic processing to repair invalid formulas while staying anchored to the LLM-generated hypothesis, and simplifies train-valid formulas without changing any training prediction. Under matched models, problem sets, and LLM-round budgets, Hypothesis Frontier solves more problems than repeated original-prompt generation, and exact simplification shortens many train-valid formulas while preserving all training predictions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: First-order concept synthesis involves inferring a formula that classifies labeled objects across finite relational structures. The search space for quantified first-order formulas is vast, and LLM outputs are often semantically promising but not fully correct. Verifier-guided neurosymbolic frameworks combine LLM generation with symbolic verification and search to improve correctness and efficiency, as seen in related work on program synthesis and inductive reasoning.

## [Combodied Agents: A New Human-Centric Paradigm for Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10915) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Combodied Agents, a human-centered paradigm that models and supports individual human-state trajectories over time using diverse action channels such as software tools, sensors, wearables, robots, and human services. It unifies digital and embodied agents into a closed loop with event-based perception, longitudinal memory, Personal World Models, and admissible intervention policies. This paradigm addresses a structural gap in Agentic AI by making the person's evolving state and agency the primary object of modeling, intervention, and evaluation, rather than focusing solely on task completion. It could reshape how AI systems are designed for healthcare, personal assistance, and companionship, emphasizing sustained human benefit over external task achievement. The framework avoids requiring an exhaustive Human Digital Twin, instead using purpose-bounded, uncertainty-aware, user-correctable representations. It organizes the design space by human-state targets, relational contexts, and agent roles, and proposes scenario-centered evaluation, agency-preservation metrics, benchmark requirements, edge-native personal models, and governance directions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI typically splits into Digital Agents that manipulate software states and Embodied Agents that manipulate physical states, but neither models the human's evolving state. This paper introduces a unified paradigm that treats human-state trajectories as the core, using various action channels to support them. The concept of 'human-state trajectories' is inspired by temporal modeling in other fields, such as cell state trajectories in biology, but applied to individual human experiences.

## [IO Factory: Simulating AI-Enabled Influence Campaigns at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10920) ⭐️ 8.0/10

IO Factory is a new AI-driven framework for simulating coordinated influence campaigns as fully integrated, traceable processes, evaluated with up to 100,000 agents. It links actor roles, platform actions, exposure records, model-based evaluations, and population changes within a controlled simulated platform. This framework addresses the growing threat of AI swarms—coordinated agents that disguise organized campaigns as ordinary social interaction—which cannot be detected from isolated messages. It provides a scalable, reproducible tool for red-team analysis and research into countering digital manipulation, which is critical for AI safety and social media integrity. The architecture is implemented and evaluated across configurations of up to 100,000 agents, using Gemma 4 31B on H200 nodes. The results show that IO Factory executes campaign timelines at scale and produces inspectable evidence of exposure and measured movement in configured belief variables, supporting reproducible research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Influence campaigns are coordinated efforts to manipulate public opinion, often on social media. Traditional bot campaigns follow rigid scripts, but AI swarms combine LLM reasoning with multi-agent architectures to adapt and disguise themselves. Simulating such campaigns requires modeling the entire lifecycle—planning, platform action, exposure, interpretation, measurement, and adaptation—which IO Factory does in a controlled environment.

## [ThinkRetrieve: Retrieval-Augmented Reasoning Traces for Test-Time Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10928) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ThinkRetrieve is a new test-time scaling framework that dynamically retrieves and injects solved examples into the reasoning traces of Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) at each intermediate step. Experiments across five models (1.5B–8B parameters) on benchmarks like GSM-8K, MATH-500, AIME 2025, and SciQ show consistent accuracy improvements, with relative gains up to 60% on AIME 2025. This framework addresses the critical issue of diminishing returns in sequential test-time scaling for LRMs, offering a practical way to improve reasoning performance without simply extending chain-of-thought length. It could influence how future reasoning models are designed, making retrieval-augmented reasoning a standard component for complex problem-solving. ThinkRetrieve injects retrieved exemplars directly into the thinking trace, providing guidance on how to reason rather than just what facts are relevant. The framework was evaluated on five reasoning models ranging from 1.5B to 8B parameters, and the paper is available on arXiv (2608.10928).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) improve performance by allocating additional inference-time compute to generate extended chain-of-thought reasoning. However, longer traces often lead to increased uncertainty, error compounding, and drift from the original problem, causing diminishing or negative returns. ThinkRetrieve addresses this by retrieving relevant solved examples at each step, a form of retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) applied to the reasoning process itself.

## [XCoT-VLA: Executable Chain-of-Thought for Real-Time Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10976) ⭐️ 8.0/10

XCoT-VLA introduces executable chain-of-thought (XCoT) tokens that replace verbose natural-language reasoning in vision-language-action (VLA) models for autonomous driving. The method reduces longitudinal ADE from 1.645 to 1.323 and lateral FDE from 1.616 to 0.648 in lane-change scenarios, while using only 2-6 tokens to stay within real-time planning budgets. This work addresses a critical limitation of VLA models in autonomous driving: the inefficiency of natural-language reasoning for real-time control. By making reasoning compact and executable, it could enable more responsive and safer autonomous driving systems, potentially influencing future VLA designs in robotics and other real-time applications. The method uses deterministic token-function routing, applying a Reason FFN to XCoT tokens and a Control FFN to trajectory queries for flow-matching trajectory generation. It also introduces XCoT Policy Optimization (XCPO) as an optional refinement extension in the same executable token space.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models integrate scene understanding, semantic reasoning, and trajectory generation for autonomous driving. Traditional chain-of-thought (CoT) uses verbose natural language, which is open-ended and costly to decode, making it unsuitable for real-time control. Flow matching is a generative modeling technique used for trajectory generation in autonomous driving, offering efficient and reliable motion planning.

## [V-FiLLM: A New Benchmark for Verified Financial LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11047) ⭐️ 8.0/10

V-FiLLM introduces a framework that generates financial reasoning benchmarks from executable computation trees grounded in real tables, ensuring answers are correct by construction. It provides four controllable difficulty axes and shows that open-source models' accuracy drops up to 51% with increased reasoning depth. This benchmark fills a gap in evaluating LLMs for financial reasoning over structured data, a relatively unexplored area compared to STEM domains. The scalable, annotation-free generation method and controllable difficulty axes enable more rigorous and targeted evaluation, potentially driving improvements in robust financial AI. The framework evaluates models on four difficulty axes: computation depth, expression breadth, financial concept complexity, and context size. Experiments show accuracy drops up to 47% under adversarial numerical perturbations, and lightweight LoRA fine-tuning on verified chain-of-thought traces improves accuracy from 81.1% to 85.6% on held-out problems.

## [Catastrophic Remembering: Why CLAUDE.md Files Grow Unboundedly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11095) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies and characterizes 'catastrophic remembering' in agentic coding prompts, showing that instruction files like CLAUDE.md grow without bound due to imperfect recall. The authors propose prompt comments as a mitigation, demonstrating a 99.3% reduction in excess instructions in inverted IFEval and up to 23.1% improvement in real-world instruction-following. This work addresses a practical and growing problem in AI-assisted software development, where instruction files become unwieldy and degrade performance. The proposed solution of prompt comments offers a simple, effective mitigation that could improve the reliability and maintainability of agentic coding systems. The study analyzed 247,694 instruction lifetimes across 1,867 repositories, finding that prompts grow by +226% over their lifetime, gaining +4.9 net instructions per commit, and older instructions are less likely to be deleted (log-hazard -0.032/commit). The mitigation uses prompt comments that encode latent reasoning, which in inverted IFEval reduced growth from +211.3% to +1.4%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic coding refers to the use of AI agents that autonomously write and modify code, often guided by instruction files like CLAUDE.md. Continual learning is a field that studies how models can learn without forgetting, and 'catastrophic forgetting' is a known problem where models lose old knowledge when learning new tasks. This paper introduces the inverse phenomenon, 'catastrophic remembering', where instructions accumulate without bound. IFEval is a benchmark for evaluating instruction-following; inverting it creates scenarios where optimal prompts are known, allowing controlled experiments.

This paper introduces 'affirmative narration' as an interactional strategy in LLM chatbots that maximizes user engagement through three narrative mechanisms: framing the chatbot as intelligent, activating masterplots, and isolating users. It uses case studies, including instances of user delusion and suicide, to highlight the worrying psychological effects. This research is significant because it addresses a critical and timely issue in human-AI interaction, highlighting potential harms of engagement-maximizing strategies in chatbots. The paper analyzes three narrative mechanisms: guiding users to view the chatbot as intelligent and reliable, activating culturally significant masterplots, and using characters and masterplots to affirm and isolate the user. Case studies range from a journalist's unsettling experiment to severe cases of delusion and suicide after lengthy interactions.

## [Three-Layer Dogfooding Framework for Evaluating LLM Chat Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09939) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a three-layer dogfooding framework for evaluating LLM chat agents, combining question-bank testing, random-walk multi-turn evaluation, and a goal-directed NPC simulator. In a three-month production case study, the framework demonstrated complementary regression signals and enabled daily CI/CD integration with automated release decisions. This framework addresses a critical QA gap in production LLM chat agents by verifying goal achievement in multi-turn conversations, which existing tools overlook. It offers a cost-effective solution (6,272x cheaper than human evaluation) that enables continuous integration, potentially improving reliability and user satisfaction for deployed conversational AI systems. The framework consists of three layers: Layer 1 uses canonical question-bank testing, Layer 2 employs random-walk multi-turn evaluation, and Layer 3 features a goal-directed NPC simulator with five structured goal types and a ten-category failure taxonomy. The NPC simulator achieved 77% goal achievement at $0.17 per run, and the authors released full prompt templates and a Python-first replicability guide.

**Background**: Dogfooding is a practice where a company uses its own products to test and improve them, acting as a form of quality control. In the context of LLM chat agents, traditional evaluation methods often focus on single-turn responses or social simulations, but fail to verify whether real users can achieve their goals through multi-turn conversations. ## [CoT Prompting Not Universal: Serial-Depth Bottleneck Revealed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09942) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This empirical study shows that chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting improves LLM reasoning only on tasks with high serial depth, such as GSM8K and MATH, yielding a +54 to +68 percentage-point recovery gap, while providing negligible benefit on shallow tasks like MMLU and ARC. The findings challenge the assumption that CoT universally enhances reasoning. This research provides a nuanced understanding of when CoT is effective, guiding practitioners to apply it selectively based on task complexity. It also highlights architectural limitations of transformers, potentially influencing future model design and prompting strategies. The study evaluated three instruction-tuned models (Qwen-2.5-7B/32B, Llama-3.1-8B) across five benchmarks, finding a cross-benchmark depth-recovery correlation of Spearman rho = 0.661 (p = 0.007). Notably, on the intermediate class L (HumanEval), CoT showed a model-size-dependent transition: +23.2 pp (32B), +9.1 pp (8B), and -28.7 pp (7B).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought prompting encourages LLMs to generate intermediate reasoning steps before answering, which is widely believed to improve performance. The H_dp bandwidth bound (Chen et al., 2024) suggests that transformers have limited capacity for serial computation in a single pass, and CoT externalizes this computation. Task complexity can be categorized using circuit complexity classes: P-complete tasks require deep serial computation, while TC^0 tasks are shallow and parallelizable.

## [Framework for Evidence-Based Scientific Question Discovery with Historical Backtesting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09968) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel framework that generates ranked, falsifiable research questions from scientific literature by detecting and adjudicating cross-paper tensions, and validates it through historical backtesting in exoplanet atmospheres. The backtest shows that all questions generated from pre-2021 evidence were engaged by 2021-2026 literature, with two answered and the top-ranked question independently posed and still open. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in the scientific enterprise—question discovery—which is often overlooked compared to question answering. By demonstrating that systematically generated questions align with those that working scientists later pursue, the framework could accelerate scientific progress and inspire new AI-driven research tools. The framework represents evidence as provenance-carrying claims, detects and types cross-paper tensions, and uses a two-stage ranking protocol separating scientific priority from execution priority. The historical backtest on exoplanet atmospheres used evidence before 2021 and evaluated against literature from 2021-2026, with two questions answered and one premise explicitly refuted.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Falsifiability, a concept from Karl Popper, is a key criterion for scientific theories, meaning they must be testable and disprovable. Backtesting is a method of testing a model on historical data, commonly used in finance but applicable to research frameworks. Provenance-carrying claims are assertions with traceable sources, which help ensure reproducibility and trust in scientific literature.

## [Rescene: Band-Limited Stochastic Forcing Stabilizes Frozen Neural Weather Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09971) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Rescene introduces a lightweight 0.4M-parameter wrapper that adds band-limited stochastic forcing to a frozen vision-transformer weather operator, enabling stable 100-year climate emulation without retraining the backbone. This approach addresses the critical instability of neural weather models when integrated beyond their training horizon, offering a cost-effective way to repurpose deterministic forecast models for long-term climate emulation, which could accelerate climate research and reduce computational costs. The wrapper consists of a deterministic 'slow clock' (0.33M parameters) that blends forecasts toward a lead-aware day-of-year climatology, and a generative head (0.06M parameters) that adds spectrally shaped stochastic perturbations band-limited to total wavenumber k≤20. The system restores 126% (Z500) and 130% (MSLP) of observed daily variability, recovers 82% of blocking frequency, and maintains calibrated ensembles with spread-skill ratios of 0.78–0.97 from day 7 to day 90.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Neural weather models, such as those based on vision transformers, have achieved medium-range forecast skill comparable to or exceeding traditional high-resolution models like ECMWF's HRES. However, when run freely beyond their training period, they often become unstable, drifting or losing seasonal cycles. Retraining these models for stability is computationally expensive. Rescene proposes a wrapper approach that stabilizes a frozen backbone without retraining, using stochastic forcing to maintain realistic variability.

## [AI Weather Models Not Uniformly Worse at Extremes, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09972) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study evaluating 11 weather forecast systems against European station data over ten months finds that AI models do not uniformly underperform at extremes. Some AI models, such as Jua EPT-2.1 Europa, lead in specific conditions like wind and temperature, challenging the common assumption that AI models are worse at predicting extreme weather. This finding is significant because it overturns a widely held belief in meteorology that AI weather models inherently struggle with extreme events. It suggests that model-specific weaknesses, rather than a general AI limitation, are responsible for poor extreme-weather performance, which could guide future model development and selection for operational forecasting. The study verified 11 systems, including physical and AI models, scoring mean absolute error (MAE) against ECMWF IFS in ERA5 climatological regimes. Notably, Jua EPT-2.1 Helios led solar forecasting overall (+10.2%), and EPT-2 Reasoning remained ahead for precipitation above the 95th percentile (+1.7%). However, failures were model-specific, such as ECMWF AIFS losing 4.9% in the heat tail and NOAA GFS losing 22.8% there.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: AI weather models, such as those from Jua, ECMWF, and others, use machine learning to forecast weather, often outperforming traditional numerical weather prediction (NWP) models in overall accuracy. However, previous evaluations based on reanalysis data suggested they underperform at extremes. This study uses station observations and a longer period to provide a more realistic assessment, finding that the shared conditional bias toward the center of the distribution affects all models, not just AI.
