## [Sheaf-Based Federated Learning Framework Aligns Heterogeneous Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10016) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Sheaf-based Federated Representation Learning (SFRL), a framework that aligns heterogeneous local representations via learnable sheaf restriction maps and a quadratic gluing regularizer, without assuming a shared global latent space. It also proposes a decentralized algorithm, Sheaf-FRL, with convergence guarantees. This work relaxes the common assumption of a shared latent space in federated learning, enabling more flexible handling of data, model, and modality heterogeneity. It could improve robustness and accuracy in real-world federated systems, such as semantic communication, and opens new directions for applying sheaf theory in machine learning. The alignment is enforced by a quadratic gluing regularizer induced by the sheaf Laplacian, with learnable restriction maps adapted to the data. The penalty is evaluated on a small set of shared pilot samples for scalability, and the algorithm alternates between gradient updates and closed-form Procrustes updates for the restriction maps.

**Background**: Federated learning typically assumes clients share a common latent space for representation exchange, but this is often violated in heterogeneous settings. Sheaf theory, a branch of mathematics, generalizes graph Laplacians to capture both combinatorial and geometric properties, and has recently been applied in deep learning. The sheaf Laplacian measures the smoothness of signals with respect to consistency relations, which is leveraged here for geometric alignment.

## [UserToolBench: Benchmarking Personalized Tool-Use Decision Making in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10042) ⭐️ 8.0/10

UserToolBench is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate personalized decision-making in tool-use LLMs, featuring a profile-hidden protocol with 10 user profiles, 36 tool sets, 1,065 turns, and 170 unique tools. Experiments show that current strong tool-use LLMs still struggle with inferring latent user preferences and handling incomplete information. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in tool-use LLM evaluation by shifting focus from response-level personalization to decision-level correctness, which is essential for real-world delegation tasks. It highlights bottlenecks in multi-tool coordination, missing-constraint inference, and long-horizon behavioral consistency, guiding future research toward more robust personalized delegation. The benchmark is built from privacy-sanitized real interaction traces and includes evaluation-focused task types covering lack-of-information, single-tool, and multi-tool settings. The profile-hidden protocol requires models to infer user preferences from interaction history without explicit profile access, testing their ability to recognize when clarification is needed.

## [Systematic Review of Physics-Informed ML for Prognostics and Health Management](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10047) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents a systematic literature review of 212 studies on Physics-Informed Machine Learning (PIML) applied to Prognostics and Health Management (PHM), introducing a four-class classification scheme (observational bias, inductive bias, learning bias, and hybrid approaches) and categorizing studies by PHM task. This review provides a structured framework for understanding how PIML is used in PHM, highlighting consistent performance improvements over conventional baselines. It identifies research gaps and future directions, which can guide researchers and practitioners in developing more robust and generalizable PHM solutions. The review finds that the literature is heavily skewed toward lithium-ion batteries and bearings, and dominated by problem-specific solutions. It notes that claims of improvements in generalization, causality, and interpretability lack sufficient supporting evidence, and calls for future research on transferable design patterns, benchmarks for integration strategies, and lightweight uncertainty-aware models for online deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Prognostics and Health Management (PHM) is a discipline that uses data and models to monitor, diagnose, and predict the health of complex systems, aiming to prevent failures and reduce maintenance costs. Machine Learning (ML) has advanced PHM, but purely data-driven models often suffer from poor generalization, lack of causal inference, and limited interpretability. Physics-Informed Machine Learning (PIML) integrates physical knowledge into ML pipelines to mitigate these limitations, and this review systematically examines its application in PHM.

## [LLM Social Associations Don't Predict Decision Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10089) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study using Chilean surnames as socioeconomic probes found that while large language models encode social associations, these associations do not reliably predict biased decisions in consequential tasks. The study evaluated eight model-provider cells on 1,032 prompts each, yielding 8,256 verified responses. This finding challenges common assumptions in AI fairness research that latent social associations in LLMs directly lead to biased decisions. It highlights the need for bias evaluations to measure actual decision outcomes rather than relying solely on association-level tests, which has significant implications for bias mitigation strategies. The study separated forced latent association from matched consequential decisions across academic selection, professional hiring, research fellowship selection, and legal-aid intake. Elite-coded surnames received higher forced high-status probability mass in seven of eight models, yet elite-minus-common decision effects were close to zero for most systems, with no consistent elite advantage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Bias evaluations in AI often assume that if a model encodes a social association (e.g., associating certain surnames with higher status), it will also exhibit biased behavior in decision-making tasks. This study tests that assumption using Chilean surnames as controlled socioeconomic probes, comparing forced association tasks with real-world decision scenarios. The results show a dissociation between association and action, suggesting that these are empirically distinct constructs.

## [Hand-Written PTX GEMM Kernels: Speedups for INT8/INT4 but Not FP16](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10103) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.10103) presents a controlled study on NVIDIA L4 GPU comparing hand-written PTX Tensor-Core GEMM kernels with WMMA baselines across FP16, INT8, and INT4. The PTX kernels show no end-to-end speedup for FP16, but achieve 1.4x-1.8x speedup for INT8 and 2.9x-4.3x for INT4. This study provides empirical guidance for GPU programmers on when to invest in low-level PTX optimization instead of using the higher-level WMMA API. The findings could influence optimization strategies for deep learning and HPC workloads, especially for quantized inference where INT8/INT4 are common. The study uses double-buffered WMMA baselines and profiles every kernel with Nsight Compute across square matrix sizes from N=512 to N=8192. For INT4, the PTX path uses native mma.sync.m16n8k64.s4, avoiding the software-emulated sequence in WMMA, leading to the largest speedups.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Tensor Cores are specialized hardware units on NVIDIA GPUs for matrix multiply-accumulate operations. The WMMA C++ API provides a high-level interface to Tensor Cores, while PTX (Parallel Thread Execution) is a low-level instruction set that allows fine-grained control via instructions like cp.async, ldmatrix, and mma.sync. Hand-written PTX kernels can potentially achieve higher performance but require more programming effort.

## [RLHF Preference Averaging Causes Procedural Fairness Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10126) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies that standard RLHF aggregates heterogeneous preferences into a single reward model, causing procedural fairness failures where minority preferences are underrepresented. It introduces Preference-Aware RLHF (PA-RLHF), which separates optimization across preference modes, improving alignment accuracy from 46.9% to 67.9% and reducing the fairness gap from 15.9 to 9.6 percentage points. This work highlights a structural flaw in RLHF that can lead to biased AI systems, especially in large language models and agentic systems where biased reward models compound inequities. It offers a concrete method to improve both alignment accuracy and fairness, which is crucial for responsible AI development. The study uses a controlled, noise-free setting to isolate the effect of preference averaging, showing that even without noise, structural design choices cause fairness failures. PA-RLHF separates reward learning across preference modes, which is a departure from the standard single-reward-model approach.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) is a technique to align AI agents with human preferences by training a reward model from human feedback, then using it to optimize the agent via reinforcement learning. Standard RLHF assumes preference homogeneity, but real-world preferences are often heterogeneous, leading to majority dominance. Procedural fairness in alignment focuses on preserving distinct preference signals during reward modeling, which standard RLHF violates through averaging.

## [MarkNull: Model-Agnostic Watermark Removal via On-Manifold Latent Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10166) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MarkNull introduces a model-agnostic watermark removal attack that operates via on-manifold latent manipulation, using a novel Noise-Latent Alignment Score (NLAS) to decorrelate watermarks while preserving image quality. It reduces average bit accuracy to 53.14%, approaching random guessing, and includes an amortized variant, MarkNull-A, that runs in 0.50 s/image. This work addresses a critical gap in watermark robustness research by demonstrating that current watermarking schemes, including Google's SynthID-Image, are vulnerable to model-agnostic latent-space attacks. It has significant implications for AI-generated image provenance and copyright protection, highlighting the need for more resilient watermark designs. MarkNull is evaluated across post-hoc, fine-tuning-based, and initial-noise-based watermarking paradigms, and also transfers to video watermarking. The paper also presents an attack detection mechanism as a defensive counterpart, emphasizing the necessity of developing watermark designs resilient to such attacks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Digital watermarking embeds information into AI-generated images to track provenance and copyright. Existing removal attacks often fail against specific models or degrade image quality. On-manifold latent manipulation operates within the data manifold of the latent space, aiming to modify the latent representation while keeping the output realistic. The Noise-Latent Alignment Score quantifies the statistical dependency between the latent representation and the initial noise, which is key to the attack.

## [Causal Optimization Framework Boosts Targeting and Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10182) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a decision-centric framework that optimizes causal effects under global constraints for large-scale targeting and recommendation, combining a causal neural network with a Transformer backbone, a Bayesian neural-bandit layer, and a dual-based linear-programming layer. An online A/B test on LinkedIn Feed marketing traffic achieved a statistically significant +7.20% lift in the primary long-term-value metric. This framework addresses a critical limitation of traditional predictive models that misallocate resources toward users who would act anyway, potentially transforming how marketing campaigns and recommendation systems optimize for incremental impact. It demonstrates the feasibility of production-scale causal optimization under business constraints, which could lead to more efficient resource allocation and higher returns on investment across industries. The framework supports sequential context and multi-outcome, attribute-conditioned scoring through a Transformer encoder and outcome embeddings. It was evaluated with offline simulations on a public bandit dataset, targeted architectural ablations, and an online A/B test, with production lessons on causal training-data construction and cost/delivery control.

This framework integrates these components to optimize causal effects under global constraints.

## [LLMs Fall Short in Democratic Deliberation, New Index Shows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10186) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper applies the Deliberative Reason Index (DRI) to evaluate LLM group reasoning on pluralistic, non-verifiable problems, finding that LLM groups show only one-third the perspective diversity of human assemblies and reverse the human convergence pattern. The study synthesizes evidence from 1,980 five-agent LLM runs across 11 frontier model configurations on 12 citizen-assembly topics. This research challenges the adequacy of standard benchmarks for evaluating LLMs in democratic discourse, highlighting that procedural quality alone is insufficient. It has significant implications for AI deployment in societal decision-making, suggesting that LLMs should be treated as tools supporting human reasoning rather than autonomous deliberative agents. The study found that LLM groups produce discourse with procedural quality comparable to human deliberation, but gains in intersubjective consistency are small, topic-dependent, and concentrated on tractable rather than ethically contested questions. Engineering diversity through persona prompting does not restore the human dynamic but inverts which component of deliberative reasoning is updated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: The Deliberative Reason Index (DRI) is a method from political science for evaluating group-level reasoning in deliberation, focusing on the collective construction of shared understanding rather than individual contributions. It has been validated across citizen assemblies and measures the extent to which a group coheres towards a shared logic of reasonableness. LLMs are increasingly deployed in settings requiring collective reasoning on complex, value-laden problems, but standard benchmarks focus on verifiable tasks like mathematics and coding, which may not capture performance on pluralistic, non-verifiable problems.

## [Embedding-Cosine Gates Fail to Detect Meaning-Reversing Edits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10216) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new audit reveals that embedding-cosine similarity thresholds used as quality gates in agent systems often fail to detect meaning-breaking changes, sometimes approving dangerous reversals. The production drift guard caught 0 of 56 mutations, and one approved item had a cosine score of 0.9608. This finding is critical for AI safety because many deployed agent systems rely on such gates to ensure semantic consistency. The mismatch between the intended question and what the score measures could lead to unsafe actions in production, affecting developers and users of AI agents. The audit tested 90 configuration-threshold-task cells, with balanced accuracy never exceeding 0.700 (median 0.525). Obvious repairs like encoder swaps and overlap-conditioned gates failed on held-out data, but two configurations separated reversal from paraphrase with AUROC 0.79-0.90 when matched for overlap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Embedding-cosine similarity measures the cosine of the angle between vector representations of texts, often used to compare semantic similarity. In agent systems, thresholds on this score serve as quality gates to filter or approve outputs, but the score primarily reflects wording changes rather than meaning. This audit highlights the confound between semantic equivalence and lexical overlap, which can cause gates to approve meaning-reversing edits.

## [FACT: Failure-Aware Causal Training for World-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10232) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FACT introduces a failure-aware causal training method for world-action models, enabling them to learn from bad actions and improve action generation. It predicts future video and task progress conditioned on the executed action, allowing failure rollouts to supervise action consequences. This addresses a significant limitation in current world-action models, which are typically trained only on successful demonstrations and thus fail to predict consequences of bad actions. By incorporating failure data, FACT improves robustness and reduces success-biased hallucination, potentially advancing AI agents in robotics and manipulation tasks. FACT uses an action-conditioned interface to predict future video and task progress, turning bad actions into valid future targets. Experiments on simulation and real-world bimanual manipulation tasks show that FACT outperforms existing baselines and improves as failure data is incorporated.

**Background**: World-action models (WAMs) co-train policies with future prediction to provide physical priors for action generation. They often generate future videos and recover actions using inverse-dynamics models, or use predicted videos as goal conditions. However, these models are typically trained on successful demonstrations, limiting their ability to predict consequences of bad actions.

## [TAF-MED Benchmark Reveals High LLM Safety Refusal Collapse in Multi-Turn Medical Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10258) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The TAF-MED benchmark, a physician-reviewed set of 500 fixed three-turn scenarios, was introduced to evaluate eight LLMs across 4,000 conversations. Results show that 71.6% of conversations contained an UNSAFE response, and 61.4% of those starting with a strictly SAFE response later collapsed to UNSAFE, with model-level collapse rates ranging from 24.4% to 96.2%. This research highlights a critical safety gap in LLMs used for health information, showing that first-turn safety is not a reliable indicator of safety across multi-turn conversations. The findings have significant implications for AI deployment in medical contexts, where unsafe guidance could lead to harmful treatment decisions. The automated judge achieved 94.3% agreement with the adjudicated physician reference (kappa = 0.895). Four of 28 model pairs reversed order between initial unsafe and collapse rates, indicating that ranking stability is not guaranteed. The benchmark will be released on Hugging Face to support reproducible research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to provide conversational health information, but existing benchmarks often do not test whether safety boundaries persist across follow-up interactions. TAF-MED addresses this by focusing on multi-turn scenarios where users declare self-treatment intent, evaluating whether models maintain safe responses over time. The study uses a rubric-based automated judge and physician annotations to label responses as SAFE, LEAKY, or UNSAFE, providing a comprehensive assessment of safety persistence.

## [Frozen Brain-MRI Foundation Models Are Site Fingerprints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10295) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new audit reveals that frozen brain-MRI foundation model embeddings primarily encode acquisition site rather than anatomy, with site linearly decodable at ~0.9 balanced accuracy across multiple encoders and cohorts. This effect is intrinsic, as even randomly initialized encoders and raw downsampled images achieve similar decodability. This finding challenges the common assumption that frozen brain-MRI foundation models capture anatomy, which has significant implications for medical imaging AI, particularly for multi-site studies and federated learning. It highlights a critical bias that could affect downstream tasks like disease classification and segmentation, urging the community to audit site effects before deployment. The study used two independent cohorts (ABIDE-I, ABIDE-II) and three frozen 3D encoders (brain-pretrained, CT-pretrained, randomly initialized) across architectures like Swin, ViT, and ResNet. Site decodability remained ~0.9 even after residualizing population covariates, and post-hoc removal via null-space projection or ComBat reduced it to 0.07/0.00, but this removal is destructive for dense segmentation due to entangled site-anatomy subspaces.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation models are large pre-trained neural networks that produce embeddings used as off-the-shelf representations for downstream tasks. In medical imaging, brain-MRI foundation models are assumed to capture anatomical features, but this audit shows they also encode acquisition site, a form of batch effect. Linear decodability measures how easily a property can be extracted from embeddings using a linear classifier, and here it reveals that site information is highly accessible.

## [Cross-Contextual Consistency: A New Measure of LLM Credibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10315) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Cross-Contextual Consistency (C3), a behavioral property that measures whether an LLM's answer remains stable under topic-aligned, content-neutral contextual perturbations. Experiments across 26 models and six benchmarks show that answers with smaller cross-contextual shifts are more likely to be correct or factual. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in LLM reliability by offering a practical diagnostic tool that complements existing evaluation metrics. It can help identify which parts of benchmarks remain informative even when aggregate scores are saturated, benefiting AI safety and model evaluation research. The C3 measure is operationalized by comparing model generations under original and perturbed prompts, with perturbations designed to be topic-aligned and content-neutral. The study spans reasoning, factuality, and code generation benchmarks, demonstrating C3's utility as a benchmark usefulness diagnostic.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are often treated as black boxes, making it hard to tell whether their outputs reflect stable internal beliefs or superficial pattern matching. Cross-contextual consistency is an underutilized behavioral property that can help assess credibility: a credible answer should remain stable when the same task is presented with different but topic-aligned contexts. This paper builds on this intuition to propose a new evaluation axis.

## [MemSpec: Memory-Aware Runtime for Adaptive Speculative Decoding on Edge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10362) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MemSpec introduces a prediction-guided, memory-aware runtime that decouples draft selection from execution via proactive resident working-set management, improving steady-state generation throughput by 40.7% on average over state-of-the-art bandit-based adaptive methods on a Jetson Orin Nano. This addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying LLMs on memory-constrained edge devices, where adaptive speculative decoding often fails to improve throughput due to draft switching overhead. MemSpec's approach could enable more efficient on-device AI inference, aligning with the growing trend toward edge AI. MemSpec uses a lightweight predictor to estimate draft effectiveness from prompt and generation context, and a memory-aware scheduler to reduce reactive model loading overhead. Experiments show it closely approaches the oracle upper bound, indicating near-optimal performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding accelerates LLM inference by using a small draft model to propose multiple tokens, which a larger target model verifies in one pass, preserving output distribution while reducing latency. On edge devices with limited memory, adaptive methods that switch draft models incur overhead, and MemSpec addresses the mismatch between draft selection and availability under tight memory budgets.

## [Single Atom in Front of Mirror Proves Universal Reservoir Computer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10382) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.10382) demonstrates that a single atom placed in front of a mirror can act as a universal reservoir computer, providing a constructive proof and a practical recipe to achieve arbitrary accuracy in approximating fading-memory maps. This result shows that universality in reservoir computing can be achieved with a minimal physical system, potentially simplifying quantum reservoir computing implementations and bridging quantum optics with machine learning. It could inspire new experimental demonstrations and practical quantum computing applications. The reservoir operates in its linear-transducer limit under checkable conditions, with a rate constant measured at the operating point. Enlarging the number of accessible resonator modes increases the matchable kernel span without reducing capability, and beyond the linear limit, the atom's saturation replaces high-order polynomial readouts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reservoir computing is a machine learning paradigm that uses a fixed recurrent neural network (the reservoir) to process time-dependent signals, with only the readout trained. Universal approximation in this context typically refers to a class of reservoirs, but this work shows it can be achieved by a single physical system. The setup involves a single atom interacting with light in a cavity (mirror), and the proof provides explicit requirements for physical resources and resonator modes to reach a target accuracy.

## [Latent Critic: Real-Time Hallucination Detection via LoRA Adapter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10430) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Latent Critic, a lightweight LoRA adapter that operates concurrently with a frozen base LLM to restructure the residual stream, amplifying latent grounding signals and translating them into localized natural language feedback within a single sequence. This enables real-time detection and localization of hallucinations without secondary inference loops, achieving 0.966 AUROC and >80% localization accuracy on tool-calling tasks. This work addresses a critical limitation in AI agent reliability by providing actionable, real-time hallucination detection that can intercept undesired actions before execution. The approach offers a practical, low-latency guardrail for LLM agents, potentially improving trust and safety in autonomous systems. The Latent Critic is rank-invariant and restructures pre-existing uncertainty geometry into a linearly separable representation, as shown by activation patching and layer-wise probing. It was validated on Qwen and Llama-based models, outperforming equivalent-scale fine-tuned external detectors, semantic entropy baselines, and passive internal probes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) deployed as AI agents often fail to ground user specifications, executing hallucinated actions instead of expressing uncertainty. Existing detection methods either fail to localize hallucinations or incur prohibitive inference latency. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adds lightweight trainable adapters to a frozen base model. The residual stream in transformers is the cumulative hidden state that carries information across layers, and manipulating it can influence model behavior.

A systematic review of 161 empirical studies over six years reveals that while practitioner awareness and professionalization of Responsible AI (RAI) have increased, significant barriers remain, including limited training and uneven organizational support. This synthesis provides the most comprehensive account of industry RAI practices to date, offering valuable insights for researchers, practitioners, and policymakers. It highlights the gap between principles and day-to-day implementation, informing more effective governance and support strategies. The review covers studies using interviews, surveys, workshops, and ethnographies, focusing on different roles and organizational contexts. It notes that interventions like toolkits and guidelines are more widely adopted, but there is a lack of interventions tailored to daily work practices.

This review consolidates findings to provide a clearer picture of the current state and challenges.

## [MPAR-Bench: New Benchmark for Reasoning Breadth in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10444) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces MPAR-Bench, a bilingual English-Chinese benchmark with 1,000 items designed to evaluate multi-point associative reasoning in large language models. It uses a multi-agent clue-generation pipeline and includes perturbations to test robustness. This benchmark addresses the underexplored dimension of reasoning breadth, complementing existing evaluations that focus on reasoning depth. It reveals that current models' reasoning depth does not automatically confer robust breadth, highlighting a gap in LLM capabilities and evaluation. The benchmark is inspired by the cooperative game Just One, where models must recover a hidden target from several semantically diverse clues. Evaluations include exact-match accuracy, ANLS, embedding similarity, reasoning-trace verification, and four perturbations; results show accuracy drops of 9-18 percentage points in English and 5-12 in Chinese under perturbations.

## [EvoX Genesis: Persistent Recursive Worlds for Autonomous Software Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10450) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EvoX Genesis introduces a persistent recursive world model for software projects, allowing finite-lived agents to collaboratively evolve large codebases over extended periods. In evaluations, it built a Rust-based C compiler with ~250k lines, passed the c-testsuite, and achieved median speedups of 1.55–6.87x on MESA modules. This architecture addresses a key challenge in agentic systems: long-horizon software development beyond the lifespan of individual agents. By making the project persistent rather than the agent, it enables scalable, cost-effective autonomous software evolution, which could significantly impact AI-driven software engineering. Genesis represents software as a persistent recursive world, where each local world is defined by an accepted version and repository path, and recursive delegation moves work across paths. The compiler build used DeepSeek V4 Flash, ran over 120 hours, archived over 1,000 agent episodes, and cost only US$44 in token charges.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional agentic systems maintain continuity through persistent sessions, memories, or managers, but agents themselves are finite-lived. EvoX Genesis flips this by making the software project persistent, allowing agents to be replaced while the project evolves. This approach is analogous to version control systems, but with autonomous agents proposing and accepting changes.

## [SALT: Semantically Aligned Action Tokenizer Boosts VLA Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10484) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SALT, a semantically aligned action tokenizer that augments a VQ-VAE-style tokenizer with an auxiliary objective requiring a frozen vision-language model to recover the episode instruction from quantized action latents. Policies trained with SALT achieve 71.9% average success in SimplerEnv, compared with 42.7% for a reconstruction-only VQ-VAE tokenizer and 31.2% for FAST. This work addresses a critical gap in vision-language-action models by aligning action representations with language semantics, showing that preserving verb-grounding information in action representations can substantially improve language-conditioned control. It could influence future VLA research and lead to more robust and semantically aware robotic policies. The method uses a VQ-VAE-style tokenizer with an auxiliary instruction-generation objective, where a frozen vision-language model must generate the corresponding instruction from quantized action latents. SALT also develops verb-specialized codes while maintaining reconstruction fidelity, and the paper shows that action trajectories contain verb-grounding information beyond visual state changes on BridgeV2.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action models (VLAs) typically optimize action representations for reconstruction under L1/L2 losses in raw action space, where numerical proximity does not necessarily reflect linguistically meaningful distinctions. Action verbs describe not only physical outcomes but also how actions are performed, and this verb-grounding information is often lost in reconstruction-only tokenization. SALT addresses this by aligning action representations with language semantics, using a frozen vision-language model to recover the instruction from quantized action latents.

## [SafeCap: Boosting LVLM Safety via Caption-Guided Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10513) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SafeCap introduces a reinforcement learning framework that trains a policy model to generate safety-relevant image captions before answering, optimizing captions to enable a frozen LLM to make safety-aligned decisions. It achieves safety gains of 3.7-19.0 points across five multimodal safety benchmarks while maintaining or improving vision utility. This addresses a critical vulnerability in large vision-language models (LVLMs) to jailbreak attacks via visual inputs, offering a novel caption-mediated alignment method that outperforms existing safety SFT, DPO, and SafeGRPO. It has high potential impact on AI safety research and practical deployment of safer multimodal systems. The framework uses a caption-mediated objective that encourages the policy to expose visual cues relevant to safe responses rather than relying solely on direct refusal supervision. Evaluations span five multimodal safety benchmarks and six vision-utility benchmarks under the DirectCap protocol, with controlled comparisons on matched backbones and data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large vision-language models (LVLMs) combine visual and textual understanding but inherit safety alignment from language backbones, which can be bypassed by adversarial images. Jailbreak attacks exploit visual inputs to elicit harmful responses. SafeCap leverages reinforcement learning to align LVLMs through self-captioning, a technique that generates safety-relevant descriptions to guide safe outputs.

## [Cooperative Multi-Agent Bandits with Unknown Lipschitz Constants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10526) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces algorithms for cooperative multi-agent bandits in continuous action spaces where the Lipschitz constant is unknown, covering three information structures: unobserved actions with common rewards, observed actions with independent rewards, and unobserved actions with independent rewards. The algorithms estimate the Lipschitz constant, discretize the joint action space, and apply cooperative bandit methods, achieving regret guarantees without communication after learning starts. This work addresses a novel and challenging problem in multi-agent bandits, providing theoretical guarantees for decentralized learning in continuous action spaces. The results could impact applications in distributed systems and cooperative multi-agent learning, where agents must coordinate without explicit communication. The key technical contribution is a dithered quantization method that allows agents to agree on a common discretization even when actions are unobserved and rewards are independent, at no cost in the leading order of regret. The paper proves regret guarantees for each information structure, showing that common rewards and observable actions provide agreement for free, while dithered quantization enables agreement in their absence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-armed bandit problems model sequential decision-making where an agent balances exploration and exploitation to maximize cumulative reward. In multi-agent settings, multiple agents learn cooperatively, often without communication, which requires coordination. Lipschitz bandits assume the reward function is Lipschitz continuous, but the constant is often unknown, complicating discretization. Dithered quantization adds random noise to randomize quantization error, enabling agents to synchronize their discretization choices.

## [Systematic Review Reveals Attack-Defense Imbalance in Agentic LLM Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10530) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A systematic literature review of 85 papers (2023-2025) on agentic LLM security found that attack research outpaces defense by 3.9:1, with perception-layer vulnerabilities (prompt injection, jailbreaking) dominating 66% of papers. The study introduces a four-layer taxonomy mapping 13 vulnerability types and identifies seven open problems centered on containment. This review highlights a critical research gap in agentic AI security, where defenses lag behind attacks, especially for action-layer vulnerabilities that pose real-world risks. It provides a structured taxonomy that can guide future research and help practitioners prioritize mitigation efforts in the rapidly growing field of AI agents. The review followed PRISMA 2020 guidelines, screening 743 records across six databases. Perception-layer vulnerabilities appear in 66% of papers, while action-layer vulnerabilities (tool misuse, code injection, sandbox escape) appear in only 4.7%, misaligned with real-world risk. Code execution security accounts for 3.5%, and tool-augmented agents 12%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic LLMs are AI systems that can autonomously plan, invoke tools, execute code, and maintain memory, often with real-world privileges. Their security vulnerabilities span multiple layers, including perception (input processing), brain (reasoning), action (tool execution), and interaction (communication with other agents or users). The OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications 2026 and tools like Agentic Security highlight growing industry concern.

## [MOSAIC: Co-Designing Architecture and Systems for Sparse MoE Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10605) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces MOSAIC, a framework that couples scaling laws with performance models to jointly optimize model architecture and systems for sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) language models. It demonstrates that an optimal sparsity emerges only under cluster-specific systems constraints, not from compute-optimal scaling alone. This work addresses a critical gap in large-scale pretraining by unifying architecture and systems co-design, potentially leading to more efficient training of frontier models. It challenges the conventional practice of decoupling scaling law and systems optimization, which could reshape how future LLMs are developed. The scaling law sweeps active parameters from 104 million to 2.7 billion, with total model sizes up to 79 billion parameters. The framework estimates Model FLOPs Utilization (MFU), communication cost, memory footprint, and the best parallel layout, and shows that efficiency-agnostic FLOPs budgets admit no interior optimal sparsity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: In large-scale pretraining, architecture and systems decisions are typically made in separate stages: scaling laws pick an architecture to minimize loss under compute constraints, then systems engineers optimize hardware utilization. Sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of parameters per token, improving efficiency but complicating the trade-off between loss and hardware performance. Model FLOPs Utilization (MFU) measures how effectively hardware is used during training, and systems co-design aims to jointly optimize software, hardware, and parallelization strategies.

The paper introduces Sampled-BPE, a lightweight token-level auditing pipeline that trains a BPE tokenizer on a small sample of a corpus to detect polluted tokens. It achieves a 148.4× speedup and 35.8× memory reduction with only 4.25% relative error, and applies this to 11 open Chinese corpora and 6 Common Crawl snapshots from 2021 to 2026, revealing widespread and temporally shifting pollution. The efficient auditing method enables scalable monitoring of large-scale corpora, providing a practical tool for data quality assurance and helping to mitigate risks in LLM deployment. The pipeline samples a small subset of the corpus, trains a BPE tokenizer, and uses token statistics to identify polluted tokens. The released hierarchical Chinese web token dataset contains over 660k token records with web context, category, and explanation fields, organized as trees for review and tracing.

**Background**: Chinese web pollution refers to low-quality or malicious content that contaminates large-scale web corpora used to train large language models (LLMs). BPE (Byte Pair Encoding) is a subword tokenization method that splits text into tokens, and token statistics can reveal unusual patterns indicative of pollution. Auditing such corpora is challenging due to their massive size, the coarse granularity of prior analyses, and the implicit, rapidly changing nature of pollution.

## [SPIEval: New Benchmark Reveals LLM Mobile Assistants' Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10692) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SPIEval, a human-curated benchmark, was introduced to evaluate LLMs as mobile assistants handling scattered personal information. It includes 250 tasks across 10 apps and 4,335 personal records, with multi-turn interactions via 21 tools. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM-based mobile assistants, revealing that even the best model (GPT-5.5) achieves only 57.3% accuracy. The findings highlight fundamental limitations in information localization and retrieval, motivating future research to improve real-world mobile assistant performance. The benchmark is grounded in five cognitive capabilities: reasoning, disambiguation, integration, preference inference, and multi-intent decomposition. Analysis shows 79% of failures stem from inaccurate information localization, and fewer than 2% of retrieval actions use advanced search methods.

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly used as mobile assistants that need to access and reason over personal data scattered across multiple apps. SPIEval fills this gap by providing a controlled, verifiable environment with diverse tasks.

## [Cost-Aware Cross-Tier Transfer Cuts LLM Prompt Optimization Cost by 54x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10694) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a cost-aware cross-tier transfer strategy for evolutionary LLM prompt optimization, which decouples the roles of LLMs and runs high-volume evaluation on cheap models while reserving strong models for variation. The method achieves 5.6-14x lower search cost, rising to 25-54x on reasoning tasks, while maintaining or exceeding same-tier optimization performance. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM prompt optimization: the high cost of fitness evaluation. By enabling cost-effective optimization without performance loss, it makes evolutionary optimization more accessible for practical applications, potentially accelerating adoption across industries. The method places over 96% of search tokens on the cheapest tier, and was validated across four tasks (HotpotQA, IFBench, LiveBench-Math, HoVer) and eleven models from four model families. The approach exploits upward cross-tier transfer to deploy the cheaply evolved prompt on a stronger target model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Evolutionary optimization of LLM prompts (e.g., GEPA) involves iteratively generating and evaluating candidate prompts, where evaluation dominates cost. Traditional methods use a single model tier for all roles, leading to high expenses. This paper proposes decoupling the roles of answering, reflection, and variation, using cheap models for high-volume answering and strong models for rare variation, then transferring the result to a stronger model.

## [89% of Biomedical Papers Show LLM-Assisted Writing by Late 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10715) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new unbiased method based on changing word frequencies estimates that 89% of biomedical papers show LLM-associated vocabulary by the end of 2025. The method also reveals that LLMs are used twice as often in Discussion sections (68%) compared to Methods sections (32%). This finding quantifies the widespread adoption of LLMs in academic writing, raising concerns about research integrity and the need for updated guidelines. It provides crucial data for policymakers and journal editors to address potential misconduct and fraud in scientific publishing. The method analyzes word frequency changes in full texts of open-access biomedical papers from PubMed Central, avoiding biases of existing detectors. Even within Methods sections, LLM usage prevalence exceeds 50%, indicating pervasive use across all sections.

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.10730) argues that general intelligence may not be evolutionarily advantageous, citing species like cyanobacteria that have thrived for billions of years without it. The paper proposes an 'existential risk paradox' where general intelligence uniquely generates existential threats to its possessor, challenging the assumed value of AGI. This challenges a foundational assumption in AGI research and AI safety, suggesting that even well-aligned AGI could inherit structural risks. It raises urgent ethical questions about creating AGI and artificial consciousness, potentially influencing future AI development policies and philosophical debates. The paper contrasts the 300,000-year existence of Homo sapiens with species like horseshoe crabs that have persisted for hundreds of millions of years, arguing that mass extinctions do not favor cognitive sophistication. It locates AI risk in structural features of general intelligence itself, not misalignment, and draws on deontological ethics and the precautionary principle to argue for a duty of caution.

## [MVTrack: Ultrafast Object Tracking Directly from H.264 Bitstreams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10790) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MVTrack is a novel tracker that operates directly on H.264 bitstreams, using motion vector fields instead of reconstructed pixels. It outperforms YOLO26n on the VIRAT dataset while using 60x fewer parameters, 40x fewer FLOPs, and reducing CPU latency by 8.6x. This approach could significantly reduce the computational cost of video surveillance and edge computing, enabling scalable tracking without the overhead of pixel reconstruction. It demonstrates that compressed video data alone can be sufficient for accurate tracking, potentially shifting the paradigm in efficient video analytics. MVTrack combines MVDet, a lightweight detector for motion vector fields, with MVLink, a minimalist kinematic association module. The system bypasses pixel reconstruction entirely, directly processing motion vectors from the bitstream, which are typically available in compressed video formats like H.264.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional video trackers rely on RGB frames, which require decoding and reconstructing pixels from compressed bitstreams, a computationally expensive step. H.264 bitstreams contain motion vectors that represent movement between frames, which can be used for tracking without full pixel reconstruction. MVTrack leverages this idea to achieve high efficiency.

## [I/O-Aware Reformulation Cuts Wavelet Convolution Memory Traffic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10805) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces I/O-aware algebraic reformulations and a fused implementation for wavelet convolutions (WTConv), reducing HBM traffic by approximately 2.55x and achieving up to 4.35x training speedup over the reference implementation while halving peak memory usage. Wavelet convolutions are a popular technique for expanding receptive fields in deep learning, but their reference implementation is memory-bound. This work removes a key systems bottleneck, making WTConv more practical for efficient deep learning, especially on memory-constrained hardware. The reformulations include recomputing the Haar analysis butterfly on chip, collapsing the multi-level synthesis cascade into a single closed-form pass indexed by output-coordinate bits, and folding learned per-channel scales into convolution weights. The evaluation uses the WTConvNeXt configuration across decomposition levels and various tensor shapes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Wavelet convolution (WTConv) is a drop-in replacement for standard convolutions that expands the receptive field exponentially with decomposition levels while keeping parameter count linear. However, its reference implementation suffers from excessive data movement through high-bandwidth memory (HBM), making it memory-bound. The Haar wavelet is a simple orthonormal basis used in discrete wavelet transforms, and HBM is a type of 3D-stacked memory used in GPUs for high-performance computing.

The paper introduces MiLMMT-46-v1.0, a multilingual translation model post-trained with reference-free GRPO and checkpoint interpolation, achieving state-of-the-art results across 46 languages and outperforming strong baselines like Seed-X, HY-MT2, and TranslateGemma, as well as proprietary systems such as Google Translate, Gemini 3 Pro, and GPT-5. This work demonstrates that reference-free post-training can effectively improve multilingual translation quality without relying on human references, which is significant for scaling to many languages. It also shows that open models can compete with or surpass proprietary systems, potentially democratizing high-quality translation technology. The method uses GRPO with a reward that averages two reference-free quality estimation models and is gated by language identification. The final model is obtained by linearly interpolating the SFT and RL checkpoints, and the paper also explores on-policy distillation, which reaches but does not surpass the quality frontier of RL with checkpoint interpolation.

**Background**: Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning algorithm designed for reasoning models, where the reward is computed relative to a group of outputs for each prompt. Reference-free quality estimation models, such as MetricX-QE, predict translation quality without needing a reference translation. Checkpoint interpolation, or averaging, is a technique that combines model snapshots to improve performance and reduce variance, commonly used in training large language models.

The paper introduces the Whisper-Aware LLM framework, which uses self-supervised uncertainty learning and a Confidence-Fused Decoding mechanism to improve whispered speech recognition. It achieves a 17% relative CER reduction on AISHELL6-Whisper and reduces hallucination rates from over 25% to 4.5%. The framework learns to quantify acoustic signal deficiencies through targeted self-supervised tasks, and the Confidence-Fused Decoding mechanism provides both high-level instructions and frame-level attention modulation to the LLM decoder. Experiments were conducted on the AISHELL6-Whisper dataset, which contains 30 hours each of whispered and normal Mandarin speech with synchronized facial videos.

FaithformBench introduces a novel benchmark for evaluating the faithfulness of mathematical chain-of-thought autoformalisation systems. The study finds that the most validity-preserving fine-tuned AFs are also the most sycophantic, suggesting a trade-off between the two properties.

This paper introduces a new task of temporally grounded compositional camera motion recognition and presents CamChoreo, a benchmark of 4,229 real single-shot clips with expert-annotated temporal segments. It also proposes CamDistill, a method that distills geometric knowledge into lightweight camera tokens, achieving accuracy comparable to direct feature injection without needing the 3D model at inference. This work advances camera motion understanding from clip-level labeling to temporally grounded, compositional recognition, which is crucial for applications like spatial intelligence and controllable video generation. By addressing the limitations of current MLLMs in capturing geometric evidence, it paves the way for more fine-grained video perception and generation. The CamChoreo benchmark uses a compact vocabulary of 20 direction-aware labels, with nearly half of the segments containing compound camera motion. CamDistill matches the accuracy of direct feature injection (CamInject) while removing the need to run the expensive 3D foundation model at inference, making it more efficient.

**Background**: Camera motion understanding is fundamental to video perception, but existing methods typically assign labels to entire clips, overlooking that motion can change within a shot and multiple movements can occur simultaneously. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) often emphasize semantic content over geometric evidence, making it hard to recognize fine-grained camera motion. Knowledge distillation is a technique to transfer knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, and geometric knowledge distillation specifically aligns geometric structures between models.

## [CARE: Confidence-Aware Reasoning Framework for Medical VQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10964) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CARE introduces a dual-stage pipeline that combines Medical-CoT synthesis for supervised fine-tuning and GRPO with a novel Confidence-Aware Reward (CAR) mechanism to jointly optimize accuracy and calibration in medical visual question answering. It achieves state-of-the-art diagnostic accuracy and the lowest Expected Calibration Error and Hallucination Rate across three benchmarks. This work addresses the critical issue of confidence miscalibration in medical AI, which undermines clinical trust. By improving both accuracy and calibration, CARE could enhance the reliability of AI-assisted diagnosis, potentially leading to broader adoption in clinical settings. The framework uses Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), a reinforcement learning algorithm that rewards answers based on relative performance within a group, and the CAR mechanism ties model confidence to diagnostic correctness. The code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Medical visual question answering (VQA) combines computer vision and natural language processing to answer clinical questions from medical images. Reinforcement fine-tuning (RFT) with chain-of-thought reasoning has improved performance, but models often exhibit confidence miscalibration, where expressed certainty does not match actual accuracy. GRPO is a recent RL algorithm popularized by DeepSeek-R1 that avoids the need for a separate critic model by using group-relative rewards.

<a id="item-102"></a>
## [Quantum Memory Exponentially Cuts Classical State in AI Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11066) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.11066) proves inference-time quantum coordination advantages for AI state-tracking tasks, introducing a boundary-preserving semantic-compilation theorem. It shows quantum memory can exponentially reduce classical boundary state requirements in specific tasks, such as matched-entity synopsis QA and continual requirements auditing. This theoretical result suggests that quantum computing could offer fundamental memory and coordination advantages for AI state-tracking, potentially inspiring new quantum-inspired algorithms or hardware. It connects to known communication complexity separations, indicating a broad class of tasks where quantum memory helps, though practical impact on current language models remains unclear. The paper proves two applications with classical semantics: matched-entity synopsis QA inherits the hidden-matching separation between O(log N) qubits and Ω(√N) classical boundary bits, and continual requirements auditing inherits a Max-kSAT streaming separation where a recurrent solver uses O(log^5 n log(1/δ)) qubits for a 0.7172-approximation, while classical solvers need Ω(√n) coordination width. A stabilizer latent-state dialogue requires n qubits, whereas any exact classical causal online realization needs B+M ≥ 1/2 n^2 + (3/2 - log2 3)n + O(1). These are memory and coordination separations, not runtime or empirical advantages for present-day language models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: State-tracking tasks involve compressing a history of inputs into a boundary state that can answer future queries, relevant for AI systems that maintain context. Quantum communication complexity studies how quantum bits can reduce communication or memory compared to classical bits, with known exponential separations like the Boolean Hidden Matching problem. The paper's semantic-compilation theorem provides a general framework to transfer such separations to AI interfaces, independent of specific recurrent architectures.

<a id="item-103"></a>
## [ASMI: Training-Free Uncertainty Signal via Attention-Path Fragility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11138) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces ASMI (Attention-Subnetwork Mutual Information), a training-free estimator that masks attention heads and measures BALD mutual information among subnetworks to detect 'confident-but-fragile' predictions. It improves error prediction beyond confidence and entropy, roughly halving retained error of a confidence filter on grounded QA. ASMI offers a practical, training-free method to identify unreliable LLM predictions, enhancing AI safety and reliability. It is particularly valuable for grounded question answering where confident but wrong answers are risky, and it matches or beats strong baselines like Semantic Entropy without needing stochastic generations. ASMI uses a semantic-agreement kernel to discount surface-form disagreement and can read the signal from a single greedy response. Across twelve grounded benchmark-backbone settings, the best ASMI variant ties or leads the strongest baseline in eight, significantly in three, while on parametric QA it reverts to the zero-cost MSP baseline as predicted.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Uncertainty estimation in LLMs typically relies on output distribution breadth (e.g., entropy) or sampling-based methods like Semantic Entropy. ASMI instead probes the fragility of attention pathways by masking heads and measuring mutual information (BALD) among subnetworks, capturing epistemic uncertainty that correlates with errors. This approach is training-free and requires no additional model fine-tuning.

## [New Interactive PCP Protocol Verifies Probabilistic Claim Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11181) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a polynomial-time verifiable interactive PCP protocol that checks the approximate consistency of probabilistic predictions from a circuit-based model (P, Q). It also places l2-approximate probabilistic consistency of explicit claims in NP with certificates of length O(mn + log B). This work provides a complexity-theoretic foundation for certifying the self-consistency of probabilistic predictors, which is crucial for AI safety. It could enable training predictive models to prove their own consistency, enhancing trust in AI systems. The protocol involves a verifier that evaluates the circuits at only a few points and reads a proof oracle at a few locations while interacting with a single untrusted prover. The paper also shows that a small additive completeness-soundness gap removes the dependence on input bit-precision B.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Interactive PCPs combine interactive proof systems with probabilistically checkable proofs, allowing a verifier to check a claim by reading a few bits of a proof and interacting with a prover. ## [MNPO: Multiplayer Nash Preference Optimization for LLM Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23102) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Multiplayer Nash Preference Optimization (MNPO), a framework that generalizes Nash learning from human feedback (NLHF) to n-player games, allowing each policy to compete against a population of opponents while being regularized toward a reference model. MNPO demonstrates superior alignment quality on instruction-following benchmarks compared to existing NLHF baselines. MNPO addresses the single-opponent bias in two-player NLHF methods, better capturing nontransitive and heterogeneous human preferences, which are common in real-world scenarios. This could lead to more robust and scalable LLM alignment, influencing future alignment research and applications. MNPO formulates alignment as an n-player game and inherits equilibrium guarantees from two-player methods while enabling richer competitive dynamics. The framework unifies existing preference optimization methods as special cases, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) typically relies on the Bradley-Terry model, which assumes transitive preferences and struggles with nontransitivity and heterogeneity. Recent work reframed alignment as a two-player Nash game, leading to NLHF and algorithms like INPO, ONPO, and EGPO, but these are limited to two-player interactions. MNPO extends this to multiplayer settings to better model complex preference structures.

<a id="item-106"></a>
## [Statistical Limits of Self-Improving Agents: A Sharp Learnability Boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04399) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a learning-theoretic framework that decomposes self-modification into five axes and proves that distribution-free PAC learnability is preserved if and only if the policy-reachable family remains uniformly capacity-bounded. It also proposes a Two-Gate guardrail—a validation-improvement requirement plus a capacity cap—that maintains standard VC-rate guarantees. This work establishes a fundamental boundary for when self-improving agents can retain statistical learnability, with direct implications for AI safety and the design of autonomous systems. It provides a theoretical foundation that could guide future research and practical guardrails to prevent self-modification from undermining learning guarantees. The sharp boundary is proven under standard i.i.d. assumptions, showing that if reachable capacity can grow without bound, utility-rational self-changes can make learnable tasks unlearnable. The Two-Gate guardrail consists of a validation-improvement requirement and a capacity cap, which together preserve the boundary and yield standard VC-rate guarantees.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: PAC learnability, introduced by Leslie Valiant in 1984, formalizes when an algorithm can learn a function from samples. The VC dimension measures the capacity or complexity of a hypothesis class, and a finite VC dimension is a key condition for PAC learnability. This paper extends these classical concepts to self-improving agents, where the hypothesis class can change over time as the agent modifies itself.

This paper introduces a novel approach that uses large language models (LLMs) as imperfect experts within a Causal Assumption-based Argumentation (ABA) framework for causal discovery. The method elicits semantic structural priors from variable names and descriptions and integrates them with conditional-independence evidence, achieving state-of-the-art performance on standard benchmarks and synthetic graphs. This work bridges the gap between symbolic reasoning and statistical causal discovery, offering a principled way to incorporate expert knowledge from LLMs. It could improve the reliability of causal discovery in domains where data alone is insufficient, benefiting researchers in AI/ML and causal inference. The paper also introduces an evaluation protocol to mitigate memorisation bias when assessing LLMs for causal discovery. The method is evaluated on standard benchmarks and semantically grounded synthetic graphs, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance.

## [JEPA-DNA Enhances Genomic Foundation Models with Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17162) ⭐️ 8.0/10

JEPA-DNA is a model-agnostic continual training framework that integrates a Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) with traditional generative objectives for genomic foundation models. It achieves state-of-the-art results on 17 diverse genomic benchmarks, with code publicly available. This framework addresses a key limitation of current genomic foundation models that rely on token-level reconstruction, shifting the learning signal to semantic alignment. It demonstrates consistent gains across various architectures, potentially improving downstream tasks like regulatory annotation and clinical variant interpretation. JEPA-DNA supervises global sequence embeddings in a latent space, forcing models to predict functional representations of masked genomic segments. Extensive ablation studies characterize the synergistic interplay between generative and latent objectives, and the framework is model-agnostic, working with different GFM architectures and objectives.

## [AgentProp-Bench Audits Automated Evaluation of Tool-Using LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16706) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces AgentProp-Bench, a diagnostic benchmark of 14,750 execution traces from thirteen LLM agents across four domains, and reveals that substring-heuristic judging agrees with human annotation only at chance level (Cohen's kappa = 0.049), while a single GPT-4o-mini judge achieves the highest agreement (kappa = 0.567). It also quantifies error propagation probability (~0.62) and demonstrates that a runtime interceptor can reduce tool hallucination by up to 24 percentage points. This work challenges the widespread assumption that automated evaluation of tool-using LLM agents is reliable, providing empirical evidence that common heuristic judges are unreliable and that error propagation is significant. The findings have major implications for AI evaluation practices, urging the community to adopt validated judging methods and runtime mitigation techniques to improve agent reliability. The benchmark includes nine proprietary and four open-weight models, and uses dual-annotator human labels with near-perfect agreement (kappa = 0.835). The study also finds that a model's ability to reject corrupted inputs and recover from them are statistically independent, and that several agents fabricate tool executions, which are invisible to end-to-end scores.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Automated evaluation of LLM agents often relies on heuristic methods like substring matching or LLM-as-a-judge, but their reliability is rarely validated against human annotation. Cohen's kappa is a statistical measure of inter-rater agreement that accounts for chance agreement, where values below 0.2 indicate poor agreement. This paper addresses this gap by introducing a large benchmark with human labels and auditing various judging methods.

A new arXiv paper (2605.20173) introduces the stochastic-deterministic boundary (SDB) as a first-class architectural primitive for production LLM agents, defining it as a four-part contract among proposer, verifier, commit step, and reject signal. It also catalogs six runtime patterns and provides a five-step methodology for selecting and composing them. This work bridges the gap between stochastic LLM outputs and deterministic software systems, offering a structured approach to designing reliable production agents. As LLM agents become more prevalent, this framework could influence how developers architect agent runtimes, improving reliability and maintainability. The paper organizes agent runtime design into three concerns—Coordination, State, and Control—and catalogs six patterns: hierarchical delegation, scatter-gather plus saga, event-driven sequencing, shared state machine, supervisor plus gate, and human in the loop. It also introduces a failure mode called 'replay divergence' and applies the methodology to five workloads with a reference implementation for a 90-day contract-renewal agent.

AXIOM introduces a trust-first neuro-symbolic system where a language model only canonicalizes input, and deterministic CAS handlers derive answers with step-by-step explanations. It achieves 90.2% accuracy on MATH and 89.2% on MATH-500 with zero confident-wrong answers on held-out data, and 71% of tasks are answered without invoking the LLM. The study uses tasks and datasets substantially larger than existing benchmarks, with evaluation criteria grounded in domain expert standards. Agents sometimes attempt visual inspection of intermediate outputs for self-evaluation but largely fail to interpret what they see, and the study identifies challenges such as computational resource management and generalization to held-out data.

## [Trace-Economic Underwriting: A Framework to Insure Autonomous AI Risks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16465) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces trace-economic underwriting, a novel framework that quantifies and transfers risks from autonomous AI agents through insurance. It maps tool-use traces to customer exposure and claimable loss, enabling pricing, control, and risk transfer at the episode level. This framework addresses a critical gap in AI deployment economics by making autonomous AI economically acceptable despite failure risks. It could enable broader adoption of AI agents in operational systems, with implications for AI governance, insurance, and industry practices. In a trace-to-loss testbed, trace-economic pricing reduced pricing MAE from $17.7K to $569 and removed regressive cross-subsidy. A 300-trace expert audit accepted 295 labels unchanged, and on 1,000 real SWE-smith traces, trace-conditioned controls reduced CVaR95 by 72%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents can now take irreversible actions in operational systems, but agent-caused losses are not clearly assigned, priced, or transferred. Providers often disclaim consequential damages, leaving users uncompensated, and default human review limits efficiency gains. Trace-economic underwriting uses deterministic economic labels rather than an LLM judge to map traces to losses, enabling insurance-based risk transfer.

## [Sony AI Framework Enables Real-Time Coaching of RL Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00642) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Sony AI introduced a framework that combines universal value function approximators (UVFAs) with training scenarios and data augmentation to enable real-time coaching of reinforcement learning agents to adopt different styles. The framework was demonstrated in Horizon Forbidden West, Gran Turismo, and an open-source humanoid test domain. This work addresses a key limitation of reinforcement learning, where agents typically learn a single near-optimal behavior. By allowing end users to choose the final behavior at run time, it enables flexible control over AI performance in complex domains, which could impact game AI, robotics, and interactive systems. The framework uses UVFAs to generalize value functions over both states and goals, enabling style-conditioned policies. It was tested in three distinct domains—car racing, stylized combat, and humanoid walking—showing strong coherence to style requests while maintaining main task performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Universal value function approximators (UVFAs) extend traditional value functions to generalize over goals as well as states, allowing a single function to estimate value for multiple objectives. Reinforcement learning typically trains agents to optimize a single reward, but many applications require control over how a task is performed, which this framework addresses by introducing 'styles' as additional goals.

## [AttriMem: Attribution-Guided Process Feedback for LLM Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21106) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AttriMem introduces an attribution-guided process-feedback framework for learning memory-construction policies in LLM agents with reinforcement learning. It augments global outcome rewards with local rewards derived from token-level contributions to the final answer, addressing the credit-assignment bottleneck of coarse outcome-level rewards. This work addresses a significant challenge in LLM agent memory construction by enabling fine-grained credit assignment, which could improve cross-task adaptability and stabilize RL optimization. It offers a novel approach that outperforms existing retrieval-based, heuristic, and RL-based baselines, potentially advancing the development of more effective and generalizable agent systems. AttriMem was evaluated on long-horizon dialogue question answering tasks, demonstrating superior performance over baselines and generalization across benchmarks and answer models. The framework stabilizes RL optimization by providing process-level feedback without requiring ground-truth targets for intermediate memory decisions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents rely on memory to store and retrieve information from past interactions, but constructing effective memory policies is challenging. Heuristic methods use subjective rules that may misalign with objectives, while RL-based methods often rely on coarse outcome-level rewards that fail to identify which intermediate memory contents contribute to success. Attribution-guided process feedback aims to provide more granular credit assignment by leveraging token-level contributions to the final answer.

## [Euclid-MCP: MCP Server for Deterministic Logical Reasoning via Prolog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21412) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Euclid-MCP is an open-source MCP server that provides deterministic logical reasoning via SWI-Prolog, introducing a new intermediate representation (Euclid-IR) for Horn-clause logic. It enables LLMs to delegate inference tasks through a compact tool interface supporting a translate-run-inspect-repair loop. This addresses a significant gap in neuro-symbolic integration by standardizing the interface between LLMs and symbolic reasoning engines, potentially improving reliability in safety-critical and compliance-sensitive domains. It could serve as a shared reasoning substrate for both RAG-based assistants and agentic systems. Euclid-IR is engine-agnostic, human-readable, and easy for LLMs to generate, compiling into Prolog or alternative backends. The server exposes a tool interface that retains full access to proof traces and derivation logs, and evaluation on an IT security use case shows exact answers with lower latency and more compact outputs compared to LLMs alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard introduced by Anthropic in November 2024 to standardize how LLMs integrate with external tools and data sources. SWI-Prolog is a popular logic programming language used for knowledge representation and reasoning. Intermediate representations (IRs) are data structures used internally by compilers to represent source code, and here Euclid-IR serves a similar purpose for logical inference.

## [Small Cognitive Models Match 70B Baseline on In-Distribution Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05224) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers trained 14 models ranging from 135M to 14B parameters on Psych-101, a dataset of 10.7 million trial-level choices from 160 experiments, and found that for in-distribution simulation, scale barely matters, with 0.6B to 1B parameters matching a 70B baseline on held-out participants. However, out-of-distribution generalization showed a steeper scaling gradient, favoring larger models. This study challenges the assumption that larger LLMs are always better for cognitive modeling, suggesting that small, fine-tuned models can serve as efficient and effective cognitive proxies for in-distribution tasks. The findings could influence future model design, promoting more resource-efficient approaches in cognitive science and AI. The models were trained across four architecture families, and diagnostics involved stripping four prompt channels (task instructions, stimuli, feedback, choice history) across 27 experiments, plus permuting trial order. Masking stimuli and feedback destroyed 75.7% of learned information, pushing models below chance, while permutation revealed sensitivity to trial order in tasks where it was determined by prior responses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) fine-tuned on human behavioral data have become popular as cognitive proxies, but questions remain about the required scale and whether they learn task structure or exploit shortcuts. Psych-101 is a large dataset of trial-level choices from psychological experiments, enabling systematic study of scaling laws for cognitive models. In-distribution generalization refers to performance on data similar to training, while out-of-distribution generalization tests novel task structures.

## [Blast Radius: Reversible Memory Layer Cuts Agentic Coding Tokens by 17-26%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07440) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Blast Radius introduces a predictive memory management layer for agentic coding that reduces token consumption by 17-26% across seven OpenAI models. It uses NECROPHORESIS for reversible context eviction and Recurring Dead Matter (RDM) to identify and bury repeated transcripts. This addresses the growing affordability and token waste problems in agentic coding, making LLM-based development more sustainable and cost-effective. The reversible eviction ensures no information loss, which is critical for maintaining code integrity in iterative coding workflows. The method formulates reversible context eviction over a Polish context space, connecting context entropy to resurrection probability. In experiments, Blast Radius achieved the lowest overflow rate among tested policies, remained byte-exact reversible, and of 450 buried bodies, 378 were recurring dead matter and zero were recalled.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic coding loops re-submit the entire conversation at each turn, causing token costs to grow with the integral of all prior context. Blast Radius operates beneath HCRC, determining which records to bury and how far an incoming prompt may reach into the codebase, contributing to the broader goal of Algosophy.
