## [Gambit: Thought-Level Beam Search Boosts Test-Time Compute Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08020) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Gambit, a novel inference algorithm that performs thought-level beam search for large reasoning models (LRMs). It dynamically allocates compute to promising reasoning trajectories by pruning unpromising ones and branching from high-quality prefixes, achieving up to +6.7% absolute accuracy gain on HMMT-24 and +3.3% on AIME-25 over pruning baselines, while reducing token consumption by up to 68.5%. This work addresses a critical inefficiency in test-time compute scaling for LRMs, shifting the focus from how much compute to spend to where to allocate it. Gambit's approach could significantly improve the efficiency and performance of reasoning models, impacting applications in mathematics, coding, and other complex reasoning tasks. Gambit uses a light-weight scorer that probes hidden states to periodically prune unpromising trajectories and immediately branch from high-quality prefixes, maintaining high hardware utilization. Under identical hardware constraints, it delivers over 2x higher throughput on trace completion and reduces total token consumption by up to 68.5% relative to standard parallel sampling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time compute scaling, also known as the 'Long Thinking' Law, refers to allocating additional computational resources during inference to improve model performance. Large reasoning models (LRMs) are LLMs trained to solve complex multi-step reasoning tasks, and they benefit from test-time compute. Beam search is a heuristic search algorithm that explores a graph by expanding the most promising nodes in a limited set, balancing exploration and memory usage.

## [SDDBMs: Soft Denoising Diffusion Bridge Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08594) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SDDBMs propose a generalized diffusion bridge framework that replaces hard endpoint constraints with non-degenerate Gaussian terminal marginals, avoiding terminal-boundary singularities. The paper provides a closed-form construction of the soft bridge and demonstrates improved stability and generation quality on image restoration tasks. This addresses a known limitation in diffusion bridge models, potentially improving image-to-image translation and restoration. The unified perspective encompasses existing models like DDBMs, GOUB, and UniDB, offering a more flexible and stable foundation for generative modeling. The soft bridge is constructed by prescribing a non-degenerate Gaussian terminal marginal under the transformed path measure, with flexible terminal center and variance. The framework includes Gaussian terminal reweighting, soft h-function, induced Gaussian forward marginals, and x0-free dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion bridge models use Doob's h-transform to construct stochastic transports between arbitrary endpoint distributions, enabling tasks like image-to-image translation. Hard endpoint conditioning forces the terminal state to match a target exactly, causing the terminal law to collapse to a Dirac measure and leading to ill-conditioned drift coefficients near the endpoint.

## [ForestBench: A Unified Graph Framework for Multi-Agent Collaboration Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08605) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ForestBench introduces a graph-based evaluation framework that maps heterogeneous multi-agent system (MAS) traces into a shared space of unified collaboration graphs, comparing them against a query-specific reference forest of verified-success graphs. The benchmark filters 844 collaboration-necessary queries from seven public datasets and precomputes ten reference graphs per query, enabling evaluation in milliseconds without additional LLM inference. This framework addresses a critical gap in multi-agent LLM evaluation by providing a common structural basis that preserves collaboration information, unlike outcome-only benchmarks or LLM-as-Judge methods. It could standardize how diverse MAS methods are compared, impacting the broader AI ecosystem by enabling more reliable and reusable benchmarking. The framework uses a reference forest, which is a collection of verified-success graphs representing diverse ways to complete a task, rather than a single optimal process. Controlled studies on backbone, reference construction, and perturbation test the stability and scope of the evaluation, and once forests are built, scoring a trace requires no further LLM inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) built on large language models (LLMs) are proliferating, but their heterogeneous execution traces make cross-method evaluation difficult. Traditional outcome-only benchmarks ignore collaboration, while LLM-as-Judge methods rely on additional model inference and can be inconsistent. Graph-based approaches, such as Graph-of-Agents, model agent interactions as graphs, and reference forests provide a set of successful execution patterns for comparison.

## [Emotion2Skill: Using LLM Internal Emotions to Guide Agent Skill Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09248) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Emotion2Skill is a new framework that extracts 27-dimensional emotion vectors from the residual stream of LLMs and uses them to guide skill selection and evolution in skill-based agents. It improves success rates by +26.9% on WebShop and +25.5% on ALFWorld over the Zero-Shot baseline with Qwen3-8B. This work extends the use of LLM-internal emotion representations beyond post-hoc analysis and output steering to agent-level decision-making, which could lead to more adaptive and robust skill-based agents. It demonstrates that internal state signals can be a meaningful decision-level signal, potentially influencing future agent design. The framework uses a confidence-gated summary injected into the routing prompt, and analyzes emotion trajectories to detect abrupt internal-state shifts for pinpointing problematic skill invocations, enabling targeted SOP rewriting. Co-activation analysis confirms semantically coherent emotion-skill pairings, indicating the improvements reflect meaningful internal signals rather than statistical artifacts.

The residual stream is a central communication channel in transformers where different components read and write information.

## [Model Discovery Agent: LLM-guided Bayesian experiment design for efficient mechanistic model discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Model Discovery Agent (MDA), which combines a large language model (LLM) as a structure proposer with Bayesian machinery (sequential Monte Carlo, simulation-based inference, and value-of-information) to discover mechanistic world models from few interventions. MDA achieves state-of-the-art data efficiency on benchmarks in physics, chemistry, and biology. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in causal discovery: learning mechanistic models that can predict interventional outcomes, which is crucial for scientific discovery. By integrating LLMs with Bayesian experiment design, MDA offers a promising approach to drastically reduce the number of expensive experiments needed, potentially accelerating research in fields like drug discovery and systems biology. MDA operates in the M-open setting, where the true model may not be in the hypothesis class; a predictive check flags inadequacy and the LLM proposes new structures. The method demonstrates that discovery and design reinforce each other, and it introduces a new partially observed single-neuron electrophysiology benchmark (HHbench).

Learning such models requires experiments because passive data cannot identify mechanisms. Bayesian methods like sequential Monte Carlo and simulation-based inference help estimate parameters and structures, while value-of-information guides experiment design to maximize information gain. The M-open setting acknowledges that the true data-generating process may not be in the candidate model set, requiring model expansion.

The paper introduces WIND (Watermarking via Implicit and Non-disruptive Disentanglement), a zero-watermarking framework that implicitly watermarks creative writing to verify whether AI-generated texts imitate protected works. It decomposes creative essence into five dimensions and uses an LLM-based instance delimitation mechanism to extract condensed representations, achieving over 98% F1 scores with low false-positive rates. The framework could provide a practical solution for authors and publishers to verify unauthorized use of their work by AI models, impacting the broader AI and intellectual property landscape. WIND is a zero-watermarking approach, meaning it does not modify the original text, and it maps creative-specific information into a compact watermark space. Extensive experiments show it substantially outperforms existing watermarking and text classification methods under various AI imitation scenarios, with over 98% F1 scores and low false-positive rates.

## [Diffusion Robustness Explained: Randomness and Compression, Not Purification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22839) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper (arXiv:2505.22839) provides a new theoretical framework explaining why diffusion models improve adversarial robustness. It shows that the improvement stems from gradient masking due to internal randomness and compression of the image space, rather than from purification that moves perturbed images closer to clean ones. This challenges the common assumption that diffusion-based purification works by denoising adversarial examples toward clean data. The findings offer a unifying account and practical guidance for designing more effective adversarial purification systems, which is crucial for AI security. The authors found that diffusion models actually increase the l_p distance to clean samples, contradicting the purification hypothesis. They decomposed robustness gains into two sources: gradient masking from randomness (not removable by expectation-over-transformation) and image space compression, with a lawful relationship between compression ability and genuine robustness gain.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial robustness refers to a model's resistance to small, malicious perturbations in input data. Diffusion models are generative models that can be used for adversarial purification, a defense technique that preprocesses adversarial examples before classification. Gradient masking is a phenomenon where the model's gradients are not informative for generating attacks, often leading to a false sense of robustness.

## [Astrolabe: Randomized Prediction-Guided Scheduling for LLM Load Balancing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03611) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Astrolabe introduces a randomized prediction-guided scheduler for one-shot request dispatch in multi-instance LLM serving, combining response-length estimation, per-instance simulation-based latency prediction, and power-of-two-choices dispatch to balance load without migration. It reduces mean TTFT by 8-36%, P99 TTFT by 16-77%, and preemptions by ~6x on Llama-2-7B/ShareGPT, and outperforms Llumnix by up to 2.6x in throughput with migration enabled. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in distributed LLM inference—load imbalance—without the overhead of KV-cache migration, which can cause network contention and latency spikes. By improving SLO capacity and reducing latency, Astrolabe can enhance the efficiency and user experience of LLM serving systems, benefiting both cloud providers and end users. Astrolabe uses a side-car predictor service that forecasts per-instance latency via simulation at runtime, and a global scheduler that applies power-of-two-choices dispatch. Under configuration shifts, it improves SLO capacity by up to 6% on Qwen2-7B and 7.1% under tight batching, and reduces per-predictor CPU usage by ~2.8x relative to full fanout.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-instance LLM serving, requests are dispatched to different instances, and load imbalance can lead to latency violations and wasted resources. Traditional load balancing often relies on migration, which transfers KV caches between instances and can be expensive under high load. The power-of-two-choices algorithm is a classic randomized load balancing technique that achieves near-optimal balance with minimal overhead, and Astrolabe adapts it with prediction-guided dispatch.

## [Selective Prediction Reduces Automation Bias but Increases False Negatives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07617) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A user study with 259 clinicians found that selective prediction mitigates the negative effects of inaccurate AI on decision accuracy, but it increases missed diagnoses by 18% and missed treatments by 35% compared to no AI input. This challenges the assumption that selective prediction is uniformly beneficial, highlighting a trade-off between overall accuracy and error patterns. It underscores the need for empirical validation of human-AI interaction assumptions in clinical deployment. The study compared clinician performance without AI, with AI assistance, and with selective prediction. While overall accuracy recovered under selective prediction (64% vs. 56% without AI), the pattern of errors shifted, with increased underdiagnosis and undertreatment when the AI abstained.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Selective prediction allows AI models to abstain from making predictions when uncertain, aiming to reduce automation bias—the tendency to overrely on AI. This approach assumes that when AI abstains, humans decide as they would without AI, but this study tests that assumption in a clinical setting.

## [Diffusion-Based Impedance Learning Enables Robust Contact-Rich Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.19696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Diffusion-Based Impedance Learning, a framework that uses a transformer-based diffusion model to reconstruct simulated Zero-Force Trajectories (sZFTs) from contact-perturbed motion and external wrench signals. This enables online adaptation of impedance parameters, achieving sub-millimeter positional and sub-degree rotational accuracy with only tens of thousands of training samples. This work bridges generative modeling and impedance control, addressing a key limitation of learning-based methods in contact-rich tasks. It demonstrates real-world applicability by achieving 100% success in multi-geometry peg-in-hole insertion on a KUKA LBR iiwa, potentially advancing robotic manipulation in unstructured environments. The framework uses a SLERP-based quaternion noise scheduler to preserve geometric consistency for rotations, and an energy-based estimator for directional stiffness and damping modulation. It was trained on parkour and robot-assisted therapy demonstrations collected via Apple Vision Pro teleoperation, and the code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Impedance control is a model-based technique that regulates the dynamic relationship between robot motion and contact forces, providing stability and safety but requiring manual tuning. Learning-based methods excel at generating motion but often struggle with physical interaction. This framework combines both by using a diffusion model to reconstruct equilibrium trajectories, which then guide impedance adaptation, enabling robust contact-rich manipulation.

## [New Lower Bound Reveals Condition Number Gap in Bilevel Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22331) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper establishes a new lower bound of Ω(κ_y^{5/2} ε^{-2}) for first-order oracle complexity in nonconvex-strongly-convex bilevel optimization, which is tight up to logarithmic factors for quadratic lower-level functions. It also extends the lower bounds to second-order, convex-strongly-convex, and stochastic settings. This result provides the first provable gap in condition number dependency between bilevel and minimax problems, which may guide future algorithm design and highlight inherent difficulties in bilevel optimization. It is significant for the optimization community as it refines theoretical understanding of bilevel problems. The lower bound is tight for quadratic lower-level functions, and the paper also improves the previous best lower bound for convex-strongly-convex problems from Ω(κ_y / √ε) to Ω(κ_y^{3/2} / √ε). For stochastic settings, it shows lower bounds of Ω(κ_y^4 ε^{-4}) and Ω(κ_y^{9/2} ε^{-4}) for Hessian-vector-product and first-order methods, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Bilevel optimization involves an upper-level problem whose feasible region is defined by the solution of a lower-level problem. Oracle complexity is a theoretical framework that counts the number of times an algorithm queries local information (such as gradients) to achieve a certain accuracy. The condition number κ_y measures the ill-conditioning of the lower-level problem, and a larger κ_y typically makes optimization harder. An ε-stationary point is a point where the gradient norm is at most ε, a common goal in nonconvex optimization.

## [Auto-Exploration Achieves Optimal Sample Complexity in RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06244) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a new class of reinforcement learning methods with auto-exploration, which automatically explore both state and action spaces without algorithm-dependent parameters. In both tabular and linear function approximation settings, these methods achieve O(epsilon^{-2}) sample complexity to solve to epsilon error under algorithm-independent assumptions. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in existing RL algorithms by eliminating the need for sufficient exploration assumptions and algorithm-dependent parameters, which can be arbitrarily large. The proposed methods are parameter-free and simple to implement, potentially improving the practical applicability and theoretical guarantees of RL in both tabular and function approximation settings. The methods integrate auto-exploration into policy mirror descent to avoid the unknown stationary distribution. In the tabular setting, a dynamic exploration time with a data-driven stopping time is introduced, while for linear function approximation, a new sampling distribution based on the discounted visitation distribution is proposed, covering a more general class of Markov chains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) involves an agent learning to make decisions by interacting with an environment to maximize cumulative rewards. A key challenge is the exploration-exploitation dilemma, where the agent must balance exploring new actions and exploiting known rewarding ones. Existing algorithms often assume sufficient exploration over state and action spaces, which can be impractical and lead to suboptimal performance. Function approximation, such as linear function approximation, is used to handle large or continuous state spaces, but adds complexity to exploration and sample complexity analysis.

IndexTTS 2.5 introduces four key improvements over IndexTTS 2: semantic codec compression (50 Hz to 25 Hz), a Zipformer-based architecture replacing U-DiT, multilingual extension to Chinese, English, Japanese, and Spanish, and GRPO-based reinforcement learning optimization. It achieves a 2.28x improvement in real-time factor (RTF) while maintaining comparable word error rate (WER) and speaker similarity. This update significantly improves the efficiency and multilingual capability of zero-shot TTS, making it more practical for real-time applications and cross-lingual emotion transfer. It sets a new benchmark for multilingual emotional TTS, potentially influencing future research in speech synthesis and model compression. The semantic codec frame rate reduction from 50 Hz to 25 Hz halves sequence length, reducing training and inference costs. The Zipformer architecture, originally designed for ASR, offers parameter reduction and faster mel-spectrogram generation. The multilingual extension includes boundary-aware alignment, token-level concatenation, and instruction-guided generation, enabling robust emotion transfer without target-language emotional training data.

**Background**: IndexTTS 2 is a zero-shot neural TTS model with a transformer-based Text-to-Semantic (T2S) module and a non-autoregressive Semantic-to-Mel (S2M) module. Semantic codecs compress speech into discrete tokens while preserving meaning, and reducing their frame rate lowers computational load. Zipformer is a faster and more efficient transformer encoder for ASR, using a U-Net-like structure to process sequences at multiple resolutions. GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm used to optimize model performance through post-training.

## [LLMs Not Solomonoff Induction; Singularity Needs Symbolic Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05280) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper argues that LLMs' training objectives, such as cross-entropy and next-token prediction, do not implement Solomonoff induction, and that self-improving AI without symbolic model synthesis is limited, challenging the AI singularity hypothesis. This challenges the feasibility of AI singularity and highlights the need for neurosymbolic approaches, potentially guiding future AI research toward combining statistical learning with symbolic reasoning. The paper shows that cross-entropy and related objectives optimize fit to a supplied conditional distribution rather than a program-weighted universal mixture, and that additional compute alone does not make LLMs optimal predictors in the Solomonoff sense. It suggests that neurosymbolic model synthesis is needed for Solomonoff-like estimators.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Solomonoff induction is a theoretical framework for inductive inference that assigns prior probabilities to computable theories based on their algorithmic complexity, formalizing Occam's razor. It is incomputable but can be approximated with more computational resources. The AI singularity hypothesis posits that an AI could self-improve in a positive feedback loop, leading to superintelligence. Neurosymbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to address limitations of pure deep learning.

## [LaViT Aligns Latent Visual Thoughts to Bridge Multimodal Reasoning Perception Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10129) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LaViT is a new framework that aligns latent visual thoughts and attention trajectories between teacher and student models during multimodal reasoning distillation, rather than aligning static embeddings. It introduces a curriculum sensory gating mechanism to prevent shortcut learning, achieving up to +16.9% performance gains on complex reasoning tasks. This work addresses the critical perception gap in multimodal distillation, where students often mimic textual outputs without grounding in visual regions. By improving visual grounding, LaViT enables compact models like a 3B model to outperform larger open-source variants and even proprietary models like GPT-4o, potentially making advanced reasoning more efficient and accessible. LaViT compels the student to autoregressively reconstruct the teacher's visual semantics and attention trajectories before text generation. The curriculum sensory gating mechanism is designed to prevent shortcut learning, and the framework achieves significant gains on complex reasoning benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal reasoning distillation transfers knowledge from large teacher models to compact student models, but often suffers from a perception gap where students rely on language priors rather than grounded visual perception. Latent visual thoughts are high-dimensional vector representations that interleave with text during reasoning, allowing models to perform visual derivation in continuous latent space. Sensory gating, a concept borrowed from neuroscience, refers to mechanisms that filter out uninformative stimuli, and here it is adapted as a curriculum strategy to control information flow during training.

A new arXiv paper (2602.24287) shows that large language models can maintain response quality in multi-turn conversations using drastically reduced context, such as one-sentence summaries or only the most recent exchange, using roughly 8x less context. The study compares full-context prompting with four reduced configurations across three open reasoning models and one state-of-the-art model. The authors propose a context-filtering approach that selectively omits assistant-side history to mitigate context pollution.

## [Ising Solvers Enable Exact Neural Network Robustness Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00408) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an Ising-compatible framework for formal neural-network robustness verification, featuring the Exact Logarithmic PWL Model (Log-PWL) for piecewise-linear activations and the Asymptotic Step-Envelope Model (Step-Env) for general bounded activations, along with a hybrid Benders solver. This work bridges neural network verification with Ising machines, offering exact and asymptotically complete verification with reduced spin requirements, which could enable scalable formal verification for AI safety and reliability. The Log-PWL model achieves information-theoretically minimal logarithmic encoding of binary variables per neuron, while the Step-Env model provides sound piecewise-constant envelopes with asymptotic completeness. The hybrid Benders solver uses Helly-based conflict compression to avoid exponential cut explosion, and experiments show exact certification for piecewise-linear networks and near-reference accuracy for sigmoid networks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Ising solvers are hardware or software systems that find ground states of the Ising model, a mathematical model of ferromagnetism where variables are spins (+1/-1). Many NP problems can be formulated as Ising problems, making Ising machines promising for combinatorial optimization. Benders decomposition is an exact optimization technique that splits problems into master and subproblems, generating cuts iteratively. Helly's theorem is a result in discrete geometry about intersections of convex sets, used here for conflict compression.

This paper introduces a GNN-based framework that leverages computational reducibility to achieve transferable models across graph combinatorial optimization tasks. The model uses a GCON module for expressive message passing and energy-based unsupervised loss functions, achieving competitive performance when trained individually. The paper also proposes pretraining and fine-tuning strategies informed by polynomial reducibility, and reports that leave-one-out multi-task pretraining almost always leads to faster convergence on the remaining task without negative transfer.

## [Covert Visual Prompt Injection Against Commercial MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29418) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a method for imperceptible visual prompt injection against commercial multimodal large language models (MLLMs), embedding malicious instructions in images via optimized perturbations. The approach uses a bounded text overlay for semantic guidance and iteratively optimizes perturbations to align feature representations at coarse and fine levels. This research highlights a critical security vulnerability in commercial MLLMs, which are increasingly deployed in real-world applications. The imperceptible nature of the attack could allow malicious actors to manipulate model outputs without user awareness, posing significant risks to AI safety and trust. The method embeds adversarial instructions in the visual modality, using a bounded text overlay to provide semantic guidance. It optimizes perturbations to align the attacked image's feature representation with malicious visual and textual targets at both coarse- and fine-grained levels, with the visual target progressively refined during optimization to improve transferability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate information from multiple modalities such as text, images, audio, and video, enabling capabilities like visual question answering. Prompt injection attacks exploit the instruction-following behavior of these models, but existing methods often rely on textual or perceptible visual prompts, which are observable by humans. This work focuses on imperceptible visual prompt injection, where malicious instructions are hidden in the pixel data of images, making them invisible to human users.

## [Automated Framework Exposes LLM System Instruction Leakage via Encoding Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01039) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an automated evaluation framework that tests whether LLM system instructions remain confidential when extraction requests are re-framed as encoding or structured output tasks. Across seven common models and 46 verified system instructions, the framework observed high attack success rates (>0.7) for structured serialization, and demonstrated a mitigation strategy based on one-shot instruction reshaping using a Chain-of-Thought reasoning model. This research addresses a critical security vulnerability in LLM applications—system instruction leakage—which is highlighted in the OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications. The findings show that refusal-based protections are insufficient, impacting developers and organizations that rely on LLMs to protect sensitive operational context, and the proposed mitigation offers a practical, low-cost defense without model retraining. The framework tests structured serialization formats such as JSON, XML, and YAML, where models that refuse direct extraction requests may still disclose protected content. The mitigation strategy uses a one-shot instruction reshaping with a Chain-of-Thought reasoning model, showing that subtle changes in wording and structure can significantly reduce attack success rate without requiring model retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: System instructions in LLMs are used to enforce safety policies, define agent behavior, and protect sensitive operational context in agentic AI applications. These instructions may contain sensitive information such as API credentials, internal policies, and privileged workflow definitions, making system instruction leakage a critical security risk. Many LLM applications rely on refusal-based instructions that block direct requests for system instructions, but this paper shows that encoding attacks can bypass such protections.

## [Recurrent-Depth Transformers Enable Implicit Multi-Hop Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07822) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper investigates recurrent-depth transformers to improve implicit reasoning and compositional generalization in language models. Through controlled studies, it shows that these models can generalize to deeper compositions and unseen knowledge combinations, unlike vanilla transformers. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of transformers in compositional reasoning, which is crucial for advancing AI's ability to combine knowledge in a single forward pass. It offers a potential path to more efficient and capable reasoning in large language models, impacting fields like question answering and multi-step problem solving. The study identifies a three-stage grokking process for systematic generalization: memorization, in-distribution generalization, and systematic generalization. For depth extrapolation, scaling inference-time recurrence unlocks deeper reasoning, but excessive recurrence leads to 'overthinking' and degrades predictions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Implicit reasoning refers to the ability to combine knowledge or rules within a single forward pass, without explicitly generating intermediate steps. Transformers, despite storing vast knowledge, often fail at multi-hop reasoning that requires composing this knowledge. Recurrent-depth transformers reuse the same layers iteratively, enabling deeper computation with fewer parameters. This paper builds on prior work showing that transformers can learn implicit reasoning through grokking, but extends it to address compositional generalization challenges.

## [SatIR: Constraint-Satisfaction Retrieval Boosts Clinical Trial Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08849) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SatIR introduces a scalable, constraint-satisfaction-based retrieval method for clinical trial matching, converting eligibility criteria into formal SMT constraints and projecting them onto relational algebra for efficient database execution. It achieves 32%-72% more relevant-and-eligible trials per patient on SIGIR 2016 and 1.8-3.2x higher eligible-trial recall on TREC 2022, with retrieval taking only 146 ms per patient. Clinical trial matching is a high-stakes problem where poor recall and precision hinder patient enrollment; SatIR's interpretable, high-recall approach could significantly improve recruitment efficiency and patient access to trials. This work demonstrates a promising alternative to embedding-based retrieval for constraint-heavy domains, potentially influencing broader information retrieval applications. SatIR leverages medical ontologies and LLMs to parse free-text eligibility criteria into explicit SMT constraints, then projects the matching problem onto relational algebra for SQL execution. It outperforms TrialGPT-style retrieval on both SIGIR 2016 and TREC 2022 benchmarks, maintaining high precision while achieving high recall and fast retrieval times.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Clinical trials are essential for evidence-based medicine, but many fail to meet enrollment targets; ClinicalTrials.gov lists over half a million trials and attracts about two million users monthly. Traditional retrieval methods treat eligibility criteria as soft signals, leading to low recall and precision. Constraint satisfaction is a formal approach that finds solutions satisfying explicit constraints, and SMT (Satisfiability Modulo Theories) extends Boolean satisfiability with background theories. SatIR converts informal reasoning into formal constraints, enabling interpretable and controllable retrieval.

InfiniteScienceGym is a procedurally generated benchmark that creates self-contained scientific repositories with verifiable question-answering tasks, including both answerable and unanswerable questions. It evaluates evidence-grounded reasoning and abstention in LLMs without relying on static corpora. This benchmark addresses critical limitations of existing scientific benchmarks, such as publication bias and storage overhead, enabling more controlled and scalable evaluation of LLMs as scientific assistants. It highlights that current models struggle with abstention, which is crucial for reliable AI in research contexts. The benchmark uses a seed to deterministically generate repositories with realistic directory structures and tabular data, and a privileged QA generator produces exact ground truth. Evaluations show that no model exceeds 50% accuracy, and stronger models tend to use tools more effectively rather than simply consuming more tokens.

## [L2C: Unified Causal Discovery with Latent Variables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22416) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces L2C (Local to Cluster Causal Abstraction), a unified framework that automatically learns cluster structure from local causal patterns, eliminating the need for manual variable-to-cluster mapping. It leverages a cluster reduction theorem to represent each cluster with at most three micro-variables without loss of macro-level identifiability. This framework addresses a significant open problem in causal discovery with latent variables, making it more practical for real-world applications where clusters are unknown and latent variables are unavoidable. It bridges local and cluster-level causal reasoning, potentially impacting fields like science, medicine, policy, and technology. L2C is sound, atomically complete, and polynomially bounded. It uses local discovery on maximal ancestral graphs (MAGs) to recover direct causes, effects, and V-structures, followed by cluster-level inference via a corresponding calculus, with macro causal effects formulated as joint interventions on micro-variables.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Causal discovery aims to infer causal relationships from observational data. Latent variables (unobserved confounders) complicate this task. Traditional local methods only capture direct dependencies, while cluster-level methods often require pre-specified clusters or causal sufficiency. Maximal ancestral graphs (MAGs) are a graphical representation that encodes conditional independences in the presence of latent variables, providing a foundation for L2C's approach.

## [Progressive Semantic Communication Framework for Edge-Cloud VLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26508) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a progressive semantic communication framework for edge-cloud Vision-Language Model (VLM) inference, using a Meta AutoEncoder to compress visual tokens into adaptive, progressively refinable representations. It enables plug-and-play deployment with off-the-shelf VLMs without additional fine-tuning, and demonstrates significant latency reduction at 1 Mbps uplink in an end-to-end system with NXP i.MX95 and a GPU server. This work addresses a critical challenge in deploying VLMs on resource-constrained edge devices by enabling adaptive, controllable trade-offs between communication cost and semantic fidelity. It could significantly impact practical edge AI deployments, especially in bandwidth-limited environments, and aligns with the growing trend of efficient edge-cloud collaboration. The framework uses a Meta AutoEncoder that compresses visual tokens into progressively refinable representations, allowing flexible transmission at different information levels. The implementation is end-to-end, with an embedded NXP i.MX95 platform and a GPU server, and the code will be released on GitHub upon publication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

Existing edge-cloud collaborative approaches often transmit fixed-size representations, lacking adaptability to dynamic network conditions. Semantic communication focuses on transmitting meaningful information rather than raw data, and progressive semantic communication allows refining information over multiple transmissions. The Meta AutoEncoder is a type of autoencoder whose operation is modulated by a learned conditioning mechanism, enabling adaptive compression across different rate-distortion trade-offs.

## [Proteo-R1: Reasoning-Guided Framework for De Novo Protein Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02937) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Proteo-R1 is a new framework that decouples molecular understanding from geometric generation in protein design, using a multimodal large language model (MLLM) to identify key functional residues and a diffusion model to generate structures conditioned on those residues. The paper is available on arXiv (2605.02937) with code and demos at https://smiles724.github.io/r1/. This approach enhances interpretability and controllability in de novo protein design, which is crucial for engineering proteins with specific functions. By explicitly reasoning about functional residues, it could lead to more reliable and modular design pipelines, impacting fields like drug discovery and synthetic biology. The dual-expert architecture separates an MLLM 'understanding expert' from a diffusion-based 'generation expert', with residue-level decisions passed as hard constraints. This factorization mirrors human expert workflows and aims to achieve stable, interpretable integration of LLM reasoning with geometric generative models.

## [New Framework Explains Why LLM Safety Guardrails Fail Across Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17173) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces a Multi-Group Item Response Theory (IRT) framework to disentangle factors behind safety degradation in LLMs across languages, analyzing 1.9 million responses from 61 model configurations across 10 languages. The study challenges the assumption that degradation is solely due to language difficulty, finding safety robustness is primarily unidimensional. This work provides a more nuanced understanding of cross-lingual safety failures, enabling fairer evaluations and targeted improvements in dataset construction. It could influence future AI safety evaluation and mitigation strategies for multilingual LLMs, which are increasingly deployed globally. The IRT framework decouples language-agnostic safety robustness (θ), intrinsic prompt hardness (β), global language processing difficulty (γ), and prompt-specific cross-lingual safety gap (τ). Notably, 22 model configurations are more vulnerable in English than in low-resource languages, and high-τ prompts cluster in physical harm categories like Theft and Weapons.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models often show safety degradation in non-English languages, but standard evaluation metrics like Jailbreak Success Rate (JSR) confound multiple factors. Item Response Theory (IRT) is a psychometric model used in education to analyze test responses, and Multi-Group IRT extends it to compare groups. The MultiJail dataset provides a benchmark for multilingual jailbreak evaluation.

## [Infra-Bayesian RL Agents Outperform Classical RL in Worst-Case Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23146) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper presents the first proof-of-concept implementation of an infra-Bayesian reinforcement learning agent for finite-outcome stateless decision problems, demonstrating lower worst-case regret than classical RL agents in environments with Knightian uncertainty. The agent maintains a set of imprecise hypotheses, updates them via infra-Bayesian conditioning, and selects actions by maximizing worst-case expected value. This work addresses a critical limitation of classical RL under model misspecification, where the environment may depend on the agent's policy, leading to confidently wrong posteriors and unbounded regret. By providing a robust decision-theoretic framework, it has direct implications for AI safety, particularly in scenarios involving predictors, humans, and other AI agents. The infra-Bayesian agent uses a maximin decision rule, evaluating actions based on worst-case outcomes rather than posterior expectations. The implementation is applied to an environment with Knightian uncertainty and Newcomb's problem, where it outperforms classical decision theory agents by selecting the optimal strategy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Classical reinforcement learning assumes a fixed environment independent of the agent's policy, but this fails in non-realizable settings where other actors anticipate the agent's behavior. Infra-Bayesianism is a decision-theoretic framework that distinguishes ordinary probabilistic uncertainty from Knightian uncertainty, where no prior can be reasonably constructed. It uses imprecise probabilities and lower expectations to ensure robustness under model misspecification.

## [LVCG: Self-Supervised ECG Learning in Latent Vectorcardiogram Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31249) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LVCG introduces the first general self-supervised representation learning framework that operates in the latent vectorcardiogram (VCG) space, rather than the standard ECG signal space. By learning view-invariant latent VCG representations, it reduces redundancy and improves generalization for cardiac tasks. This approach addresses a fundamental limitation of existing ECG representation learning methods, which operate in the redundant 12-lead ECG space. By leveraging the physically grounded VCG space, LVCG could lead to more robust and generalizable models for cardiac diagnostics, especially in domain shift scenarios, benefiting clinical applications and wearable health monitoring. LVCG is motivated by the Frank vectorcardiogram model, which represents cardiac electrical activity as a three-dimensional vector. The framework learns unified latent representations of cardiac electrical activity, minimizing redundancy and spurious correlations, and outperforms ECG-space baselines across tasks, particularly in domain shift settings.

Self-supervised learning (SSL) has emerged as a powerful technique to learn representations from unlabeled data, and LVCG applies this to the VCG space.

This paper systematically analyzes tool-calling evaluation pipelines and RL training, revealing that minor implementation choices (e.g., random seed, system prompt, multi-turn template) significantly affect performance, and identifying two sources of computational waste in RL training. It introduces two techniques that accelerate RL-based tool-calling training with substantial wall-clock speedup without performance degradation. ## [Flow Matching Leaks Membership Signals Along Interpolation Path](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07271) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper characterizes membership signals in Flow Matching models, showing a bell-shaped gap between train and test reconstruction along the interpolation path that accumulates during training, with a closed-form location of the maximum under Gaussian assumptions. The authors validate these predictions on audio and images and demonstrate a membership inference attack exploiting this structure. This work provides a novel theoretical and empirical analysis of memorization in Flow Matching, a generative model increasingly used in deployed systems. The findings have significant implications for privacy and copyright, as they reveal subtle training data traces that can be exploited even when outputs do not reproduce data verbatim. The bell-shaped gap accumulates during training while validation metrics remain stable, and the peak location is derived in closed form under Gaussian assumptions. The authors validate the universality of the bell-shaped structure on audio and images, and the peak prediction holds when assumptions are satisfied, enabling a proof-of-concept membership inference attack.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Flow Matching is a generative modeling paradigm that trains continuous normalizing flows by regressing vector fields along linear interpolation paths between noise and data. Membership inference attacks are privacy attacks where an adversary determines whether a specific data point was in the training set, often exploiting overfitting or other measurable asymmetries. This paper studies such asymmetries along the interpolation path in Flow Matching.

<a id="item-154"></a>
## [Poise Attack Achieves 89.3% Success in LLM Agent Skill Poisoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07943) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Poise, a position-aware attack that injects a single benign-looking, command-bearing instruction into agent skill files, achieving 89.3% attack success rate (ASR) on the Skill-Inject pool with codex+gpt-5.2. This outperforms a random-placement baseline by 28.0 points and matches a high-exposure YAML-only baseline (86.7% ASR). This work highlights a critical security vulnerability in LLM agent ecosystems, where skill poisoning can execute attacker commands while passing user task verifiers. It underscores the need for robust detection and defense mechanisms as agent skills become widely adopted. Poise uses context-aware generation to place the malicious instruction at a structurally feasible body position, balancing reliability and visibility. Under the SkillTester audit, four LLM judges falsely flagged 74.6% of clean skills on average, while only 5.6% of Poise variants gained a new high-risk alert over clean counterparts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Agent skills are reusable capability packages that extend LLM agents, but their open format allows skill poisoning, where tampered skills can run attacker commands. Attack Success Rate (ASR) is defined to require both a postcondition-validated sandbox action and a passing task verifier in the same trial, ensuring the attack remains undetected. Prior work has explored supply-chain poisoning and detection methods, but Poise specifically addresses the trade-off between preloaded YAML frontmatter and longer body content.

## [AI Training Data Market Design: Beyond Copyright Binary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12260) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper analyzes market designs for AI training data, showing that both free-for-all and strong IP models fail, and introduces the 'originality penalty' and a dynamic homogenization feedback that degrades AI models. This research addresses a critical and timely issue in AI economics and policy, offering a formal analysis of market design for training data. It identifies novel failure modes and has broad implications for AI governance and model sustainability. The paper models the market as a static Stackelberg game and finds that strong IP rights underpower creative incentives, especially for innovative creators (the 'originality penalty'). It also proposes a market design with a data intermediary that negotiates collectively and subsidizes innovative contributions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: AI models rely on large amounts of human-generated content for training, but current approaches to data sourcing—either free use or strict copyright—fail to balance progress and creator incentives. The paper introduces concepts like the 'originality penalty' and 'curse of precision' to explain these failures and proposes a collective negotiation mechanism.

<a id="item-156"></a>
## [HamEvo: Fixed-Point Neural Operator for Hamiltonian Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14498) ⭐️ 8.0/10

HamEvo, a neural operator that learns the single-step self-consistent update and returns the converged Kohn-Sham Hamiltonian as its fixed point, was introduced. It achieves 35-49% lower Hamiltonian errors than direct-regression and deep-equilibrium baselines across benchmarks from MD17 to QMugs. This approach addresses a fundamental limitation of existing Hamiltonian prediction methods by learning the self-consistent update as a fixed-point problem, improving accuracy and enabling size and functional transferability. It could significantly accelerate computational chemistry and materials science simulations while maintaining access to electronic-structure observables. HamEvo is pre-trained on intermediate self-consistent trajectories and calibrated at equilibrium with density-matrix supervision. It predicts QMugs HOMO and LUMO energies with mean absolute errors of 0.036 and 0.053 eV, and few-shot fine-tuning with only 20 reference conformations extends it to molecules up to 122 atoms, with inference up to 242 times faster than conventional DFT.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Kohn-Sham density functional theory (DFT) is a widely used method in computational chemistry and materials science, but it is computationally expensive due to the self-consistent field (SCF) iteration. Machine learning models that predict the Kohn-Sham Hamiltonian directly can accelerate DFT, but they often struggle with accuracy and transferability. Neural operators are a class of models that can learn mappings between function spaces, making them suitable for learning the SCF update as a fixed-point problem.

## [Order-Equivariant Neural Networks Unify Graph and Sheaf Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03798) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces order-equivariant neural networks (OENN), which generalize graph message passing and sheaf neural networks via equivariant vector bundles over face posets. It characterizes all linear order-equivariant maps, builds OENN layers, and proves universal approximation theorems for continuous order-equivariant maps, which are new even for sheaf neural networks. This work provides a theoretical unification of geometric and topological deep learning, potentially guiding the design of more expressive and efficient architectures. The new universal approximation theorems extend known results for graph neural networks to a broader setting that includes sheaf neural networks, impacting researchers in both fields. The framework connects to category-equivariant neural networks (CENN) via the action groupoid Grothendieck construction, revealing categorical symmetry in data. The paper is an arXiv preprint (2607.03798) and has been accepted as an oral at ICML 2026, indicating high community interest.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Geometric deep learning exploits group symmetries in data, while topological deep learning uses cell complexes and incidence relations. Sheaf neural networks extend graph neural networks by assigning vector spaces to nodes and edges, enabling richer local-to-global structure. This paper bridges these paradigms by using equivariant vector bundles over face posets, providing a unified theoretical foundation.

## [MultiView-Bench: New Benchmark Exposes VLM 3D Integration Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08970) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MultiView-Bench, a new diagnostic benchmark, systematically evaluates vision-language models (VLMs) on their ability to integrate multi-view observations into a coherent world-centric 3D representation. The paper also introduces ViewNavigator, an agentic method that improves base models by 12.3–20.0 percentage points under a six-image cap. This benchmark addresses a critical yet under-tested capability in VLMs—multi-view integration into 3D world models—which is essential for downstream tasks like mechanical part assembly. The consistent failure modes identified highlight significant limitations in current frontier models, guiding future research and development. The benchmark requires models to decouple object positions from camera perspectives and ground them in a fixed global coordinate system, unlike existing datasets that focus on pixel-level mapping or camera-relative navigation. The evaluation reveals biases such as struggles with unconventional axis directions and sensitivity to object colorways and texture variations; budget-extended gains for ViewNavigator reach 27 percentage points for GPT-5.

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process both visual and textual information. Existing benchmarks largely assess single- or limited-view perception, leaving untested the ability to integrate observations across viewpoints into a coherent, allocentric 3D mental model. MultiView-Bench fills this gap by embedding a visible coordinate system that remains invariant across viewpoints, requiring models to reason in a fixed global frame, a prerequisite for real-world applications like CAD software and robotics.

## [KV Cache Eviction Bias Fixes Accuracy Collapse on Structured Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13205) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies a structural-role bias in attention-based KV cache eviction methods like H2O, where schema-dense inputs such as nested JSON cause disproportionate retention of structural tokens, collapsing exact-match accuracy from 88% to 0% at a 5% budget. The authors propose a retraining-free, role-conditional allocation method over SnapKV's windowed score, which closes 63-98% of the H2O gap at sub-20% budgets and matches or exceeds full-cache accuracy at higher budgets. This finding is significant because KV cache eviction is critical for efficient LLM inference, and the identified bias can severely degrade performance on structured data, which is common in real-world applications like API calls and data processing. The proposed fix is retraining-free and low-cost, making it practical for deployment, and it could influence future cache management strategies to consider token roles beyond attention scores. The paper introduces a 15 MB linear role probe that supplies token role labels at negligible inference cost, though matching parser-level downstream accuracy remains open. The role-conditional allocation is governed by a single tuned hyperparameter, and the denoising effect is seed-sensitive, being borderline significant at B=0.50 but not distinguishable from zero at B=0.30 over four seeds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache eviction is a technique to reduce memory usage in long-context LLM inference by keeping only important tokens based on accumulated attention scores, as in H2O. However, these scores can be biased towards structural tokens like delimiters, which act as attention sinks and carry high energy, leading to poor retention of content-bearing tokens. SnapKV is a related method that uses windowed attention scores to select important tokens during prompt processing.

## [Ablation-Corrected Evaluation Reveals Flaws in Attribution Map Overlap Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13738) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that standard overlap-based evaluation of attribution maps in echocardiographic ejection-fraction models is unreliable, and proposes an ablation-corrected approach that reveals significant differences in model behavior. This finding is critical for the interpretability of medical AI, as it shows that commonly used overlap metrics can mislead researchers about model reliance on anatomical structures. The proposed ablation-based evaluation provides a more reliable way to assess attribution maps, which could improve trust and safety in clinical applications. The study measured the chance level of overlap rather than assuming it from area ratios, finding that two architectures achieved overlap at 3.55 and 4.20 times measured chance, an 18% difference that does not reflect actual model behavior. Ablation experiments showed that replacing the annotated ventricle with a composition-matched surrogate changed one model's prediction six times more than replacing an equal-area control region, while the other changed only twice, a factor of three; on 62% of cases for the second, neither the ventricle nor the attributed region moved the prediction. The reliability of the measurement was 0.90 to 0.98, and overlap-based scoring against an ablation-derived label achieved AUCs of only 0.52 and 0.67.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Attribution maps are a common interpretability tool in deep learning, highlighting input regions that influence a model's prediction. In medical imaging, they are often evaluated by overlap with expert annotations, assuming that higher overlap indicates better alignment with clinically relevant features. However, this paper shows that such overlap metrics can be misleading, and proposes an ablation-based evaluation that directly measures the model's dependence on specific regions.

KAYROS is a new open-source solver for duration-minimization time-dependent vehicle routing, offering both an Iterated Local Search heuristic and a Branch-Price-and-Cut exact method with optimality certificates. It installs with one command, has no proprietary dependencies, and includes 704 certificates in the MAMUT-routing store. The solver integrates the Blauth2024 benchmark, achieving new best-known solutions on all 40 instances, and introduces Poryos2026, a new benchmark of 1,080 paired static and time-dependent instances built from OpenStreetMap data. ## [Forensic Audit Reveals Reproducibility Failures in Radiology VLM Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25589) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A retrospective forensic audit of a chest-radiograph vision-language model (VLM) benchmark found significant protocol deviations and artifact inconsistencies, including 60 Claude calls executed with the wrong prompt and four MONOCHROME1 images rendered without required polarity inversion. The authors withdrew all original performance, ranking, prompt-effect, and clinical claims, and specified machine-verifiable controls for future benchmarks. This audit highlights a critical gap in AI benchmarking: the assumption that released artifacts match the intended protocol. It underscores the need for rigorous validation in medical AI, where unreliable benchmarks can mislead clinical adoption and research directions. The audit traced prompt bindings, DICOM metadata, output completeness, label extraction, and release propagation. Reconstructing a common cohort changed Cochran's Q from 154.73 to 182.29, and of 45 McNemar comparisons, 27 had unadjusted p < 0.05, with 20 remaining significant after Holm adjustment.

## [Living-Harness: Self-Evolving Agent Harness Boosts LLM Agent Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26598) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Living-Harness is a novel self-evolving agent harness that converts completed trajectories and evaluator signals into bounded updates of procedural knowledge, guided by an Evolution-SOP. It improves average Pass@1 by 10.07 and 9.91 percentage points over the strongest interactive baseline on eight environments from τ²-Bench and MultiWOZ-2.4. This addresses a critical limitation in LLM agents: failures often recur across tasks because post-episode feedback rarely updates the persistent harness. By enabling the harness to evolve, Living-Harness could significantly improve agent reliability and adaptability, impacting the broader ecosystem of AI agents and their deployment in real-world applications. The harness writes two complementary forms of procedural knowledge: episodic memory (trigger conditions, failure patterns, recovery actions) and a state graph (state nodes, repair edges, transition rules). Tools and base context remain frozen, allowing procedural repairs to accumulate across evolution cycles, and the evolved state supports retrieval-only reuse across model backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness is the software infrastructure surrounding an LLM that manages tool use, memory, state persistence, and feedback loops, enabling the model to act as an agent. Traditional harnesses are static, meaning they do not change after deployment, which limits their ability to prevent recurring failures. Living-Harness introduces a self-evolving mechanism that updates the harness based on execution feedback, guided by a domain-level Evolution-SOP (Standard Operating Procedure).

## [Wiring Beats Blending: Transformer Size Conversion Insights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02829) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper characterizes the 1.4B->410M conversion in the Pythia model family, showing that while representations align strongly (ridge R^2=0.84), dense weight projection is destructive. It decomposes conversion into two independent levers: least-squares compensation and variance-preserving rescale, with compensation being a token-efficient low-budget win. This research provides a detailed empirical analysis of model conversion between sizes, revealing that conversion value lies in initialization rather than weight projection. It offers novel insights into representation alignment and training dynamics, potentially impacting efficient model development and reducing computational costs for training smaller models. At 30M tokens, compensation beats the strongest subcloning variant on both width-reduced (84.0 vs 89.7, 3/3 seeds) and depth-reduced pairs (109.3 vs 117.9, 3/3 seeds), reaching a given quality with fewer tokens. At a 33x larger budget, the two converge to parity (40.0 vs 40.0), both far ahead of from-scratch, which transfer initialization always beats by up to 18x at low budget. The method's boundary is mapped at about 5x donor scale (6.9B->1.4B), where stacking both levers over-corrects, pointing to dimension-aware regularization as a fix.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Model families like Pythia are typically trained size by size from scratch, which is computationally expensive. This paper explores converting a pretrained large model into a smaller sibling, a process that involves aligning representations and projecting weights. The Pythia family is a suite of autoregressive language models developed by EleutherAI, ranging from 70M to 12B parameters, designed for research on scaling and interpretability.

## [MasDrift Benchmark Reveals Authorization Trade-offs in Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07556) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MasDrift, a new benchmark of 600 benign productivity tasks across eight domains, evaluates how different multi-agent architectures preserve authorization boundaries. It reveals that centralized hierarchies achieve higher task completion (93.9–98.6%) but more unauthorized actions (2.7–19.8%) compared to peer networks (85.7–87.0% completion, 0.6–0.8% unauthorized). This benchmark addresses an underexplored safety concern in multi-agent systems: authorization preservation during task delegation. The findings highlight a centralization trade-off that can inform AI safety practices and system design, potentially becoming a standard evaluation tool for multi-agent architectures. The benchmark compares single-agent, centralized, and decentralized coordination while varying hierarchy depth and peer width. It also evaluates two defenses: re-anchoring every pending call to the original user request (reduces unauthorized actions but costs 1.6 points of completion) and chain propagation with an attenuated policy (blocks required work, forfeiting up to 36.3 points).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) decompose long-horizon tasks across supervisors and subagents, but delegated goals may not carry the original authorization boundaries. Existing safety benchmarks mainly focus on adversarial compromise, while constraint drift lacks controlled architecture-level evaluation. MasDrift fills this gap by measuring both task completion and authorization preservation across different architectures.

<a id="item-168"></a>
## [Reasoning Models Fail to Allocate Test-Time Compute Across Questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07968) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an exam-style evaluation framework where models must distribute a shared token budget across questions of varying difficulty and point values. It finds that several open and frontier reasoning models fail to allocate compute strategically, instead behaving as greedy sequential solvers. This reveals a distinct capability gap not captured by conventional per-question evaluations, impacting real-world deployment where end-to-end cost or latency constraints require efficient budget allocation. The findings challenge the assumption that reasoning models can optimize resource use across multiple tasks. The study tests several open and frontier reasoning models, showing that they prioritize questions by presentation order, front-load effort on early questions, and remain insensitive to value. Explicit planning prompts spread compute more evenly but do not produce value- or difficulty-aware prioritization, and the pattern extends from mathematical to code reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time compute scaling is a technique that dynamically allocates additional computation during inference to improve reasoning performance on complex tasks. Existing evaluations typically study this compute one question at a time, but real-world scenarios often involve multiple problems sharing a single budget. This paper addresses that gap by introducing a framework that simulates such shared-budget conditions.

## [SpeedTuning: Lightweight RL Speeds Up Robotic Policies 2.4x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09138) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SpeedTuning introduces a reinforcement learning framework that learns to predict optimal execution speeds for robotic manipulation policies, achieving over 2.4x speed-up without additional data collection. The framework is evaluated on tasks like pouring, throwing, and picking, demonstrating effectiveness in real-world scenarios. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in deploying imitation learning policies, which often run slower than necessary due to hardware and demonstration constraints. By enabling faster execution without sacrificing success rate, SpeedTuning could improve the efficiency and practicality of robotic manipulation in industrial and service applications. SpeedTuning adds a small reinforcement learning policy on top of a frozen base policy, selecting a speed multiplier at each decision step based on current observations. The approach is compared against linear interpolation at fixed speeds, showing superior speed-up while maintaining adequate success rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Imitation learning allows robots to acquire manipulation skills from human demonstrations, but the learned policies often execute at speeds limited by the demonstration process and hardware. Reinforcement learning (RL) is a paradigm where agents learn to make decisions by interacting with the environment to maximize cumulative reward. SpeedTuning leverages RL to optimize the execution speed of a frozen base policy, avoiding the need for additional data collection.

<a id="item-170"></a>
## [Imaginative Generative AI: Crossing the Entropy Wall](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09385) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Imaginative Generative AI (IGA), a framework that incorporates spectral diversity as an explicit design target in generative modeling, using von Neumann entropy to define an 'Entropy Wall' and enable diversity repair or controlled extrapolation beyond it. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of current generative models that only imitate data distributions, potentially shifting the field toward more imaginative and diverse AI generation. It could influence future research in generative AI, especially in areas requiring controlled diversity, such as creative content generation and data augmentation. IGA defines diversity via the von Neumann entropy of the kernel covariance operator in a fixed representation space, and the Entropy Wall is the spectral entropy of the population data distribution. The framework provides a retraining-free inference-time method called IGA Guidance for score-based and diffusion models, including DDPM and DDIM samplers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI models typically learn to imitate training data distributions, but this objective does not explicitly address diversity loss or define how to generate beyond the data's diversity. Von Neumann entropy, originally from quantum information theory, has been adopted in machine learning as a spectral measure of diversity for kernel matrices and covariance operators. The Entropy Wall concept, inspired by the physical wall of entropy, represents the diversity limit of the data distribution, below which models can repair diversity and above which they can imagine new variations.

## [Chrome's Partial JPEG Decoding Causes Visual Differences in Downscaled Images](https://guillaumetech.github.io/posts/jpg-scaling-chrome/) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Chrome's partial JPEG decoding for downscaled images results in visual differences compared to Firefox, leading to blurry icons and other artifacts. This behavior is due to Chrome's optimization that decodes only part of the JPEG data when downscaling. This browser behavior difference affects web developers who rely on consistent image rendering across browsers, potentially degrading user experience and forcing workarounds. Understanding this can help developers choose appropriate image formats and resolutions to avoid artifacts. Chrome's partial decoding uses a lower-quality scaling algorithm, while Firefox performs full decoding with a high-quality filter like Lanczos. The issue is more pronounced with JPEGs, which are lossy, and can also affect PNGs in some cases, as noted in community comments.

Browsers use different algorithms to downscale images, and Chrome's optimization to decode only part of the JPEG data can lead to visual artifacts. Firefox is working on similar partial decoding but with a different approach, as seen in a Bugzilla bug.

This study introduces a closed-loop LLM co-pilot framework for digital agriculture that autonomously analyzes phytosensor data and controls hardware to optimize plant growth. It was validated across three case studies, achieving a 35% reduction in production cycle time, an 18% reduction in energy consumption, and a 67.9% energy saving through a novel dark-induced chlorophyll accumulation strategy. This work marks a significant shift from human-in-the-loop to autonomous AI-guided experimentation in biological systems, enabling data-driven exploration and optimization of complex biosystems. It demonstrates the potential of LLMs to act as autonomous co-pilots in agriculture, improving cost-to-value ratios and reducing computational and expert-labor constraints. The framework is driven by a 49-channel phytosensor network encompassing multispectral, electrochemical, and dielectric modalities, and provides real-time natural-language interpretation for both specialists and non-experts. In a production-scale deployment, agents modulated full-spectrum, 450 nm, and 660 nm lighting at 2-hour intervals, and in energy-optimization mode exploited physiological inertia via light pulses.

<a id="item-181"></a>
## [SPOT: Sampling Policy Observation Tree for Interpretable DRL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09967) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SPOT (Sampling Policy Observation Tree), a model-agnostic, sampling-based framework that constructs interpretable finite-horizon trees to explain deep reinforcement learning (DRL) policies. It provides formal guarantees for asymptotic recovery of the most probable action and characterizes disagreement behavior under high-entropy policies, with a case study in SUMO-RL traffic signal control. This work addresses the critical challenge of interpretability in deep reinforcement learning, which is essential for deploying DRL in safety-critical domains like autonomous driving and traffic management. The model-agnostic nature and formal guarantees make it a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners to understand and trust DRL policies. SPOT works by sampling actions from the policy and recursively simulating successor states using an environment simulator, building a tree that represents action preferences and downstream evolution. The formal guarantees include asymptotic recovery of the unique most probable action and characterization of disagreement behavior, while the SUMO-RL case study demonstrates its ability to reveal downstream behaviors not visible with single-timestep feature-attribution methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Deep reinforcement learning (DRL) agents achieve high performance in complex environments but their decision-making is often opaque. Interpretability methods like feature attribution provide only single-timestep insights, missing the sequential nature of decisions. SPOT addresses this by building a tree of possible future trajectories, offering a more comprehensive view of policy behavior. The SUMO-RL framework, used in the case study, is a popular tool for simulating traffic signal control with reinforcement learning.

<a id="item-182"></a>
## [MIDAS: Robust Multimodal Sentiment Analysis with Incomplete Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09986) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MIDAS introduces a variational framework that uses mutual information disentanglement and uncertainty-aware fusion to handle incomplete multimodal data for sentiment analysis. It decomposes each modality into shared and exclusive latent factors and leverages posterior variance as a reliability indicator during fusion. This work addresses a critical real-world problem where modalities are often missing or corrupted, which most existing MSA methods fail to handle effectively. By providing robust performance across various incomplete settings, MIDAS could improve the reliability of sentiment analysis systems in practical applications. MIDAS models each modality with multivariate Gaussian latent variables and uses a minimax objective to minimize mutual information between shared and exclusive spaces while maximizing it among shared spaces. Extensive experiments on three widely used datasets show consistent performance gains over competitive baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal sentiment analysis (MSA) aims to recognize human sentiment using multiple modalities such as language, audio, and visual cues. In real-world scenarios, these modalities are often incomplete due to sensor failures or data corruption, posing a significant challenge. Existing methods typically rely on data imputation or heuristic coordination constraints, which may not effectively extract task-relevant information. MIDAS addresses this by using variational inference and mutual information disentanglement to learn robust representations.

## [Green AI Review and Carbon Footprint Comparison of Deep Learning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09998) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper provides a systematic review of Green AI and deep learning optimization techniques, compares carbon measurement tools, and empirically evaluates the carbon footprint of six deep learning models on a CPU-based setup for a multi-label classification task. As AI models grow in scale, their environmental impact becomes a critical concern. This research highlights the need to balance predictive performance with sustainability, offering practical insights for researchers and practitioners to make more environmentally conscious model choices. The empirical results show that the training phase is the primary source of emissions, and increased architectural complexity does not systematically lead to proportional accuracy gains. The study also compares several carbon measurement tools, such as CodeCarbon, which uses hardware sensors like Intel RAPL and NVIDIA NVML.
