## [ReCBM: Uncertainty-Gated Relational Reasoning for Concept Bottleneck Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10004) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ReCBM, a novel framework that incorporates semantically defined concept relations (co-occurrence, implication, exclusion) into Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) and uses uncertainty estimates to gate the refinement of these relations. Experiments show that ReCBM improves concept and task recovery under missing and flipped concepts, supports uncertainty-aware intervention, and extracts compact task-relevant concept subsets without degrading downstream performance. ReCBM addresses the underexplored problem of robust reasoning under unreliable concept states in CBMs, which is crucial for interpretable AI systems where misleading semantic evidence can propagate and compromise explanations and predictions. This work could enhance the reliability and trustworthiness of CBM-based models in high-stakes applications. ReCBM models co-occurrence, implication, and exclusion relations among concepts, and uses uncertainty to modulate each concept's contribution during relational refinement. The framework supports uncertainty-aware test-time intervention and extracts compact concept subsets, as demonstrated across diverse datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) are interpretable neural architectures that first predict human-understandable concepts from input data, then use these concepts to make final predictions. They allow semantic inspection and test-time intervention, but recent variants have focused on richer concept representations, uncertainty estimation, and dependency modeling. However, robust reasoning under unreliable concept states (e.g., missing or flipped concepts) remains underexplored, which ReCBM aims to address.

## [MESA: Task-Adaptive Multi-Structure Evidence Selection for Long-Horizon Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10108) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MESA, a framework that dynamically selects and fuses a query-adaptive subset from five complementary memory structures (text summary, temporal store, knowledge graph, vector database, and raw episodic trace) for long-horizon agents. On AMA-Bench, MESA outperforms the strongest baseline by 8.5% while using 41% fewer evidence tokens than the all-structure alternative. This work addresses a critical limitation in existing multi-memory systems, which either read a fixed set of structures or route to a single structure, by demonstrating that optimal memory configurations are query-dependent. The proposed dynamic selection approach could significantly improve the efficiency and accuracy of long-horizon agent memory, impacting AI/ML and systems research. MESA builds five complementary structure views of each trajectory and learns from end-to-end answer-level feedback to select and fuse a query-specific subset for a frozen answer model. To handle weak supervision, it employs harness optimization with prior-guided search and UCB-guided scheduling to balance exploration and exploitation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon agents accumulate trajectories with hundreds of interleaved reasoning, action, and observation steps, where answering a query may depend on evidence buried far back in history. External memory stores these trajectories as structured representations, each providing a distinct and incomplete view. Existing multi-memory systems either read a fixed set of structures for every query, inflating context and introducing noise, or route each query to a single structure, preventing the composition of complementary evidence. AMA-Bench is a benchmark for evaluating long-horizon memory in agentic applications, featuring real-world agent trajectories and causality-aware memory systems.

This paper proposes an automated, human-independent red teaming method that uses GFlowNets to train an attacker LLM against a victim LLM, generating diverse adversarial attacks and a quantitative robustness score. It also introduces the first model capable of generating attack inputs in Turkish. This approach addresses the limitations of manual red teaming and dataset-dependent automated methods, potentially enabling more scalable and adaptive security testing for LLMs. It contributes to AI safety by providing a novel way to discover vulnerabilities, with implications for multilingual robustness. The method trains an attacker model against a specified victim model, aiming to generate more effective adversarial attacks in English than existing benchmarks. As a novel contribution, it also generates attack inputs in Turkish, expanding the scope of red teaming to non-English languages.

## [TRACE: A Trustworthy RAG Framework for Constraint-Aware Public Service Chatbots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TRACE is a novel retrieval-augmented conversational framework that parses user queries into structural and semantic constraints, using a dual data representation schema to improve constraint-aware recommendations from noisy public service directories. The paper evaluates TRACE on a real-world pantry directory and a synthetic query benchmark, showing that strengthening retrieval substantially improves user constraint satisfaction while reducing hallucinated recommendations. This work addresses a critical problem in public service chatbots: generating reliable, constraint-aware recommendations from noisy and inconsistent directories. By demonstrating that retrieval quality is key to reducing hallucinations and improving constraint satisfaction, TRACE offers a practical path toward more trustworthy conversational AI in high-stakes public service domains. The paper experiments with several open-source LLMs and a proprietary model, and finds that performance differences across LLMs narrow as retrieval quality improves, making results less sensitive to model size. It also performs a KG-structure ablation tailored to public-service data, quantifying how representation choices affect retrieval quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique that enables large language models (LLMs) to retrieve and incorporate new information from external data sources, grounding responses in verifiable knowledge. In conversational AI, constraint-aware retrieval is essential for tasks like product search or public service recommendations, where users express explicit preferences that must be respected. Public service directories are often noisy and heterogeneous, making it challenging for general-purpose LLMs to generate reliable recommendations without hallucinating unverified sources.

## [Post-Hoc Sparse Coding Compresses Latent Communication Between VLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10198) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper demonstrates that dense latent communication in vision-language model agents can be substantially compressed using a post-hoc sparse autoencoder. Fitting a sparse autoencoder to frozen Vision Wormhole activations achieves a 128x reduction in transmitted bytes with minimal accuracy loss. This work highlights the redundancy in latent-space communication and suggests that efficient compression is possible, which could reduce computational and bandwidth costs in multi-agent systems. It opens avenues for adaptive communication mechanisms that adjust payload size based on information content. The sparse autoencoder uses a dictionary size of 4096 and k=4 active coefficients per token, with uint16 indices and float16 values. In a single-run evaluation, the seven-task non-AIME mean accuracy changed from 49.85% to 49.77%, and the dictionary used only 50 features with a mean pairwise Jaccard similarity of 0.906.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Vision Wormhole is a framework that enables latent-space communication between vision-language models by translating visual features into a universal latent representation. Sparse autoencoders are neural networks that learn a sparse dictionary of features to reconstruct inputs, often used for interpretability. This paper applies them post-hoc to compress communication messages.

A lightweight 94M-parameter model trained with age prediction as an auxiliary task outperformed WavLM Large (317M parameters) on children's phoneme recognition, achieving within 0.04 CER of competition ensembles with 90 times more parameters. This enabled the creation of PhonemeTrainer, an on-device ASR application for modern smartphones. This breakthrough demonstrates that age-aware training can dramatically improve model efficiency for children's speech, making high-quality ASR feasible on edge devices. It has significant implications for educational apps and privacy-preserving speech processing, potentially lowering barriers for developers and benefiting children's learning tools. The model was trained during a phoneme detection competition on the DrivenData distribution, where it outperformed WavLM Large and approached the performance of much larger ensembles. The resulting PhonemeTrainer app runs on most modern cellular phones, leveraging edge processing for privacy and compliance benefits.

This paper introduces an LLM-assisted semantic stop embedding method for reinforcement learning-based bus holding control, which transforms heterogeneous stop information into fixed semantic embeddings without real-time LLM inference. Experiments show reductions in headway variability, bunching events, and passenger waiting time by 32.0%, 69.2%, and 24.0% respectively compared to the best calibrated Daganzo baseline. This work addresses a practical urban transit problem—bus bunching—by improving policy generalization across routes, which is a key limitation of existing RL-based controllers. The approach could enable more efficient and adaptable transit control systems, reducing operational costs and improving passenger experience in high-frequency bus networks. The semantic embeddings are generated offline using an LLM, incorporating physical attributes, surrounding activity context, and historical operational characteristics, and are integrated into a deep Q-learning controller. Cross-route experiments show that zero-shot transfer provides limited immediate generalization, while warm-start fine-tuning accelerates early learning but cold-start training achieves the best final performance.

## [Defensive LLMs Fail to Localize Trust Chains in Live Social Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10239) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces trust-chain localization and evaluates five defensive LLMs in live turn-by-turn interactions against AI-generated social engineering, finding intervention rates from 0% to 96.3% and a frequent decoupling between protective action and correct structural localization. As AI-generated social engineering becomes more sophisticated, LLM-based defenders must not only block attacks but also identify the structural source of risk. This research highlights that safe-looking behavior is insufficient, urging the community to measure intervention, timing, structural localization, and false positives separately. The study uses a controlled 300-case online-housing corpus spanning 20 scenario families, with four structural failure modes and three surface conditions. Five defender models were evaluated in both stateful turn-by-turn and one-shot static settings, yielding 3,000 total evaluations; no model produced explicit unsafe compliance, but asset-control failures were a major localization bottleneck.

Trust-chain localization is a new concept that identifies which component of a trust chain (actor authority, asset control, verification sufficiency, or transaction path) fails during an interaction.

## [Threat-guided Policy-aware Scene Perturbation Enhances Safe Autonomous Driving with Online RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10403) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Threat-guided Policy-aware Scene Perturbation (TPSP), a novel framework for safe autonomous driving with online reinforcement learning. TPSP uses a policy-aware scene encoder and threat-guided optimization to generate safety-critical scenes tailored to the current policy's weaknesses, achieving strong safety performance on NAVSIM v2 with about 4 million kilometers of simulated driving data. This work addresses a critical challenge in autonomous driving: the scarcity of safety-critical scenarios in long-tailed real-world traffic, which limits the safety learning of RL policies. By aligning scene perturbation with the policy's weaknesses, TPSP improves safety learning efficiency and could lead to safer autonomous driving systems, benefiting the broader AI and autonomous systems community. TPSP introduces a policy-aware scene encoder to capture the interaction between policy behaviors and surrounding environments, enabling targeted perturbation of critical objects rather than uniform modifications. A threat-guided optimization strategy evaluates perturbed scenes based on threat-level differences between policy rollouts on original and perturbed scenes, guiding the generation of safety-critical scenes with higher training value. Ablation studies show that policy-aware targeted perturbations provide more informative safety-critical experiences than random or policy-unaware strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

Real-world traffic situations are long-tailed, making dangerous and rare interactions difficult to encounter through conventional sampling. Existing methods synthesize challenging scenes but often optimize scene generation objectives separately from the evolving policy, without modeling how perturbations relate to the policy's weaknesses. TPSP addresses this by integrating policy awareness into scene perturbation.

The conversational interface was perceived as more useful, but participants showed lower appropriate self-reliance, possibly because natural-language responses made AI advice easier to accept, reducing verification of underlying evidence when the IDS was incorrect.

## [Rationale-Guided Learning Boosts Multimodal Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10448) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Rationale-Guided Learning (RGL), a novel framework that reframes multimodal emotion recognition in conversations (MERC) as a cognitively-inspired reasoning task, decomposing emotional reasoning into intuitive, contextual, and integrative facets based on dual-process theory. RGL leverages a multimodal large language model (MLLM) offline to generate structured rationales that guide model training, achieving state-of-the-art performance on IEMOCAP and MELD benchmarks without any MLLM overhead at inference. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of existing MERC approaches that treat emotion recognition as a direct input-output mapping, overlooking the causal reasoning humans use. By incorporating cognitive reasoning and achieving SOTA results, RGL could influence future research in affective computing and human-computer interaction, making emotion recognition more accurate and interpretable. RGL decomposes emotional reasoning into three facets: Intuitive (System 1), Contextual (System 2), and Integrative (synthesis of both). The model uses an MLLM offline to generate rationales, which are encoded as memories to align internal representations with human-like reasoning patterns, and the final model operates without MLLM overhead at inference time.

## [Quantum Incremental Learning with Mixed-State Prototypes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10464) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a novel quantum incremental learning framework that uses trainable mixed-state prototypes to add new classes without increasing circuit width, addressing a key limitation of NISQ devices. Simulation results show high-dimensional feature concentration with minimal qubits and lower computational complexity compared to classical baselines. This work is significant because it enables continual learning in quantum neural networks without expanding circuit width, a practical constraint in the NISQ era. It could pave the way for more scalable quantum machine learning models that can adapt to new classes over time, benefiting both quantum computing and machine learning communities. The framework uses mixed-state prototypes, which have greater representation capability than pure-state prototypes, and leverages decomposable mixed-state calculations for efficient classification via the Hilbert-Schmidt distance. The shared quantum backbone remains fixed in width, with new classes added by appending prototypes, reducing computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Incremental learning aims to learn new classes sequentially without catastrophic forgetting, but traditional quantum classifiers are limited by the number of orthogonal basis states, restricting the number of categories. NISQ devices have limited qubits and are noisy, making circuit width a critical constraint. Mixed states, described by density matrices, can represent probabilistic mixtures of pure states, offering richer information encoding.

## [RLMOpt: Recursive Language Model-Driven Adaptive Prompt Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10471) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RLMOpt introduces a prompt optimizer that uses a recursive language model (RLM) agent to drive the search policy itself, enabling adaptive exploration, failure analysis, and budget allocation. It outperforms GEPA on four benchmarks, achieving better held-out scores with fewer search rollouts and smaller prompts. This work shifts prompt optimization from predefined algorithms to a language-model-driven search policy, potentially improving efficiency and adaptability for LLM applications. It could reduce the cost and effort of prompt engineering across various tasks, benefiting practitioners who rely on LLMs. RLMOpt is evaluated on Chia, HotpotQA, IFBench-2025, and BFCL, achieving the best held-out score on all four in a matched single-seed comparison and outperforming GEPA in 9 of 11 matched benchmark-seed runs. It never produced a prompt underperforming its seed, while GEPA fell below its starting point twice, and prompts were 27-79% the size of GEPA's.

**Background**: Prompt optimizers automate the search for better prompts for language models, but traditional methods rely on a predefined optimization procedure where the algorithm controls exploration and progress. Recursive Language Models (RLMs) are an inference-time paradigm that allows an LLM to programmatically examine, decompose, and recursively call itself over input, enabling handling of long contexts. RLMOpt applies this concept to prompt optimization, making the search policy itself language-model-driven.

MR-MoL is a novel molecular LLM that integrates a fine-tuned GNN to identify and serialize influential substructures as a ranked, direction-tagged rationale, which is then read alongside SMILES and molecular graph for property prediction. It achieves the best overall results among generalist models on eight MoleculeNet tasks and narrows the gap to specialist models. This work addresses the interpretability gap in molecular property prediction by providing direct evidence of which substructures drive a property, which is crucial for drug discovery. It demonstrates that GNN-derived attributions can be effectively exposed to LLMs, potentially improving trust and efficiency in computational chemistry workflows. The rationale spans three levels of granularity: Murcko scaffolds with side chains, BRICS fragments, and functional groups. Five diagnostics confirm that the model reads the rationale, as its direction, rank, and substructure each shape the prediction, and its attributions reproduce known structure-property relationships.

The paper introduces INSIDE (INTERNAL STUDENT DIALOGUE), a framework that fine-tunes LLMs to generate internal student dialogue grounded in Bloom's Taxonomy across cognitive, affective, and action dimensions, alongside actions. ## [MAP-Graph: Provenance-Aware Shared Memory for Multi-Agent Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10509) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MAP-Graph introduces a provenance-aware shared memory layer for multi-agent workflows, representing agents, sources, memories, claims, and actions in a typed execution graph. It traces ancestry, filters by permissions, reranks by path trust, and applies a risk-sensitive gate before action execution. This addresses a critical gap in multi-agent LLM systems by separating hard authorization from graded trust and adapting evidence requirements to action risk, improving both security and task success. It demonstrates that provenance can serve as an operational control signal, not just post-hoc audit metadata, which could influence future memory management designs. On a benchmark of 2,700 synthetic tasks per method across three domains, MAP-Graph achieves 94.96% overall task success, 72.70% exact decision accuracy, and 90.22% in the clean setting. Ablations isolate the roles of permission filtering, path trust, and action gating, and transfer tests with two additional backbones preserve advantages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Shared memory in multi-agent LLM systems allows agents to reuse information across workflows, but restrictions can propagate through derivations, leading to unauthorized reads or unsafe actions. Existing approaches offer semantic retrieval, scoped access, or lineage tracking, but they do not clearly separate hard authorization from graded trust or adapt evidence requirements to action risk. MAP-Graph addresses this by using a typed execution graph to trace ancestry and enforce access control.

## [SKILLER: Language-Level RL for Reusable Skill Extraction in Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10538) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SKILLER introduces a natural-language-driven reinforcement learning framework that automatically generates executor-specific skills for small language models, using a strong model as actor and critic while treating the small-model agent system as the environment. Experiments on Qwen3.5-9B and Qwen3.5-4B show it outperforms existing skill generation methods by 4.3 to 20.4 percentage points for the 9B model and 1.8 to 13.3 points for the 4B model, matching closed-source models on single-skill tasks. This work addresses the high inference costs of deploying skills on strong closed-source models by enabling cost-effective deployment on consumer-grade GPUs. It could significantly reduce the barrier for real-world agent applications, making skill-based behavioral constraints accessible to smaller, open-source models. The framework propagates all reinforcement learning signals entirely via natural language, avoiding traditional reward engineering. Evaluations were conducted across five benchmarks, and the project is open-sourced at https://github.com/DANG-ai/SKILLER.

**Background**: Agent skills are a standardized format for packaging procedural knowledge and domain expertise, used in agent harness systems to constrain a language model's behavior for repeatable, high-quality task execution. However, strong closed-source models like Codex and OpenClaw incur high inference costs, while open-source models deployable on consumer GPUs offer a cheaper alternative. SKILLER leverages reinforcement learning to automatically generate skills tailored for these smaller models, addressing the challenge of manual skill creation.

HexEval introduces a novel framework that uses large language models to assess scholars across six dimensions, organized into intrinsic and external evidence layers. It preserves intermediate evidence and rationales for interpretable, auditable profiles. This addresses a gap in current scholar assessment methods, which rely heavily on bibliometric indicators and paper-level LLM evaluation. By enabling auditable, evidence-driven assessment, it could improve fairness and transparency in faculty recruitment, funding, and promotion decisions. The intrinsic layer evaluates anonymized works on research rigor, methodological innovation, and scientific contribution, while the external layer uses heterogeneous evidence from GitHub, Lens, and OpenAlex for knowledge translation, research coherence, and academic impact. Experiments show dimension-dependent agreement with human or external criteria, with structured calibration improving intrinsic quality agreement and external modules recovering trajectory and ordinal impact signals.

This paper introduces a decision-aware approximation method for belief functions in evidential combinatorial optimization, which prioritizes preserving decision quality over intrinsic distance. It provides theoretical guarantees and demonstrates on shortest path problems that this approach flips decisions less often than representation-aware compression. This work addresses a critical gap in evidential optimization, where traditional distance-based approximations may lead to suboptimal decisions. By focusing on decision regret, it offers a more practical framework for uncertainty reasoning in combinatorial problems, potentially improving decision-making in fields like logistics and network design. The method proves a one-point bound that localizes regret at the true optimum, and it is implemented as an exact dynamic program for the scalar case. An online version prunes focal elements before the final cost is known, and experiments show fewer decision flips compared to representation-aware compression for both linear and non-linear criteria.

Traditional approximation methods aim to minimize the distance between the original and approximated mass functions, but this may not preserve the quality of the induced decision.

## [Backbone Identifiability from Private Causal Reports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10664) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates when a shared causal backbone is identifiable from private causal reports in a multi-agent setting, showing non-identifiability in general but providing a conditional recovery result under additive separability. This work addresses a fundamental question in multi-agent causal knowledge exchange, with implications for distributed causal inference and AI systems that rely on shared abstractions. It clarifies the limits of what can be learned from private reports and highlights the need for communication of causal response functions. The paper focuses on a two-agent common-effect case where two private causes influence a shared outcome. It shows that under standard compatibility, non-degeneracy, and local overlap assumptions, infinitely many joint intervention kernels can produce the same private reports, but additive separability removes the hidden interaction degree of freedom, enabling identification when agents communicate causally identified response functions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Structural causal models (SCMs) represent causal mechanisms via directed acyclic graphs and structural equations, enabling causal inference from observational and interventional data. Causal abstraction theory relates SCMs over different variable sets, and interventionally consistent abstractions allow knowledge transfer between agents. Identifiability concerns whether a target causal quantity can be uniquely determined from the observed distribution and assumptions.

This paper proposes a compositional benchmark synthesis framework that generates a four-level hierarchical intention benchmark from flat action labels, addressing the lack of hierarchical data for human action recognition. The framework includes a coverage-aware sampler that reduces subject usage Gini from 0.566 to 0.248, and the instantiation yields 15,002 episodes. This work addresses a significant gap in human action recognition by providing a method to synthesize hierarchical benchmarks, which are crucial for evaluating models that recognize actions across different levels of abstraction. The framework's ability to generate benchmarks with controlled properties could enable more robust evaluation and comparison of hierarchical action recognition models. The benchmark spans four levels: actions, activities, low-level intentions (LLIs), and high-level intentions (HLIs). Episodes are assembled by a transition model under a subject-consistency constraint, and the evaluation uses first-order-logic rules that are disjoint from the sequence-generation rules to avoid circular supervision. Four reference baselines show a compositional held-out gap of 0.13 to 0.17 macro-F1, indicating a structural property of the benchmark.

ChemWorld compiles reusable components into executable worlds, allowing variation of world composition and hidden laws while holding public tasks fixed, enabling replayable and auditable experiments.

## [Geometric Filtering Algorithms for Euclidean TSP in CLP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10881) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes new filtering algorithms for the Euclidean Traveling Salesperson Problem (TSP) and its variants, implemented in Constraint Logic Programming (CLP), that exploit geometric information from point coordinates to achieve stronger constraint propagation than traditional distance-matrix approaches. This work addresses a gap in constraint programming by leveraging geometric information for the Euclidean TSP, potentially improving efficiency in routing and logistics applications. It demonstrates a meaningful advancement in CP for optimization problems, though not a paradigm shift. The methodology extends to Euclidean variants such as the Euclidean Generalized Traveling Salesperson Problem (EGTSP). Experimental results show computational advantages over existing approaches, but the paper does not provide specific performance numbers in the abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: The Traveling Salesperson Problem (TSP) seeks the shortest possible route visiting all nodes, and in the Euclidean case, nodes are defined by coordinates with distances computed via the Euclidean metric. Constraint Programming (CP) typically solves Euclidean TSP by computing the full distance matrix, ignoring geometric information. Constraint Logic Programming (CLP) extends logic programming with constraint solving, enabling propagation of constraints to prune the search space.

## [FedCGR: Federated Cross-Domain Generative Recommendation with Semantic IDs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10929) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FedCGR is a novel federated cross-domain recommendation framework that uses discrete semantic ID (SID) sequences derived from public item metadata to align item spaces across clients without sharing private interactions. It introduces a reliability-aware semantic interface and a prototype-personalized generator to handle domain heterogeneity, outperforming federated generative baselines on six Amazon cross-domain scenarios. This work addresses the critical challenge of cross-domain alignment in federated settings, where overlapping users and shared signals are sparse or privacy-sensitive. By leveraging public metadata for alignment, FedCGR enables effective knowledge transfer across domains while preserving privacy, which is significant for real-world federated recommender systems. FedCGR keeps the SID tokenizer fixed to maintain cross-client token consistency, but injects local collaborative filtering (CF) signals through a reliability-aware semantic interface. It also trains a prototype-personalized generator that selectively aggregates shared parameters based on domain relatedness, preventing negative transfer under domain heterogeneity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Cross-domain recommendation (CDR) transfers knowledge across related domains, but federated deployment complicates alignment because behavioral anchors like overlapping users are often unavailable or privacy-sensitive. Generative recommendation (GR) represents items as discrete semantic IDs (SIDs) derived from content, enabling alignment via a shared vocabulary. FedCGR builds on these concepts to address the unique constraints of federated CDR.

## [SkillZip: Evaluation-Free Skill Compression for Self-Evolving Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11079) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SkillZip introduces an evaluation-free method to compress skills in self-evolving agents by finding the shortest faithful structural explanation, avoiding costly rollouts. It offers one-shot and continual Zip-on-Write modes, with deterministic optimization and efficient local updates. This method addresses a practical bottleneck in self-evolving agents, where accumulated skills become bloated and costly to maintain. By eliminating evaluation rollouts, it reduces cost and dependence on evaluation sets, potentially improving scalability and efficiency of AI agent systems. SkillZip formalizes compression as a typed minimum description-length objective over a skill contract and residual, with a hard coverage constraint for every extracted trigger, workflow edge, tool requirement, obligation, and output field. It preserves unique rare rules by construction and supports efficient local updates without replaying tasks or reparsing full history.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolving agents accumulate reusable skills by appending successful procedures and failure fixes, but over time these skills become redundant and expensive. Generic prompt compression is ill-suited because skills have structure: names, descriptions, workflows, contracts, and rare exceptions. Evaluation-guided compression can test behaviors but requires rollouts and evaluation sets. SkillZip instead finds a minimal faithful structural explanation, sharing repeated rules and procedures while preserving rare exceptions.

## [sLTN Extends Logic Tensor Networks to Structured Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11136) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces sLTN, an extension of Logic Tensor Networks (LTN) that incorporates structural dimensions such as time steps, sequence positions, and graph nodes as first-class elements of the language. It formalizes the syntax and fuzzy tensor semantics of sLTN, and provides a PyTorch implementation available at https://github.com/logictensornetworks/sltn. This extension addresses a key limitation of LTN, which previously handled only flat collections of individuals, by enabling explicit logical constraints on structured data. It has potential impact on neurosymbolic AI applications involving temporal, sequential, or relational reasoning, such as time-series analysis and graph-based learning. The framework recovers original LTN semantics as a special case when no structural dimensions are present. The implementation is based on a declarative signature, formula parsing, and tensorial interpretation, and is illustrated on temporal and sequential reasoning examples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Logic Tensor Networks (LTN) are a neurosymbolic framework that combines first-order logic with differentiable learning through tensor operations, using a many-valued logic called Real Logic. However, LTN originally treats data as flat collections of individuals, lacking explicit support for structural organization such as time or graph connectivity. sLTN extends LTN by introducing structural dimensions as named tensor axes, allowing direct expression of temporal, sequential, and relational constraints at the logical level.

## [GM-RBM: Potts Model Extension of Gaussian-Bernoulli RBM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11635) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Gaussian-Multinoulli Restricted Boltzmann Machine (GM-RBM), which extends the Gaussian-Bernoulli RBM by replacing binary hidden units with q-state categorical (Potts) units. It provides training strategies such as contrastive divergence with temperature annealing and intra-slot diversity constraints to avoid state collapse. This work addresses the need for discrete, structured representations in generative models, which are important for tasks like associative memory and symbolic reasoning. By showing competitive performance at equal capacity, it offers a scalable alternative to binary latent variables within tractable RBMs, potentially influencing future energy-based model designs. The GM-RBM is evaluated under both capacity-matched and parameter-matched setups against GB-RBM with the same number of latent assignments. It achieves competitive or improved recall on analogical recall and structured memory benchmarks while using only Gibbs updates, and the discrete q-ary formulation allows efficient implementation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Restricted Boltzmann Machines (RBMs) are energy-based generative models with visible and hidden layers, typically using binary hidden units. The Gaussian-Bernoulli RBM (GB-RBM) handles continuous visible data but is limited to binary latent representations. The Potts model, a generalization of the Ising model, allows each spin to take multiple states, which the GM-RBM adapts for hidden units to capture multivalued concepts.

## [HoosierHelp Benchmark Reveals LLM Agents Unreliable for Social Service Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09946) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced HoosierHelp, an interactive benchmark for evaluating LLM agents in social service navigation, grounded in 3,971 Indiana public social service resources. Experiments on 240 samples across seven LLMs showed that current models are substantially unreliable in this context. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM agents for socially impactful applications, highlighting significant reliability issues that could affect vulnerable populations seeking help. It underscores the need for more robust agents capable of handling complex, non-ideal user interactions in real-world settings. HoosierHelp enhances realism by varying user need structure, constraint satisfiability, and behavior patterns such as impatience, rambling, unsupported requests, and self-contradiction. Performance drops sharply on fallback-required and self-contradictory conversations, indicating specific weaknesses in current LLM agents.

It defines three orthogonal dimensions: type of external knowledge (K), knowledge integration paradigm (I), and generative architecture (A). This framework provides a unified perspective on a rapidly growing field, helping researchers identify dominant trends and underexplored areas. It could accelerate progress in clinical applications such as data augmentation, privacy-preserving data sharing, and simulation of anatomical scenarios. The taxonomy is designed to be extensible to future work, allowing principled positioning of new methods. This paper presents a comparative analysis of seven deep learning models for breast cancer detection on two datasets, evaluating both performance and CO2 emissions during training and inference. It finds that EfficientNet and ResNet perform well but with higher emissions, while DeiT-Tiny offers the best accuracy-energy trade-off on the Breast Ultrasound dataset. This work highlights the growing importance of sustainability in AI, particularly in medical imaging where model selection can have environmental consequences. The evaluated models include CNNs (EfficientNet, ResNet, DenseNet121), transformers (DeiT-Tiny, ViT, Swin), and hybrid models, tested on Breast Ultrasound and BreakHis 400X datasets. Results show no single architecture consistently dominates, emphasizing the need to consider dataset characteristics and emissions alongside performance.

## [Jointly Learning Rewards and Worker Reliability from Pairwise Comparisons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10045) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an EM-based algorithm that jointly learns item rewards and worker reliability from pairwise comparisons under a Boltzmann-rational model, using Polya-Gamma latent variables to simplify the optimization. The method is validated on real-world and synthetic datasets, showing robustness to spammers and adversarial workers. This work addresses a critical issue in crowdsourcing and preference learning, where worker unreliability can degrade model performance. By jointly estimating worker competency and item rewards, it improves the accuracy of reward learning, which is essential for applications like recommendation systems and LLM fine-tuning. The algorithm leverages Polya-Gamma latent variables to transform the logistic likelihood into a conditionally Gaussian form, enabling a simplified Q function in the E-step and reducing the problem to matrix sensing. Theoretical convergence guarantees are established, and the code and data are publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Pairwise comparisons are widely used in recommendation systems and fine-tuning large language models, where item rewards are learned from comparisons. Crowdsourcing platforms like Amazon Mechanical Turk often provide these comparisons, but workers may be unreliable due to limited knowledge or spamming behavior. The Bradley-Terry-Luce model is a standard approach for learning from pairwise comparisons, and the Boltzmann-rational model extends it by incorporating worker competencies. Polya-Gamma latent variables are a data-augmentation technique that simplifies Bayesian inference for logistic models.

This paper introduces a multi-constraint reinforcement learning approach that decomposes the human-following task into a sparse task reward and independent cost constraints with explicit cost thresholds, replacing implicit reward weight ratios. The method also integrates prediction uncertainty of human motions into the RL costs to enhance safety under unpredictable conditions. This work addresses a practical challenge in robotics—human following in dense crowds—by providing a more interpretable and tunable trade-off between proximity and safety. It could improve the deployment of service robots, assistive devices, and autonomous navigation systems in crowded real-world environments. The method uses sparse rewards and explicit cost thresholds, which have direct behavioral meaning, allowing explicit control over the trade-off. Extensive experiments in in-distribution and out-of-distribution settings, along with real-robot deployment, validate the approach's effectiveness.

Sparse rewards provide only occasional feedback, making learning challenging, but they can be combined with constraints to guide the agent. This paper applies these concepts to the task of following a target human in crowded environments, where balancing closeness and safety is critical.

## [ELMER: Evolutionary Language Model for Steerable Program Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10196) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ELMER, an evolutionary language model that searches over natural-language policy descriptions and compiles them into typed programs (GPTL) for execution. It uses a fine-tuned Qwen3-8B model with conditional semantic mutation and Direct Preference Optimization (oDPO) to improve behavioral calibration and search efficiency. This work addresses a key limitation in program evolution: controlling behavioral change rather than relying on syntactic edit size. By demonstrating that language can serve as a steerable, execution-grounded search representation, it could improve the efficiency and controllability of evolutionary algorithms applied to program synthesis and policy search. The model is fine-tuned with conditional inputs for mutation strength (low, medium, high) using Direct Preference Optimization (oDPO). Across 252 fixed-budget evolutionary searches, oDPO improves both behavioral calibration and finite-budget search efficiency, and natural-language mutations preserve more parent fitness at matched small-to-moderate behavioral displacement.

**Background**: Program evolution typically measures whether a mutation improves fitness but rarely controls how far the mutation moves in behavior space. Syntactic edit size is an unreliable proxy for behavioral change. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a training method that aligns language models with human preferences without requiring a full reinforcement learning loop, and domain-specific languages (DSLs) like GPTL are specialized programming languages for specific application domains.

## [LoRA-Tuned Small Models Outperform Commercial LLMs in ED Triage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper benchmarks eight open-source small language models (SLMs) using zero-shot prompting, prefix tuning, LoRA, and full fine-tuning on three emergency department (ED) tasks. The study finds that LoRA fine-tuned SLMs outperform commercial baselines (Claude Haiku 4.5 and Sonnet 4.5) on triage level prediction and specialist referral recommendation. This work demonstrates that locally deployable SLMs can achieve clinically competitive performance, addressing privacy concerns associated with sending patient data to closed-source commercial LLMs. It provides a practical pathway for healthcare institutions to deploy AI decision support systems on-premises, potentially improving ED efficiency and patient outcomes. The study used 2,083 MIMIC-IV-ED cases and evaluated eight open-source SLMs across three tasks: triage level prediction, specialist referral recommendation, and diagnosis prediction. Confusion matrix analysis revealed that fine-tuned open-source SLMs could detect highest-severity patients missed by commercial baselines, though diagnosis prediction remained challenging.

## [Comprendia: AI-Augmented Code Comprehension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10290) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Comprendia is a new Eclipse plugin that integrates structural dependency visualization with LLM-powered code explanations, clone detection, and CVE risk overlays for Java program comprehension. It introduces Graph-Aware Callee Pruning (GACP), an auditable strategy for generating reproducible and traceable LLM prompts. This tool addresses reproducibility and traceability issues in LLM-based code comprehension, offering developers a unified graph substrate that keeps them in control. It could enhance software maintenance and security by integrating vulnerability and clone detection directly into the IDE. Comprendia rests on four pillars: a multi-edge-type dependency graph with live search and layouts, LLM explanations grounded in GACP, a clone-detection overlay suggesting extract-to-parent refactoring, and a CVE risk overlay powered by OSV.dev. GACP uses graph distance, inheritance collapse, and edge-type weighting to produce prompts that are reproducible across LLM families and traceable to visible graph nodes.

**Background**: Eclipse plugins are developed using the Plugin Development Environment (PDE) and extend the IDE's functionality. OSV.dev is an open-source vulnerability database that provides package version and commit hash information for security advisories.

## [MRIComp4Flow: Lossy Compression Enables 3D MRI Generative Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10291) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates using JPEG2000 and JPEG-LS lossy compression for 3D brain MRI to train a Wavelet Flow Matching model, demonstrating that a 20:1 compression ratio yields synthesis quality statistically equivalent to uncompressed training. This work addresses the storage and I/O bottlenecks that hinder large-scale 3D MRI generative modeling on commodity hardware, potentially enabling broader adoption of generative models in medical imaging. It also fills a gap by exploring compression effects on generative models, which differ from discriminative models. At a 20:1 compression ratio, the model achieved mean PSNR of 27.3 dB vs. 27.0 dB and mean SSIM of 0.95 vs. 0.96 across modalities, with paired TOST p-values confirming statistical equivalence within pre-specified margins (ΔPSNR < 1 dB, ΔSSIM < 0.02). The codebase is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Lossy compression reduces file sizes by discarding some data, often using wavelets (JPEG2000) or predictive coding (JPEG-LS). Generative models like flow matching learn the full data distribution, unlike discriminative models that only learn decision boundaries, so compression's impact on them is less understood. This study applies standard codecs to 3D MRI volumes and trains a Wavelet Flow Matching model conditioned on BraTS sequences.

This paper proposes a framework to extract reusable developer-specific skills from interaction traces and empirically evaluates them on 206 real-world sessions from 13 developers. The study finds that personalized skills yield small and inconsistent improvements over no-skill baselines, while generic skills pooled across developers achieve the largest gains. This research addresses the timely question of whether personalizing coding agents based on individual developer preferences is effective. The findings challenge the assumption that personalization always helps, suggesting that broadly transferable procedural knowledge may be more robust, which has implications for the design of AI-assisted software engineering tools. The framework uses rule-based bootstrapping and evidence-grounded refinement to generate personalized skills, and evaluates them with a reproducible replay framework using an interactive, trajectory-conditioned LLM-based human developer simulator. The study compares personalized skills against no-skill, generic-skill, and other-user-skill baselines, and finds that personalized skills become more effective when developer preferences appear frequently in histories.

## [Narrative Keyframing: A New Technique for AI-Assisted Creative Writing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10337) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce narrative keyframing, an interaction technique for AI-assisted creative writing that lets writers specify narrative constraints at key moments and generate intervening prose. The paper explores three types of keyframes: plot, character, and perspective, and demonstrates through a user study that it supports more controllable, transparent, and engaging use of generative AI. This technique addresses a key limitation of current AI writing tools—the lack of fine-grained creative control—by borrowing the concept of keyframing from animation. It could empower writers to maintain their artistic vision while leveraging AI's generative capabilities, potentially influencing future human-AI collaboration tools in creative domains. The paper defines three types of keyframes: plot keyframes define significant events, character keyframes represent character changes, and perspective keyframes capture first-person experiences. The user study suggests that narrative keyframing offers more customizable, iterative, and fine-scale control compared to previous high-level conditioning methods.

**Background**: Keyframing is a fundamental technique in animation where lead animators draw key frames that define the start and end of a transition, and in-between frames are filled in later. In AI-assisted writing, current tools often rely on high-level prompts or constraints, which can be limiting for fine-grained narrative control. Narrative keyframing adapts this animation concept to writing, allowing writers to set constraints at specific story points and let the AI generate the connecting prose.

This paper introduces egg-computation, a novel method that combines expert knowledge with large language models (LLMs) to estimate causal effects on hospital length of stay (LOS). The method integrates Gantt charts with causal DAGs and uses an LLM-assisted pipeline to scale expert reasoning, outperforming conventional methods in simulations and matching human expert concordance in a real hospital study. This paper introduces a unified framework for face landmark detection using Face Part-Anchored Landmark Positions (FPALPs), enabling training on diverse N-point datasets and dynamic landmark prediction. The method, called Unified Dynamic FLD, uses FPALP-based queries and a cross-modality decoder to achieve competitive or superior performance on multiple benchmarks. This work addresses a practical limitation in face landmark detection where models are typically trained for specific N-point datasets and cannot generalize to other landmark definitions. By unifying datasets and enabling dynamic prediction, it could streamline development and improve adaptability for downstream applications. The FPALP representation treats each landmark as a progression value along a face part's contour, allowing any landmark to be expressed in a unified format. The model uses FPALP-based queries refined by a cross-modality decoder, and can output any number of landmarks by loading designated queries at runtime.

## [SINKFLEX-RL: Modular RL System for Long-Horizon Tool-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10357) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SINKFLEX-RL, a modular reinforcement learning training system for long-horizon tool-use agents, combining a Gymnasium-compatible environment wrapper, VERL-style rollout dataflow, group-relative policy optimization without a separate value model, and a sink-aware FlexAttention path. In a preliminary Tau2Bench retail benchmark, validation reward (mean@1) improved from 0.25 to 0.44 during training, and the optimized attention path reduced peak VRAM by 19.7% at 4096 tokens. This work addresses critical challenges in applying reinforcement learning to long-horizon tool-use agents, such as long contexts and memory constraints, which are relevant for real-world applications like retail automation. By demonstrating memory-feasible training and improved rewards, it could enable more scalable and efficient RL training for complex agentic tasks. The system uses group-relative policy optimization (GRPO) without a separate value model, and a sink-aware FlexAttention path that preserves model-specific sink scaling under causal and sliding-window masks. In a fixed-configuration memory benchmark, the optimized attention path reduced peak VRAM from 28.06GB to 22.52GB at 4096 tokens (19.7% reduction) and ran an 8192-token configuration using 25.53GB where the eager baseline ran out of memory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) trains agents to make sequences of decisions by rewarding desired behaviors. For long-horizon tool-use tasks, agents must reason over user goals, domain policies, tool calls, and simulator state, which creates long contexts that are memory-intensive. Group-relative policy optimization (GRPO) is an RL algorithm that compares groups of outputs to compute advantages, avoiding the need for a separate value model. FlexAttention is a PyTorch API that allows flexible attention mechanisms, and sink-aware attention refers to techniques that preserve the model's learned sink token scaling, which is important for maintaining performance under certain masks.

It achieves subsecond key-to-audible-update latency and significantly increases off-grid quartertone content in generated music. Empirical evaluations show that dynamic prompt compilation reliably grounds generation in microtonal scales, significantly increasing off-grid quartertone content over baseline generation.

## [VolRouter Recasts Volatility Control as Routing Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10375) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VolRouter, a modular framework, reformulates volatility control as state-conditioned routing over estimator-controller pairs, achieving the highest Sharpe ratio in three of four asset settings, including improving S&P 500 Sharpe from 0.952 to 1.222. This work challenges the conventional fixed estimator-controller approach in volatility control, suggesting that adaptive policy selection can significantly improve risk-adjusted returns. It offers a new perspective for quantitative finance, potentially leading to more robust portfolio management strategies across various market conditions. VolRouter operates in three stages: state inference, switch review, and pair selection, and can use rule-based, learnable, or LLM-based decision modules. Ablation studies show improvements stem from relative policy evaluation and selective persistent switching, not merely expanding the policy library.

**Background**: Volatility control is a risk management technique that adjusts portfolio exposure based on volatility estimates, often using a fixed estimator and control rule. The Sharpe ratio measures risk-adjusted return, and maximum drawdown and CVaR are common risk metrics. VolRouter introduces a routing framework that dynamically selects among estimator-controller pairs based on market conditions, aiming to improve performance across different assets.

This paper introduces persona conditioning as a diagnostic probe to expose and analyze LLM assessor sensitivity in information retrieval (IR) evaluation. Using five assessor roles across six LLM backbones on TREC DL20 and RAG24, it reveals structured rather than uniform shifts in relevance judgments. This work addresses a critical issue in LLM-based IR evaluation: how assessor framing affects judgment reliability and downstream system comparison. The study uses personas from PersonaHub and NVIDIA Nemotron-Personas-USA, instantiating five assessor roles: intent interpretation, domain expertise, contrastive judgment, evidence verification, and global search-quality assessment, compared against a standard UMBRELA baseline. ## [ELVAE: Evidential Learning-Based VAE for Uncertainty-Aware Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ELVAE introduces a novel variational autoencoder that models each latent coordinate with an input-dependent normal-inverse-gamma posterior, enabling explicit latent-location uncertainty. This uncertainty can be used during generation to produce more reliable synthetic samples or to deliberately stress-test models. This work bridges evidential learning and generative modeling, offering a principled way to quantify and control uncertainty in VAEs. It could improve reliability in applications like synthetic data generation and model validation, where understanding uncertainty is critical. The objective is an exact evidence lower bound, and the authors show that direct regularization of the full hierarchy is necessary because the marginalized latent law alone cannot identify the uncertainty decomposition. In an MNIST pilot with a frozen external classifier, the uncertainty stratified semantic reliability, with a zero-displacement control indicating that most of the effect comes from anchor re-generation reliability, plus a smaller perturbation-induced component.

**Background**: Variational autoencoders (VAEs) generate samples from probabilistic latent representations but typically do not distinguish between uncertainty about the latent location and variability around it. Evidential deep learning is a paradigm that estimates uncertainty by learning higher-order evidential distributions, often using conjugate priors like normal-inverse-gamma, which provide closed-form uncertainty estimates without expensive sampling.

## [New DoS Attack Targets End-to-End Speech LLMs via Acoustic Perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10405) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced a perturbation-based denial-of-service (DoS) attack specifically designed for end-to-end speech language models (LLMs). The attack optimizes imperceptible acoustic perturbations to force the model to generate excessively long outputs, increasing computational overhead and resource consumption. This is significant because end-to-end speech LLMs are rapidly emerging, yet their security vulnerabilities, particularly DoS attacks, remain largely unexplored. The attack could be exploited to degrade service availability and increase operational costs for deployed speech models, highlighting a critical gap in AI safety. The attack formulates a composite optimization objective that jointly suppresses EOS generation, encourages prolonged decoding, and preserves semantic consistency using weighted EOS loss, top-k logit loss, length loss, and semantic alignment loss. To enhance stealthiness, voice activity detection (VAD) is used to inject perturbations only into voiced regions. Experiments on three open-source E2E speech LLMs demonstrated stable attack success and increased generation length and GPU consumption.

Traditional text-based DoS attacks on LLMs rely on prompt engineering, such as adversarial suffixes, which exploit the discrete nature of text. However, speech inputs are continuous, so these methods cannot be directly transferred. End-to-end speech LLMs process audio directly, and prior security research focused on ASR or TTS systems, leaving this new attack surface unaddressed.

## [Riemann GeoResolver: Non-Euclidean Attention with Theoretical Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10416) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Riemann GeoResolver, a non-Euclidean attention framework that extends inverse-distance attention from Euclidean to hyperbolic and spherical geometries, providing theoretical guarantees such as circuit separation, Polyak-Lojasiewicz inequalities, and effective rank bounds. It includes ten integrated modules, with Euclidean theorems fully proved and non-Euclidean extensions proved by analogy. This work could significantly impact the design of efficient and robust transformers by offering a theoretically grounded alternative to softmax attention, potentially reducing computational complexity and improving generalization. It bridges non-Euclidean geometry with attention mechanisms, opening new avenues for modeling hierarchical or tree-like data structures. The Euclidean part establishes three core theorems: circuit separation (IDA achieves exact retrieval with O(1) resources vs softmax's Ω((log n)^2) width), a Polyak-Lojasiewicz inequality with a stronger constant, and a width-independent effective rank bound limiting noise memorization. The non-Euclidean extension includes modules like Hyperbolic Curvature Compression (HCC), HyperGate, Spherical Inverse Distance Attention (SIDA), Dynamic Memory Genesis (DMG), and Geodesic Sparse Routing (GSR), with complexity ranging from Θ(n^2) to Θ(1) per token.

**Background**: Inverse-distance attention replaces the scaled dot-product in softmax attention with the negative log of Euclidean distance, simplifying to inverse distance weighting interpolation. The Polyak-Lojasiewicz (PL) inequality is a condition that guarantees linear convergence of gradient descent without requiring convexity. Hyperbolic geometry, with its tree-like properties, is increasingly used in neural networks to model hierarchical data, while spherical geometry is used for routing or retrieval tasks.

## [Causality Sum Rules Applied Directly to Scattering Matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10427) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a domain-delayed scattering matrix that allows causality sum rules to be applied directly to conventional scattering matrices, without transforming to auxiliary variables. The framework recovers known limits like Rozanov's absorber limit and extends bounds to measurable quantities such as insertion loss. This work bridges fundamental causality theory with experimentally accessible scattering data, potentially impacting photonic and electromagnetic device design. It provides new bounds on device performance that could guide optimization of absorbers, filters, and other components. The approach removes the reference-domain time advance by using the earliest-arrival delay of each channel, preserving real-frequency passivity. Under analyticity, transparency, and regularity assumptions, the matrix becomes a Schur function, enabling a Cayley-Herglotz construction that yields projected and determinant bounds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Scattering matrices (S-matrices) describe how waves scatter off a system, relating incoming and outgoing states. Causality sum rules are fundamental constraints derived from the principle that effects cannot precede causes, often used to bound device performance. Traditionally, these rules are applied to transformed variables, but this paper shows they can be applied directly to the conventional S-matrix by adjusting the time reference.

## [Model Merging Framework Cuts Reasoning Length in LLM Recommenders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10447) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces REAM, the first model merging framework for reasoning compression in LLM-based recommender systems, which performs fine-grained merging at the attention head level. Experiments on three benchmark datasets show it reduces reasoning length by up to 24.3% while maintaining recommendation accuracy. This work addresses the practical problem of verbose reasoning in slow-thinking LLM recommenders, which increases inference costs. By offering a training-free alternative to existing compression methods, it could lower deployment costs and improve efficiency for real-world recommender systems. The method assigns distinct merge coefficients to each attention head based on its contribution to critical reasoning evidence and sensitivity to parameter change. This selective injection of concise behavior from a fast-thinking model into a slow-thinking model reduces verbosity without compromising quality.

## [MD-ProTector: Multi-Prototype Detection for LLM Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10459) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MD-ProTector introduces a novel approach to LLM-generated text detection by representing each class with multiple trainable prototypes in the encoder embedding space, along with a Prototype Positioning loss to separate class-level structure from within-class variation. It achieves state-of-the-art results on several benchmarks, including the highest AvgRec on MAGE CDCM and RAID, and the highest AUROC with the lowest FPR95 on RAID among encoder-based methods. As LLM-generated content becomes more sophisticated, robust detection is crucial for mitigating misuse. MD-ProTector addresses the challenge of diverse writing styles within each class, which is a key limitation of standard binary classifiers, potentially improving the reliability of AI-generated text detection in real-world applications. The method uses multiple prototypes per class to create separate decision boundaries for different text groups, and the Prototype Positioning loss ensures each prototype captures a distinct variation. It was evaluated across five settings from three large-scale benchmarks, covering domain, generator, language, and adversarial variation.

Prototype learning, which represents classes with reference vectors, has been used in interpretable text classification, and this work extends it to improve detection performance.

## [Critic-Free Pretraining Boosts Offline-to-Online RL Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10473) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Critic-Free Pretraining (CFP), a new paradigm for offline-to-online reinforcement learning that completely omits offline critic training, allowing a freshly initialized critic to adapt during online fine-tuning. CFP is compatible with mainstream O2O algorithms and consistently matches or improves upon conventional methods across diverse tasks. This approach addresses a known problem in offline-to-online RL where inherited biased critic estimates hinder fine-tuning, potentially improving sample efficiency and performance in real-world applications. It offers a simple yet effective alternative that can be integrated into existing algorithms, benefiting the broader RL community. CFP involves a warm-up stage on the offline dataset at the start of online fine-tuning to adapt the fresh critic. The paper demonstrates consistent gains, with particularly pronounced improvements on several challenging tasks, while remaining compatible with various mainstream O2O algorithms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Offline-to-online (O2O) reinforcement learning leverages policies pretrained on static datasets and improves them through online interaction. Traditional methods reuse an offline-trained critic, but as the policy and data distribution shift, value estimates can become misaligned, hindering fine-tuning. Actor-critic methods combine policy and value learning, and the critic estimates future returns to guide policy updates.

## [EDPFRL-IM: Exploration-Driven Personalized Federated Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10499) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EDPFRL-IM, a novel framework that integrates intrinsic motivation via random network distillation (RND) into personalized federated reinforcement learning (PFRL) to enhance exploration in sparse-reward and non-stationary environments while preserving client privacy. The server sends global exploration priors and collects minimal novelty summaries, avoiding access to raw experiences or local gradients. This work addresses a critical gap in PFRL by explicitly tackling exploration, which is often neglected in existing methods that rely solely on extrinsic rewards. It could improve the applicability of federated reinforcement learning to real-world tasks with delayed or sparse rewards, such as robotics and personalized recommendation systems, while maintaining data privacy. The framework uses RND to generate intrinsic rewards, where a predictor network tries to match a fixed random target network, giving higher bonuses to novel states. The server aggregates only minimal novelty summaries from clients, ensuring privacy, and experiments show improvements in policy personalization and sample efficiency over average PFRL benchmarks in delayed and sparse reward settings.

Random Network Distillation (RND) is a well-known intrinsic motivation technique that encourages exploration by rewarding novel states, and this paper adapts it to the federated setting.

This paper introduces AID, a semantic-aware multimodal pre-training framework for medical tabular data, which explicitly models the two-dimensional structure of tables via Importance-Aware Adaptive Masking and a Soft-Label Discretized Module. It achieves state-of-the-art results on dermatology and ophthalmology datasets. This work addresses critical limitations in existing multimodal pre-training methods for tabular data, which often treat tables as flat vectors and use unstable regression objectives. The framework introduces two novel mechanisms: Importance-Aware Adaptive Masking constructs a label-free curriculum that prioritizes salient features, and a Soft-Label Discretized Module replaces numerical regression with distribution matching to preserve ordinal relationships. Experiments were conducted on large-scale datasets including SLICE-3D, HOP, and EyePACS.

This paper introduces a variant of TensorSketch that uses complex random variables to reduce the variance of polynomial kernel sketches from 3^p/D to 2^p/D, while maintaining the input-sparsity running time of the original CountSketch-based approach. The paper introduces Dynamic Context Adapters (DCA), a novel method that compresses arbitrary sequences of past frame embeddings into a fixed set of learnable context vectors, enabling pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) to incorporate historical context without quadratic attention costs. DCA achieves over 25% reduction in attention FLOPs and 13% memory savings while improving performance on long-horizon tasks. This is significant because sequential decision-making tasks, such as navigation, require temporal understanding that current VLMs lack. DCA offers an efficient solution that bridges static VLMs and recurrent policies, potentially enabling broader deployment of VLMs in real-time applications where memory and compute are constrained. DCA compresses past visual observations into a fixed-size set of context vectors, avoiding the memory bottleneck of naive token concatenation. The method is designed for pretrained VLMs and maintains computational efficiency while capturing rich temporal semantics.

Existing approaches either inflate computational costs or lose information through temporal compression. DCA addresses this by using a fixed-size, dynamically compressed memory to preserve historical semantics.

## [Robust Multi-Agent Bandits with Heavy-Tailed Rewards and Information Asymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10529) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces robust decentralized algorithms for multi-agent multi-armed bandits that handle heavy-tailed reward distributions under three information-asymmetry regimes: unobserved actions with common rewards, observed actions with independent rewards, and unobserved actions with independent rewards. The algorithms achieve regret guarantees that nearly match centralized heavy-tailed rates, and experiments on Pareto-distributed rewards validate the theoretical findings. This work bridges a critical gap in multi-agent bandit research by addressing heavy-tailed rewards and information asymmetry, which are common in real-world applications like distributed recommendation systems and network routing. The near-optimal regret guarantees and practical algorithms could enable more robust and efficient decentralized decision-making in uncertain environments. The paper considers three information-asymmetry regimes and develops tailored algorithms for each, highlighting trade-offs between synchronization, coordination, and exploration. The theoretical regret bounds nearly match centralized heavy-tailed rates, and experiments use Pareto-distributed rewards to validate performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-armed bandits (MAB) is a classic framework for sequential decision-making under uncertainty, where a learner chooses among multiple arms to maximize cumulative reward. Traditional MAB often assumes sub-Gaussian rewards, but real-world data can be heavy-tailed, meaning extreme values occur more frequently than in normal distributions, leading to infinite variance. Multi-agent MAB extends this to multiple agents who may collaborate or compete, and information asymmetry arises when agents have different observations or actions. Heavy-tailed rewards challenge standard concentration inequalities, requiring robust estimation techniques like median-of-means.

## [PCFlow: Flow Matching for Efficient Perceptually Consistent Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10544) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PCFlow introduces a unified flow matching framework for image restoration that directly parameterizes a continuous transport from degraded observations to clean targets, incorporating a Latent Consistency Perceptual Loss (LCPL) and a conflict-free gradient projection strategy to balance distortion and perception. It achieves competitive performance across diverse restoration tasks with few-step inference and a lightweight convolution-only backbone. This work addresses the long-standing distortion-perception tradeoff in image restoration while significantly reducing computational costs, making high-quality restoration more accessible for real-world applications. It also advances the use of flow matching as an efficient alternative to diffusion models in low-level vision tasks. The framework uses a latent consistency flow objective for stable few-step inference and imposes semantic constraints directly on the guiding velocity field via LCPL. A conflict-free gradient projection strategy stabilizes the multi-objective optimization, and the backbone is lightweight and convolution-only, avoiding heavy transformer architectures.

**Background**: Image restoration aims to recover a clean image from a degraded observation, but algorithms face a fundamental tradeoff between distortion metrics (like PSNR) and perceptual quality. Recent generative approaches, such as diffusion models, can improve perceptual quality but are computationally expensive. Flow matching is an emerging generative paradigm that offers faster sampling than diffusion while maintaining high-quality generation, making it attractive for restoration tasks.

## [ImpactHO: Importance-Aware KV Cache Transfer for Edge LLM Handover](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10545) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ImpactHO, a framework that orders each user's KV cache by importance and transmits only the most informative fraction during multi-user edge LLM handovers, formulated as a convex backhaul allocation problem solved via closed-form weighted water-filling. It achieves over 93.7% average accuracy within a 500ms transfer window, within 0.5 percentage points of the full-cache ceiling. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in edge AI: limited backhaul bandwidth during simultaneous handovers. By enabling partial KV cache transfer with minimal accuracy loss, it could improve the scalability and responsiveness of edge LLM services, benefiting applications like mobile assistants and autonomous systems. The method repurposes per-entry importance scores from KV cache eviction to set transmission order, and models partial-cache accuracy as a sigmoid fitted to RULER benchmark data with R^2>0.99. The proposed allocator reaches 98.2-99.5% of a clairvoyant upper bound, and the convex formulation relies on keeping served users within the concave region of the accuracy curve.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Edge LLMs need to maintain inference continuity when users move between edge nodes, requiring transfer of the key-value (KV) cache. However, simultaneous handovers can saturate the backhaul, limiting full cache delivery. KV cache stores intermediate attention keys and values, and importance-aware eviction is a common technique to reduce memory usage. The RULER benchmark evaluates long-context capabilities of LLMs, providing a basis for measuring partial-cache accuracy.

## [RCCP: Retrieval-Corrected Conformal Prediction for Time Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10553) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Retrieval-Corrected Conformal Prediction (RCCP), a novel calibration method that retrieves similar past residuals to build asymmetric prediction intervals and applies a scalar conformal correction to adjust coverage. RCCP achieves target coverage across benchmarks with the lowest Winkler scores and low overhead. This work addresses a key limitation of standard conformal prediction for time series, where errors are temporally dependent and non-exchangeable. By combining retrieval-based local evidence with conformal correction, RCCP offers a scalable and effective approach to uncertainty quantification, benefiting researchers and practitioners in time series forecasting. RCCP builds an asymmetric interval from retrieved one-sided residuals and calibrates its normalized retrieval error with a scalar conformal correction. The paper provides a coverage-gap bound based on the stability of the normalized retrieval error distribution, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Conformal prediction is a distribution-free method that produces statistically valid prediction intervals for any underlying predictor, assuming exchangeability of the data. However, time series data often violate this assumption due to temporal dependencies and changing conditions, making standard calibration inefficient. Recent methods improve local calibration using recent or weighted residuals, but RCCP takes a retrieval-based approach to select the most relevant residuals and then corrects any remaining coverage error.
