## [Physics-Informed Synthetic Underwater Dataset π-SUB Bridges Synthetic-to-Real Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10589) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces π-SUB, a physics-informed framework for generating synthetic underwater benchmark datasets that extends the classical underwater image formation model with depth-dependent downwelling irradiance, biologically resolved absorption, and environmental scattering across all ten Jerlov water types. The dataset achieves a 46% lower global Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) than Syrea, and improves UIQM by 4.18% over PHISWID and 9.46% over Syrea across four UIE architectures and six real benchmarks. This work addresses the synthetic-to-real gap in underwater image enhancement (UIE), a critical challenge for training robust models. By providing a more realistic and generalizable benchmark, π-SUB can accelerate the development of next-generation UIE methods, benefiting applications like autonomous underwater vehicles, marine biology, and underwater robotics. The π-SUB dataset includes paired synthetic underwater-reference images spanning shallow-to-deep and coastal-to-oceanic environments. The framework incorporates independently controllable residual phenomena, and the code and dataset are publicly available at https://github.com/airl-iisc/pi-SUB.

The classical underwater image formation model, extended in this work, describes how light attenuates and scatters in water. Jerlov water types classify ocean waters based on optical clarity, and the Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) is a metric for assessing the realism of generated images.

## [DegradeQuery: Counterfactual Pretraining Boosts PROTAC Degradation Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10595) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DegradeQuery, a new framework, uses counterfactual tuple pretraining to predict PROTAC-induced protein degradation, achieving an AUC of 0.9065 and accuracy of 0.8500 on the PROTAC-8K benchmark, outperforming existing methods. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in PROTAC drug discovery by leveraging unlabeled data, potentially accelerating the development of targeted protein degradation therapies and inspiring similar approaches in other drug discovery tasks. The counterfactual pretraining objective contrasts recorded molecule-target-E3 tuples with alternatives formed by replacing the target, E3 ligase, or both, without assigning activity pseudo-labels. Controlled analyses show the improvement is primarily due to tuple-level pretraining, can be recovered using only label-missing records, and remains complementary to protein language model representations.

## [Inferential Capability Does Not Determine Legal Scope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10601) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper argues that inferential capability does not determine legal scope under EU digital law, distinguishing the constitutive role in the AI Act from the protective role in the GDPR. It proposes a two-level framework with three pathways and a compositional-effects test for agentic systems. This clarification is significant for the legal and technical communities as agentic architectures become more prevalent, making the non-coincidence of AI Act and GDPR perimeters operationally acute. It provides a framework that could influence how inference-based systems are regulated and designed. The paper introduces concepts such as the inferential threshold, inferential reach, and inferential chain, mapped onto the chain of imputation. It also proposes a compositional-effects test to identify the decision unit under Article 22 GDPR, along with documentation duties calibrated to inference chains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: The EU AI Act defines an AI system by its capability to infer, while the GDPR governs inference protectively without defining it. Agentic systems, which use multi-step planning and tool access, make the interaction between these two legal frameworks more complex. The paper addresses this by proposing a two-level framework that separates constitutive and protective functions of inference.

## [Temporal-Context Framework Boosts Cross-View Localization Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10660) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a temporal-context-enhanced framework for cross-view sequence visual localization in autonomous driving, using a recurrent cross-frame module to aggregate historical context. On the CVIS dataset, it reduces mean localization error from 3.80 m to 1.57 m and increases R@1 m from 8.14% to 40.22%. This work addresses a critical limitation in existing cross-view localization methods that process frames independently, improving robustness under dynamic occlusion, illumination variation, and repetitive textures. The significant accuracy gains and successful real-world deployment suggest practical impact for autonomous driving systems that rely on GNSS and HD maps. The framework enhances coarse ground features with historical context, then performs satellite candidate-region classification and hierarchical fine-grained local offset estimation. Direct transfer to KITTI-CVL achieves a mean error of 2.61 m, fine-tuning reduces it to 2.27 m, and zero-shot field experiments on a real vehicle achieve a mean error of 2.84 m with R@5 m of 96.86%.

## [ENTLORE: Benchmarking Latent Organizational Reasoning in Enterprise QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10679) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ENTLORE introduces a graph-grounded benchmark framework for evaluating latent organizational reasoning in enterprise question answering, reconstructing audited enterprise worlds from routine documents and organizational tables. It includes 2,341 documents, 907 questions, and proof certificates for golden answers, tested across 56 model and access configurations. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing enterprise QA benchmarks by focusing on latent organizational reasoning, where target relations are implicit and not directly stated in the corpus. It highlights that enterprise QA depends not only on document recall but also on making implicit organizational relations usable, which could guide future model development and evaluation. The benchmark includes three source types and questions spanning explicit lookup, cross-source composition, and latent organizational reasoning. Even with gold documents, 30.4% of latent questions remain unanswered, compared to 12.6% and 6.2% for explicit and compositional questions, respectively.

## [ProTAGAD: Decoupled Prototypes for TAG Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ProTAGAD introduces a foundation model for anomaly detection in text-attributed graphs (TAGs) that decouples topological and textual prototypes. It constructs dual prototype banks to independently model structural normality and semantic consistency, mitigating the Blurred-Anomaly-Boundary (BAB) issue. This work addresses a critical limitation in graph anomaly detection for TAGs, where conventional GNN-based methods suffer from cross-modality coupling that obscures anomaly signals. By achieving state-of-the-art performance across 14 datasets, ProTAGAD could enable more robust and transferable anomaly detection in applications like social network moderation and cyber threat identification. The method uses decoupled topological and textual prototypes, which are independently learned to capture structural and semantic normality. Extensive experiments on 14 benchmark datasets show consistent state-of-the-art performance in cross-domain settings, and ablation studies confirm that the decoupled design effectively mitigates the BAB issue.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Text-Attributed Graphs (TAGs) combine textual content with graph structure, enabling richer representation learning for tasks like classification and anomaly detection. Traditional graph anomaly detection (GAD) relies on structural irregularities, but TAG anomaly detection must jointly leverage both topology and text semantics. However, current GNN-based detectors fuse these modalities during message passing, causing deep coupling that amplifies noise and blurs the boundary between normal and anomalous nodes—a problem known as the Blurred-Anomaly-Boundary (BAB) issue. Foundation models for GAD aim to generalize across domains, but the BAB issue hampers their effectiveness.

This paper formalizes the self-refinement process of foundation models as an optimal stopping problem, deriving stopping policies that are efficiently computed via stochastic approximation. Experiments on a coding benchmark show these policies are significantly more cost-efficient than prior stopping policies. This work provides a principled method to balance improvement and cost in self-refinement, which is crucial for practical deployment of foundation models. It could lead to significant cost savings and more efficient use of computational resources in iterative refinement loops. The optimal stopping policies are derived using stochastic approximation, which allows efficient computation. The empirical evaluation is conducted on a coding benchmark, demonstrating cost savings compared to prior work.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation models can improve outputs through self-refinement, an iterative process where the model generates outputs, receives feedback from verifiers, and refines responses via in-context learning. Optimal stopping is a decision-making framework where one observes a stochastic process and decides when to stop to balance costs and benefits. Stochastic approximation algorithms use random samples to efficiently approximate properties like zeros or extrema, useful in settings with big data.

## [GenAI Catch-22 in Norwegian Newsrooms During 2025 Election](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10773) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A case study of Norwegian newsrooms during the 2025 parliamentary election reveals that ambitious plans for audience-facing GenAI collapsed, leading to mundane internal uses. The study identifies a 'GenAI Catch-22' where reliance on GenAI erodes the human expertise needed to monitor it. This study challenges the dominant focus on external disinformation, highlighting internal threats from journalists' own GenAI use. It provides timely empirical evidence for media organizations and policymakers grappling with AI integration during elections. The research is based on interviews with managers and journalists over a ten-month period. It shows that newsroom managers held unrealistically optimistic sociotechnical imaginaries about GenAI capabilities, leading to the collapse of audience-facing plans and a shift to internal mundane uses.

## [BPG: Dynamic Adapter Sizing and Soft Domain Mixture for Domain Incremental Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10804) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BPG, a unified framework for domain incremental learning that dynamically adjusts adapter hidden dimensions based on domain-specific feature separability and employs a soft domain mixture strategy at inference time. Experiments on DomainNet, CDDB, and CORe50 show state-of-the-art average accuracy with forgetting reduced to as low as 0.22% on DomainNet. This work addresses a key limitation in parameter-isolation-based domain incremental learning: the one-size-fits-all adapter capacity that leads to either underfitting or parameter redundancy. By dynamically sizing adapters and using soft domain mixture, BPG improves both plasticity and generalization, which could benefit continual learning systems deployed in evolving environments. BPG consists of two components: BPG-Adapter, which determines each domain's adapter hidden dimension based on feature separability, and BPG-Inference, which integrates multiple domain-specific models at test time to mitigate domain ID misselection. The method consistently outperforms uniform adapter-based approaches and hard domain selection strategies across three benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Domain incremental learning (DIL) is a continual learning paradigm where a model sequentially adapts to shifting input distributions while maintaining a fixed label space, with the key challenge of alleviating catastrophic forgetting. Parameter-isolation methods, such as PackNet, achieve state-of-the-art performance by allocating task-specific parameters, but often use fixed-capacity adapters. Adapters are parameter-efficient modules inserted into transformer layers, and their bottleneck dimension controls capacity and parameter count.

This paper proposes using implicit neural representations (INRs) to model geographic atrophy (GA) progression from longitudinal fundus autofluorescence (FAF) images, generating both past and future FAF images and segmentations. The method achieves the lowest Mean Absolute Error for GA lesion area and the highest DICE score among comparison models. This work addresses a significant clinical problem—understanding and predicting the progression of late-stage age-related macular degeneration (AMD), a leading cause of blindness. By enabling accurate prediction of GA growth, it could improve patient monitoring and treatment planning, and demonstrates the potential of INRs in low-data medical imaging settings. The approach generates both FAF images and GA segmentations at past and future time points, achieving competitive segmentation quality without sacrificing FAF image quality. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/SimoneSarrocco/ga-progression-with-inrs.

**Background**: Implicit neural representations (INRs), also known as neural fields, are neural networks that map continuous coordinates (e.g., spatial or temporal) to continuous outputs, enabling compact and differentiable representations of signals like images. Fundus autofluorescence (FAF) imaging is a non-invasive technique that maps lipofuscin in the retinal pigment epithelium, commonly used to monitor retinal diseases such as AMD. Geographic atrophy (GA) is the late dry phase of AMD, characterized by irreversible atrophic areas, and its progression is highly individual, making prediction challenging.

## [CUE-Bench: A New Benchmark for Affective Stance in Chinese Discourse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10810) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced CUE-Bench, a Chinese benchmark for affective stance that captures implicit, ironic, and pragmatically reshaped emotions. It provides nine human-interpretable affective stances and annotations for intent and fine-grained emotion, improving fine-grained emotion recognition by 3.5 percentage points and pragmatic intent detection by 7.8 percentage points over strong baselines. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in emotion understanding by moving beyond surface sentiment to model deeper affective meaning, which is often concealed or reshaped in real communication. It provides a more sensitive evaluation tool for NLP models, potentially driving progress in affective computing and dialogue systems, especially for Chinese language processing. CUE-Bench constructs affective stances from the interaction of explicit and implicit polarity, and includes annotations for pragmatic intent and fine-grained emotions. The benchmark covers diverse Chinese dialogue scenarios, and experiments show that incorporating affective stance improves fine-grained emotion recognition and pragmatic intent detection over strong baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Emotion understanding in discourse often requires reasoning beyond surface sentiment, as speakers may convey affect indirectly through politeness, irony, or deliberate mismatch. Existing benchmarks typically annotate only surface polarity or final emotion categories, lacking a structured account of how explicit expression, implicit affect, pragmatic intent, and fine-grained emotion interact. CUE-Bench aims to fill this gap by focusing on affective stance, which captures the speaker's underlying emotional position even when not explicitly stated.

<a id="item-266"></a>
## [MIRA: Reflective Medical Image Diagnosis Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10827) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MIRA is a new medical visual diagnostic framework that dynamically uses tools like zooming, grounding, and web search, and reflects on the evidence to improve diagnostic accuracy. It achieves an average score of 64.73 across nine benchmarks, improving its Qwen3-VL-8B backbone by 7.44 points. This framework addresses a key limitation in medical AI agents: indiscriminate tool use can introduce noisy evidence. By verifying tool necessity and evidence consistency, MIRA improves reliability, potentially enhancing AI-assisted diagnosis in clinical settings. MIRA uses a two-stage training strategy: first, a tool-augmented Monte Carlo Tree Search data engine constructs supervised fine-tuning trajectories; second, reinforcement learning with online reflective principle evolution improves decision-making. It increases useful tool-use judgments from 56.2% to 73.8% and reduces harmful judgments from 8.9% to 1.6%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Medical visual agents are AI systems that can inspect medical images and retrieve external knowledge to assist diagnosis. However, indiscriminate tool use can lead to misleading evidence. Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm used for decision-making, and visual grounding associates image regions with textual descriptions. MIRA integrates these techniques to enable reflective verification.

## [TACTICL: Task-Aware Compression for Tabular ICL Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10837) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TACTICL is a novel framework that prunes transformer layers and replaces them with lightweight adapters, achieving up to 85% layer reduction on 47 benchmark datasets while preserving performance and robustness. This addresses the high inference costs of tabular foundation models, enabling more efficient deployment in resource-constrained environments. It also preserves in-context learning capabilities, which is crucial for adaptability to new tasks. The framework blends in-context and in-weight learning by training adapters on downstream tasks. Experiments show robustness to data shifts, indicating that the in-context ability remains intact after compression.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular foundation models like TabPFN and TabICL use in-context learning to make predictions in a single forward pass without weight updates, but they are computationally expensive. Model compression techniques such as layer pruning and adapter insertion are commonly used to reduce inference costs, but they often sacrifice adaptability. TACTICL specifically targets the depth-wise redundancy in these models to achieve efficient compression while retaining task-specific performance.

## [Cost-Efficient Routing Pipeline Boosts Low-Resource Language Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10939) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a routing pipeline for multilingual short-text classification that selectively translates low-resource languages into English before zero-shot classification, improving performance without fine-tuning. On SIB-200, low-tier Macro-F1 rose from 0.4632 to 0.6828, and on MASSIVE, from 0.2143 to 0.4417. This approach addresses the performance gap between high- and low-resource languages in multilingual NLP, offering a cost-efficient and accessible solution for production systems like content moderation and customer support. It demonstrates that selective translation can be a reliable intervention without the need for task-specific fine-tuning. The pipeline uses pretrained compact sentence encoders and is fully self-hosted. The optimal routing boundary varies by task: on SIB-200, translating only the low-resource tier (R1) was best, while on MASSIVE, full translation (R3) achieved the highest overall Macro-F1 of 0.4647.

Zero-shot classification allows models to classify text into unseen categories without fine-tuning, relying on pre-trained language models. Benchmarks like SIB-200 (topic classification in 200+ languages) and MASSIVE (intent classification across 60 intents) are used to evaluate such systems.

## [AD2-Bench: Benchmark and EGVOR Framework for Trustworthy Multimodal Reasoning in Complex Urban Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10954) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces AD2-Bench, a benchmark with a Hierarchical Visual Diagnosis framework that decomposes reasoning into a Chain of Evidence (CoE) to diagnose failures in multimodal LLMs under adverse urban conditions. It also proposes EGVOR, a method that uses explicit Evidence Atoms and hierarchical curriculum training to improve reasoning stability. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating multimodal LLMs by focusing on reasoning processes rather than just outcomes, which is essential for trustworthy AI in real-world applications like autonomous driving and urban surveillance. The proposed EGVOR framework offers a new direction for improving evidence-grounded reasoning, potentially enhancing model reliability in complex environments. The benchmark identifies two primary causes of reasoning failure: Spatial Ambiguity (localization errors due to background clutter) and Semantic Uncertainty (understanding errors from degraded visual features). EGVOR replaces implicit reasoning with explicit generation of Evidence Atoms—structured spatial-semantic triplets—and is trained via a hierarchical curriculum from reflective supervision to reinforcement learning that rewards reduced reasoning variance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) combine vision and language to perform tasks like visual question answering and image captioning. In benign conditions, they perform well, but in complex urban scenes with adverse conditions (e.g., poor lighting, occlusion), their reasoning often relies on implicit inference without sufficient visual evidence, leading to unreliable outputs. Existing benchmarks typically evaluate only final predictions, failing to diagnose reasoning process failures. The Chain of Evidence (CoE) concept, borrowed from legal and forensic domains, provides a structured way to trace reasoning steps, which this paper adapts for MLLM evaluation.

## [ReLTEx: Reliable LLM-based Taxonomy Expansion Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10970) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReLTEx, a novel framework for reliable LLM-based taxonomy expansion, was introduced. It combines LLM-driven candidate generation with structure-aware validation and recursive expansion control to reduce hallucinations and improve consistency and quality. This framework addresses the critical issue of hallucinations in LLM-generated taxonomies, which is essential for reliable knowledge graph construction and automated taxonomy enrichment. It offers a practical solution that could enhance the trustworthiness of LLM applications in structured knowledge domains. The framework was evaluated using benchmark taxonomies under a masked taxonomy expansion setting, comparing multiple validation strategies. Experimental results, supported by adapted evaluation metrics and human evaluation, demonstrate that ReLTEx produces more reliable and semantically coherent taxonomy expansions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Taxonomy expansion is the task of automatically enriching an existing taxonomy with emerging concepts, which is important for knowledge graphs and ontology maintenance. Large Language Models (LLMs) can generate relevant concepts and relations, but they are prone to hallucination, producing plausible yet nonfactual content. Structure-aware validation and recursive expansion control are techniques to mitigate such issues by ensuring hierarchical consistency and controlling the expansion process.

## [TimeRoute: Time-Aware Modality Routing and Diffusion for Multi-Modal Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10983) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TimeRoute introduces a temporal-aware modal router that assigns personalized modality distributions to users over time, and a diffusion-based graph reconstructor conditioned on temporal profiles via FiLM with dual-stream denoising heads. Experiments on TikTok, Amazon-Baby, and Amazon-Sports show consistent improvements of up to 9.8% in Recall@K, Precision@K, and NDCG@K over strong baselines. This work addresses the underexplored issue of temporal modality drift in multi-modal recommendation, where the importance of different modalities (e.g., text, image, audio) changes over time. By replacing globally shared fusion weights with personalized, time-varying modality distributions, TimeRoute could improve recommendation accuracy in dynamic domains like e-commerce and social media, benefiting both users and platforms. The method uses a temporal-aware modal router to map aggregated user behavioral features to a personalized modality distribution, and a diffusion-based graph reconstructor with Feature-wise Linear Modulation (FiLM) and dual-stream long- and short-term denoising heads to suppress outdated modality edges. The code is available at an anonymous repository, and experiments use 10-seed paired tests for robustness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-modal recommender systems integrate collaborative signals with item modalities such as text, images, and audio to improve recommendation quality. However, the relevance of each modality can drift over time and at different rates, leading to suboptimal fusion. Diffusion models, a class of generative models, have recently been applied to recommender systems for tasks like reconstructing user interaction vectors, offering robust generative capabilities and training stability.

## [Task-Adaptive Pruning with Registers for Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10989) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Task-Adaptive Pruning (TAP), a method that uses task-specific register tokens to adapt token pruning policies for different vision tasks. The jointly adapted model, TAP-J, achieves 47.0 mIoU at 1.30x encoder throughput on ADE20K and 53.7 box AP at 1.32x throughput on COCO while remaining competitive on ImageNet-1K. This work addresses the challenge of deploying pretrained Vision Transformers across multiple tasks with different spatial demands, offering a practical solution for efficient inference. It provides insights into which parts of pruning policies transfer across tasks, which is valuable for optimizing model deployment in real-world applications. The method introduces one task register per task and activates only the current one, using its evolving state to rank tokens, distribute an exact removal budget over depth, and set recovery scale for dense features. Controlled probes without retraining reveal that segmentation and detection rank pruning criteria differently, and classification is especially sensitive to attention-based pruning in the earliest layers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) have computational costs that scale quadratically with the number of tokens, making token pruning an important technique for efficient inference. Register tokens were previously introduced to remove artifacts in ViT feature maps, and this work repurposes them for task-adaptive pruning. The paper builds on prior work in token pruning and task-adaptive pruning, such as TAP-ViTs, which also addresses task-adaptive deployment of ViTs on edge devices.

## [R4DSG: Relative 4D Scene Graph Memory for Egocentric Video QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11017) ⭐️ 7.0/10

R4DSG introduces a relative 4D scene graph memory that converts long egocentric video into compact, queryable memory entries indexed by time, place, persistent objects, anchor-relative change, and local interaction context. It achieves a 6.7-point overall gain over EgoRAG-Text on a 255-question object-related subset from EgoLifeQA, and a 12.5-point gain on 'when' questions. This approach addresses a critical gap in long-horizon egocentric video QA, where caption- and transcript-based memories fail to preserve persistent object identity and structured spatial change. It offers a practical memory substrate for wearable AI assistants, AR systems, and embodied multimedia agents, potentially improving their ability to answer object-centric questions in real-world scenarios. R4DSG builds on recent RGB-only advances in promptable video segmentation, temporal propagation, and relative 3D lifting, avoiding the need for point clouds, RGB-D input, or posed views. It separates stable anchors from dynamic objects, maintains persistent object identity across frames, and represents object state through anchor-relative transitions rather than a globally aligned world model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon egocentric video is a rich substrate for wearable AI assistants, but object-centric questions remain difficult because caption- and transcript-based memories rarely preserve persistent object identity or structured spatial change. Existing long-video QA methods mainly emphasize temporal grounding and clip retrieval, while prior 3D scene-graph methods typically assume stronger geometry than free-motion wearable RGB video provides. 4D scene graphs extend traditional 2D/3D scene graphs by incorporating temporal evolution, enabling structured representation of dynamic environments.

## [Workflow Cards: Structured Summaries of Workflow Executions Using Provenance Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11022) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Workflow Cards, a new type of structured documentation that condenses provenance data from ML workflow executions into a human- and LLM-readable format. It defines a template and shows that LLMs using Workflow Cards nearly double answer quality compared to schema-based querying of provenance databases. This addresses a critical gap in ML documentation, as Model Cards and Data Cards overlook the execution-level details where bias, performance variation, and reproducibility issues originate. By making provenance data accessible, Workflow Cards can improve transparency, reproducibility, and analysis in ML workflows, benefiting practitioners and researchers. The Workflow Card template is informed by a representative set of provenance questions that surface from execution-level data missing from existing card types. The evaluation uses LLM-as-a-Judge and human assessments, showing consistent improvements in answer quality with Workflow Cards.

Model Cards and Data Cards are existing documentation standards for ML artifacts, but they focus on static artifacts like datasets and trained models, not the dynamic executions that produce them. Workflow Cards aim to fill this gap by summarizing provenance data in a structured, readable format.

## [3D-SPFES: Monocular Depth-Aware Geometric GNN for Sheep Pain Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11050) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces 3D-SPFES, a novel monocular depth-aware geometric graph neural network that integrates SPFES facial landmarks into 3D Euclidean space using VideoDepthAnything, eliminating the need for specialized depth hardware. It employs a Weighted Geometric Graph Neural Network (WG-GNN) with K=3 geometry-aware message-passing layers and scaled dot-product attention to produce a Normalized Pain Score (NPS) in [0, 100%]. This work advances animal welfare by enabling automated, objective pain assessment in sheep using only a single RGB camera, which is practical and cost-effective for real-world farming. It also demonstrates the value of 3D geometric graph neural networks in a niche but important application, potentially inspiring similar approaches in other animal or human pain assessment domains. The model uses VideoDepthAnything for monocular depth estimation, and each landmark node includes 3D spatial coordinates, estimated surface normal, and facial attribute class embedding. Edge weights are based on an aggregate metric combining Euclidean distance and surface co-planarity, and the node embeddings are clustered into O=3 pain-level clusters to derive the NPS.

Geometric graph neural networks (GNNs) are designed to handle data with geometric structure, such as 3D coordinates, and can capture spatial relationships effectively. VideoDepthAnything is a monocular depth estimation model that provides consistent depth from videos, enabling 3D reconstruction from a single RGB camera.

## [TORF: Two-Stage Odd Residual Flows for Mean-Preserving Probabilistic Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Two-stage Odd Residual Flows (TORF), a novel framework that decouples mean forecasting from uncertainty estimation in probabilistic time series forecasting. It uses a pre-trained deterministic model for accurate mean prediction and a Restricted Normalizing Flow with strictly odd functions to model residual distributions, ensuring mean preservation without sampling. TORF addresses a fundamental trade-off between distributional flexibility and accurate mean prediction in probabilistic forecasting, which is crucial for risk-sensitive decision-making. By achieving state-of-the-art deterministic accuracy (NMAE) while maintaining strong density estimation (CRPS), it offers a practical improvement for both short and long-horizon forecasting tasks. The framework consists of two stages: first, a pre-trained deterministic model generates the mean forecast; second, a Restricted Normalizing Flow with strictly odd functions learns flexible residual distributions, guaranteeing mean preservation without Monte Carlo sampling. Experiments demonstrate that TORF achieves state-of-the-art NMAE and competitive CRPS on both short and long-horizon benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Probabilistic time series forecasting aims to predict the full distribution of future values, which is essential for risk-sensitive applications. Traditional methods like Mean Variance Estimation (MVE) often suffer from degraded point accuracy when trained under joint Negative Log-Likelihood (NLL) objectives, while flexible generative models such as Normalizing Flows and Diffusion Models rely on costly Monte Carlo sampling and may yield suboptimal mean estimates. TORF addresses this by decoupling mean prediction and uncertainty estimation, using a two-stage approach that preserves the mean while maintaining distributional flexibility.

## [TrustNLP Workshop Review Reveals Shift from Interpretability to Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11171) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A comprehensive review of 144 TrustNLP workshop papers (2021-2026) documents a field-wide transition from post-hoc interpretability of static models to mechanistic understanding and proactive control of generative systems. The analysis reveals that truthfulness has become the fastest-growing trust dimension, rising from absent in 2021-2022 to 37% of papers by 2025-2026. This meta-analysis provides valuable insights into the evolution of trustworthy NLP, highlighting trends that can guide future research directions. The observed shift from interpretability to control reflects the broader AI alignment challenge, where ensuring safety and truthfulness of generative models is becoming paramount. The review classifies papers along six trust dimensions grounded in established frameworks like TrustLLM and DecodingTrust. It also includes a cross-venue comparison with ACL, NAACL, EACL, and EMNLP (~2K papers), showing that TrustNLP's topical distribution closely follows the field average. Fairness remains the most consistent theme, while explainability exhibits a U-shaped trajectory, resurging in 2026 through mechanistic interpretability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: The TrustNLP workshop, co-located with major ACL conferences since 2021, has grown from 8 to 41 papers per edition. Trustworthiness in NLP encompasses dimensions such as truthfulness, safety, fairness, robustness, privacy, and explainability. Post-hoc interpretability methods approximate model behavior from outside, while mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer the model's internal computations directly. The release of high-impact chat models activated all trust dimensions simultaneously, shifting focus toward truthfulness and safety alignment in subsequent generations.

## [Test-Time Self-Evolving GUI Grounding via Reflection-Guided Self-Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11191) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a test-time self-evolving framework for GUI visual grounding that adapts models to unseen interfaces without human labels. It constructs a closed-loop of exploration, evaluation, reflection, and internalization, using an MLLM-based Reflector and Reflection-Guided On-Policy Self-Distillation. This work addresses a practical limitation of frozen GUI grounding models, enabling post-deployment adaptation and improving performance on unseen interfaces. It could enhance the robustness and generalization of GUI agents, which are increasingly used in automation and accessibility applications. The framework achieves an average accuracy improvement of 7.4% over the base model across six benchmarks. It also introduces a Contrastive Calibration method to prevent incorrect auto-regressive prefixes from corrupting supervisory signals during failed explorations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: GUI visual grounding is the task of locating screen elements based on natural language instructions, which is fundamental for GUI agents. Test-time adaptation (TTA) allows models to adapt to new data distributions at inference time without retraining, and self-distillation is a technique where a model learns from its own outputs. This work combines these ideas to enable continuous improvement of GUI agents after deployment.

## [ConVAWG: Retrieval-Grounded Synthetic Dialogues for VAWG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11200) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ConVAWG, a retrieval-grounded framework that generates controlled synthetic multi-turn dialogues about violence against women and girls (VAWG). It releases over 6,000 dialogue events across 200 scenarios with rich metadata. This addresses a critical gap in modeling abuse as a relational, multi-turn phenomenon rather than just sentence-level toxicity. It enables researchers to study conversational dynamics in a sensitive domain where real data are scarce, with potential for social impact and downstream applications. The framework builds scenarios from persona seeds, UK ONS demographic patterns, official crime definitions, and retrieved Domestic Homicide Review cases. It uses hierarchical event timelines, multi-scene role-play, and activation-steered toxicity control, with evaluations including human assessment and LLM-as-Judge.

## [Surgical WAM: Video Pretraining Boosts Data-Efficient Robot Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11204) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Surgical WAM, a world-action model built on Cosmos Policy that jointly predicts future endoscopic observations and robot action chunks. It first pretrains on action-free endoscopic video, then fine-tunes on a fixed budget of action-labeled demonstrations, achieving an average success rate improvement from 63.5% to 77.8% across four simulated surgical tasks. This work addresses the critical challenge of data scarcity in surgical robotics by leveraging abundant action-free video, potentially reducing the cost and effort of collecting teleoperated demonstrations. It demonstrates a practical path toward scaling up surgical robot learning, which could accelerate the development of autonomous and assistive surgical systems. The model acts as a closed-loop, receding-horizon controller, executing a short prefix of each predicted action chunk and replanning from the resulting observation. The largest improvements were seen on contact-rich and bimanual tasks, including an absolute gain of 20 percentage points on PegTransfer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: World-action models (WAMs) are a class of embodied AI models that jointly predict future world states and robot actions, often leveraging video pretraining. In surgical robotics, collecting action-labeled demonstrations is costly, while endoscopic video is relatively inexpensive and abundant. This paper explores whether action-free video pretraining can provide transferable visual dynamics priors for closed-loop surgical control.

## [RL Framework Mapping Criterion Reveals Fundamental Differences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.07752) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a criterion for preserving relative intelligence when mapping between reinforcement learning (RL) frameworks and demonstrates that this criterion cannot be satisfied when mapping between certain deterministic and stochastic RL frameworks. This work provides a theoretical foundation for comparing RL frameworks, which is crucial for transferring agents across different environments and for AI safety research. It highlights inherent limitations in cross-framework interoperability, potentially guiding future framework design and standardization. The criterion is sufficient for preserving relative intelligence according to a specific method of measuring intelligence. The paper shows that this criterion cannot be met between certain deterministic and stochastic RL frameworks, suggesting fundamental differences between these versions of RL.

## [GAM-Agent: Game-Theoretic Multi-Agent Framework Boosts Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23399) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GAM-Agent is a novel multi-agent framework that models vision-language reasoning as a non-zero-sum game between specialized base agents and a critical agent, with an uncertainty-aware controller to dynamically adjust collaboration. It achieves significant accuracy improvements on benchmarks like MMMU, MMBench, MVBench, and V*Bench, boosting small-to-mid scale models by 5-6% and strong models like GPT-4o by 2-3%. This work introduces a game-theoretic perspective to multi-agent visual reasoning, offering a modular and scalable approach that improves both accuracy and interpretability. It could influence future designs of multi-agent systems for complex multimodal tasks, especially for smaller models that benefit most from collaboration. The framework uses structured claims, evidence, and uncertainty estimates for agent communication, and triggers multi-round debates when disagreement or ambiguity is detected. The approach is model-agnostic and has been validated across various VLM backbones, including Qwen2.5-VL-7B, InternVL3-14B, and GPT-4o.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) often struggle with complex reasoning tasks that require multiple perceptual subtasks. Multi-agent systems decompose problems into specialized roles, but traditional collaboration may not handle conflicts or uncertainty effectively. Game theory provides a framework for modeling strategic interactions, and non-zero-sum games allow agents to have both cooperative and competitive elements, leading to more robust outcomes. Uncertainty-aware control helps the system decide when to debate or rely on individual agents.

## [Closing 17-Year Gap: Open-Source Rank Reversal Detection in MCDA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00129) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents an algorithmic framework implemented in the open-source Scikit-Criteria library that operationalizes Wang and Triantaphyllou's three rank reversal test criteria (RRT1, RRT2, RRT3). It includes a large-scale audit of 27 pipeline/dataset combinations from 20 published MCDM methods, revealing that RRT3 fails for nearly half (48.1%) of published examples. This work closes a 17-year gap between theory and practice in MCDA, providing researchers and practitioners with a validated, open-source tool to detect rank reversal. The audit's findings highlight that rank reversal is pervasive in the current MCDM literature, underscoring the need for robustness checks in decision-making pipelines. The framework includes a controlled degradation strategy with hierarchical tie-breaking for RRT1, and dominance-graph construction with exact condensation and transitive reduction for RRT2/RRT3. The audit shows top-alternative stability (RRT1) is nearly universal (96.3%), but transitivity (RRT2) fails for 14.8% and recomposition consistency (RRT3) fails for 48.1% of cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-Criteria Decision Analysis (MCDA) is a field that helps decision-makers evaluate alternatives based on multiple conflicting criteria. Rank reversal occurs when the relative order of alternatives changes in ways that violate rational decision axioms, threatening the reliability of MCDA methods. Wang and Triantaphyllou (2008) proposed three test criteria to detect this phenomenon, but until now, no validated open-source implementation existed. Scikit-Criteria is a Python library that integrates MCDA methods into the scientific Python stack.

## [Situation Graph Prediction: A New Task for Perspective-Aware AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13319) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Situation Graph Prediction (SGP), a new task that frames user perspective modeling as an inverse inference problem, reconstructing structured, ontology-aligned representations from multimodal artifacts. The authors also propose a structure-first synthetic data generation strategy and present a diagnostic study across three frontier foundation models (GPT-4o, Gemini 2.5 Flash, Claude Sonnet 4). SGP addresses a critical data bottleneck in perspective-aware AI by enabling grounding without real labels, which could accelerate progress in personal agents and multimodal AI systems. The task formalizes a challenging inverse inference problem that goes beyond surface-level extraction, potentially leading to more human-centric AI. The diagnostic study shows a consistent positive gap between surface-level extraction and latent perspective inference across all three models, indicating that latent-state inference is consistently harder. The authors provide code, data, and supplementary resources on GitHub for reproducibility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Perspective-aware AI aims to model evolving internal states like goals, emotions, and contexts, but progress is limited by a data bottleneck: digital footprints are privacy-sensitive and perspective states are rarely labeled. Inverse inference problems start with effects (observations) and calculate the causes (latent states), which is the core of SGP. The structure-first synthetic generation strategy aligns latent labels with observable traces by design, providing a way to create training data without real labels.

CoEvoSkills introduces a self-evolving framework that enables LLM agents to autonomously generate complex, multi-file skill packages without ground-truth supervision. It couples a Skill Generator with a Surrogate Verifier that co-evolves to provide informative feedback, outperforming five baselines on SkillsBench across Claude Code and Codex. This addresses the limitations of manual skill authoring and existing self-evolving methods for tools, which cannot handle the complexity of skills. By enabling autonomous skill generation, CoEvoSkills could significantly reduce human effort and improve agent performance across diverse LLMs, impacting the broader AI agent ecosystem. The framework is validated on SkillsBench, a benchmark with 87 tasks across 8 domains, and generalizes to six additional LLMs beyond Claude Code and Codex. The code is publicly available on GitHub, and the paper is under review.

SkillsBench is a benchmark that measures how effectively agents leverage skills, and co-evolutionary verification is a paradigm where candidate artifacts and verifiers evolve iteratively to enhance robustness. Existing self-evolving methods for tools are not directly applicable to skills due to their increased complexity.

## [Planning Task Shielding: Detecting and Repairing Flaws via Unsolvability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07042) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces planning task shielding, a novel problem of detecting and repairing flaws in planning tasks by making them unsolvable. It proposes three approaches, including the optimal algorithm ALLMIN, which minimally modifies original actions to render the task unsolvable. This work addresses a practical challenge in AI planning: validating task definitions before deployment. By enabling automatic detection and repair of flaws, it improves the reliability and quality of planning systems, benefiting developers and users in safety-critical applications. The paper proposes three approaches, with ALLMIN being an optimal algorithm that minimizes the number of action modifications. Empirical evaluations show ALLMIN effectively shields planning tasks of increasing size by turning them unsolvable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: In automated planning, a planning task typically involves finding a sequence of actions to achieve a goal. However, goals can also encode undesirable properties, and planners can identify traces leading to flawed states. This paper flips the objective: instead of finding a plan, the goal is to modify the task to prevent reaching such states, making it unsolvable. This concept, called planning task shielding, helps validate and repair planning task definitions.

## [SCALAR: When Critique Improves AI-Assisted Physics Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06772) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SCALAR, an Actor-Critic-Judge pipeline for AI-assisted theoretical physics, and systematically analyzes how different critic feedback strategies and model pairings affect reasoning performance on quantum field theory and string theory problems. This work addresses a timely question about human-AI interaction in research, providing a controlled testbed for evaluating interaction structures that help or hinder AI-assisted reasoning. It has potential impact on agentic AI applications and the design of AI research assistants. The study varies the Actor persona, Critic feedback strategy, and Actor model family and scale. Key findings include that multi-turn dialogue improves over single-shot attempts, but the mechanism of improvement depends on the Actor-Critic pairing; increasing model scale (e.g., from DeepSeek-R1 8B to 70B) improves easier problems but not the hardest bottleneck; and constructive feedback matters most in asymmetric settings (e.g., Haiku Actor with Sonnet Critic).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for research-level reasoning tasks, and agentic AI systems that interact with users are becoming more common. The Actor-Critic-Judge pipeline is a common framework where an Actor generates solutions, a Critic provides feedback, and a Judge evaluates the final output. This paper applies this framework to theoretical physics problems, exploring how different interaction strategies affect performance.

## [CuSearch: Curriculum Rollout Sampling via Search Depth for Agentic RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11611) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CuSearch introduces a curriculum rollout sampling framework for RLVR in agentic RAG, using Search-Depth Greedy Allocation (SDGA) to prioritize deeper-search trajectories. Experiments show up to 11.8 exact-match points improvement over standard GRPO on ZeroSearch. This addresses a key inefficiency in RLVR training for agentic RAG, where uniform rollout sampling ignores the varying informativeness of trajectories. By improving training efficiency, it could accelerate development of more capable retrieval-augmented agents, benefiting applications like question answering and knowledge-intensive tasks. SDGA-Auto targets the deepest available trajectories in each batch, while SDGA-Phase explicitly advances the curriculum threshold as deeper trajectories become abundant. The method is annotation-free and works across different model types and retrieval frameworks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) trains policies using outcome-only rewards, which is promising for agentic RAG where intermediate steps are hard to supervise. In agentic RAG, an agent iteratively retrieves and reasons, and trajectories with deeper search provide more decision points for the retrieval sub-policy, offering denser supervision. Uniform sampling treats all trajectories equally, but deeper ones are more informative, motivating a curriculum that prioritizes them.

## [Singing Deepfakes: New Dataset and Text-Guided Detection Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27944) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the Singing Head DeepFake (SHDF) dataset, the first benchmark specifically for singing deepfakes, and proposes a Text-guided Audio-Visual Forgery Detection (T-AVFD) framework that generalizes across talking and singing scenarios. Singing deepfakes pose a new challenge because rhythmic vocalization weakens the audio-visual coupling that existing detectors rely on, leading to performance degradation. This work fills a critical gap by providing a benchmark and a robust detection method, which is essential for combating the growing threat of realistic singing deepfakes. The T-AVFD framework consists of a facial authenticity pattern learner that aligns facial features with multi-granularity textual descriptions, and a multi-modal differential weight learning module that preserves intrinsic audio-visual consistency and adaptively integrates it with authenticity patterns. Experiments on multiple talking head datasets and SHDF show consistent improvements over baselines and robustness under diverse perturbations.

**Background**: Audio-visual deepfake detection typically relies on cross-modal inconsistencies between audio and visual streams. However, in singing, rhythmic vocalization weakens this coupling, causing a domain shift that degrades detection performance. The proposed framework uses text guidance to learn generalizable authenticity patterns, addressing this limitation.

## [ReMMD: Realistic Multilingual Multi-Image Benchmark and Agentic Verifier for Misinformation Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReMMD introduces ReMMDBench, a real-world benchmark with 500 samples and 2,756 images covering five languages, two cross-lingual settings, and multi-image posts, along with ReMMD-Agent, a persistent-memory verifier that achieves 41.80% accuracy and 39.12% macro-F1 using GPT-5.2, while reducing cost by 17.5% relative to MMD-Agent and 79.9% relative to T²-Agent. This work addresses a critical gap in multimodal misinformation detection by focusing on realistic, multilingual, multi-image scenarios that existing benchmarks and methods often ignore. The benchmark and agentic framework provide valuable resources for the research community, potentially improving the reliability of automated fact-checking and misinformation mitigation in real-world settings. ReMMDBench includes five-way veracity labels, eight distortion labels, evidence provenance, and rationales, with three text-length tiers. ReMMD-Agent decomposes posts into atomic points, builds a reusable evidence set, and predicts structured verdicts, distortion diagnoses, and rationales, outperforming proprietary systems, open LVLMs, MMD-Agent, and T²-Agent in five-way veracity performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal misinformation detection involves analyzing posts that combine text and images to identify false or misleading content. Existing benchmarks often simplify the task by using short captions, single images, and binary labels, which do not reflect the complexity of real-world viral posts that feature long multilingual narratives and multiple images. Agentic verification refers to using AI agents that autonomously search for and evaluate evidence to verify claims, which can be costly but more thorough than static methods.

## [Affordable AIoT Benchmark Platform Measures Sim-to-Real Gap in RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10309) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers developed a real-world AIoT benchmark platform using commercially available components costing under USD 400, which measures the Sim-to-Real gap in reinforcement learning. The platform enables an agent on an edge device to play video games via a hardware-emulated keyboard, and experiments revealed a 1160% performance degradation for a simulation-trained agent after deployment. This is significant because it addresses the lack of a universal Sim-to-Real benchmark platform for AIoT, which is crucial for evaluating and improving RL algorithms in real-world settings. The low-cost, safe design (game score maximization) makes it accessible to researchers, potentially accelerating progress in AIoT and RL research. The platform uses two computers and hardware-emulated keyboard input, with vision input guiding the agent. Direct real-world training with DQN achieved about 49% of human-level performance after 10 million steps, demonstrating feasibility, while the simulation-trained agent showed a significant Sim-to-Real gap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is often trained in simulation due to the cost and safety risks of real-world trial-and-error. However, policies trained in simulation may not transfer well to real environments, a problem known as the Sim-to-Real gap. Industries like robotics have developed concurrent simulation and physical platforms to study this, but AIoT lacked such a benchmark. This platform fills that gap by providing a low-cost, safe environment for studying RL in AIoT systems.

## [UPAIR: Diagnosing Reasoning States via Uncertainty-Progress Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17188) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UPAIR introduces a training-free framework that uses the Uncertainty-Progress Alignment Hypothesis to diagnose reasoning states in large reasoning models, enabling selective intervention to mitigate overthinking and underthinking. It achieves up to 16.67 percentage points accuracy improvement and reduces generated tokens by up to 29.64%. This work addresses the critical issue of reasoning state-action mismatch in large reasoning models, which is exacerbated by test-time scaling. By providing a training-free diagnostic and intervention method, it offers a practical solution to improve efficiency and accuracy, potentially benefiting a wide range of AI applications that rely on chain-of-thought reasoning. The framework couples lightweight uncertainty monitoring with event-triggered joint diagnosis, mapping reasoning states to native continuation, selective strategy switching, or verification-guided stopping. Across three LRMs and five benchmarks, the stagnation diagnosis detects 64.3% of natural errors while flagging only 5.4% of correct samples, with online diagnosis costing less than 1% of natural-generation time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large reasoning models (LRMs) use test-time scaling to improve problem-solving by generating longer chain-of-thought reasoning, but this can lead to overthinking (excessive reasoning) or underthinking (insufficient reasoning), degrading performance. The Uncertainty-Progress Alignment Hypothesis posits that the relative timing of proxy answer uncertainty and latent reasoning progress can distinguish healthy, stagnant, and ready states, enabling appropriate interventions. This work builds on prior research that has empirically studied overthinking and underthinking in LLMs, such as the study 'Between Underthinking and Overthinking'.

## [SAE-StatSteer: Optimization-Free Activation Steering via Statistical Consensus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19364) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SAE-StatSteer introduces a transparent, optimization-free pipeline for activation steering in LLMs, which filters sparse autoencoder features through six reliability conditions and ranks survivors using an unweighted Borda consensus over three statistics (F-test, KSG mutual information, and Cohen's d). The method is evaluated on three Gemma-family models across four behavioral domains, demonstrating competitive performance with optimization-based baselines while maintaining output quality. This work addresses a key limitation in activation steering—raw success often overstates usable control because strong shifts can degrade generation quality. By providing a quality-conditioned evaluation protocol and an auditable, optimization-free method, it enhances the interpretability and practical applicability of SAE-based interventions, potentially influencing future research in LLM control and interpretability. The pipeline first filters features via six reliability conditions, then ranks survivors by Borda consensus over F-test, KSG mutual information, and Cohen's d, and finally combines selected SAE decoder rows using Cohen's-d weights. The evaluation protocol requires attribute movement while preserving relevance, richness, and coherence, and the authors report that effective steering is not governed by a universal layer or strength.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Activation steering is a lightweight technique for controlling LLM behavior at inference time by adding a direction to the residual stream, avoiding fine-tuning. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) decompose dense activations into approximately monosemantic features, making interventions more auditable. The Borda consensus is a voting method that aggregates rankings from multiple criteria, and the KSG estimator is a k-nearest-neighbor method for estimating mutual information between continuous variables.

## [Conditional Diffusion Models for Validated Dynamic Graph Simulation Continuations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21421) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a method that combines conditional diffusion models with symbolic admissibility checks to generate and structurally validate simulation scenario continuations in dynamic graph systems. The approach is evaluated on two controlled regimes, demonstrating that hard filtering can remove all invalid scenarios while retaining 84.4% of generated continuations. This work addresses a critical gap in generative modeling for simulations: ensuring structural consistency of generated continuations, not just distributional similarity. It provides a framework for assessing learned-distribution support, structural admissibility, and probability calibration separately, which is valuable for reliable scenario generation in complex systems. The method uses a Boolean admissibility indicator and a continuous violation score for hard filtering and soft weighting, with optional projection as a deterministic repair baseline. In the compact positive-control regime, unconstrained invalid mass is 0.002996, while in the medium-complexity regime it rises to 0.155929; soft weighting preserves an effective sample size ratio of 0.998764 but only reduces invalid mass to 0.148807.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Conditional diffusion models are a class of generative models that can produce data samples conditioned on certain inputs, such as partial trajectories, enabling controllable generation. Dynamic graph simulations model processes on networks where the state evolves over time, and ensuring that generated continuations satisfy structural constraints is crucial for their validity. This paper integrates an external symbolic layer to validate generated scenarios, addressing the divergence between learned distribution support and structural admissibility.

## [EviBack: Evidence-Constrained Teacher Backoff Improves Agentic RAG RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23955) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EviBack introduces an evidence-constrained teacher backoff mechanism that provides auxiliary supervision to all-zero rollout groups in reinforcement learning for Agentic RAG, while preserving verifiable actor rewards. It also features a fully automated GPT-5.5-assisted prompt engineering pipeline that produces a gated two-stage teacher model. This method addresses the sparse reward problem in outcome-based reinforcement learning for Agentic RAG, improving search efficiency and answer validity. It demonstrates practical gains in F1 and valid-answer rate across multiple benchmarks, which could enhance the reliability of autonomous search agents. EviBack separates evidence assessment from answer refinement to prevent reference answers from overriding evidence-insufficiency judgments. Across seven open-domain QA benchmarks and three Qwen3 scales, it improves F1 over Search-R1 and raises both single- and multi-hop macro F1, while reducing search, duplicate queries, and forced termination.

## [TrAC: Trace-Conditioned Answer Consistency for Efficient LLM Uncertainty Quantification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00422) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces TrAC, a novel uncertainty quantification framework that re-elicits a short answer from a completed reasoning trace to measure consistency, combining active and passive signals. It improves macro AUROC by 1.8% and reduces AURC by 3.4% relative to eight-sample self-consistency, while using only one complete trace and a short cached answer probe. This work addresses a gap in existing uncertainty quantification methods by actively re-eliciting answers from completed traces, offering a more compute-efficient alternative to sampling-based methods. It has implications for AI safety and reliability, enabling better abstention and adaptive compute allocation in LLM applications. TrAC consists of two components: Prefix-Conditioned Elicitation (PCE) for active re-elicitation and Trace Uncertainty Profile (TUP) for passive token-level uncertainty tracking. The framework is evaluated on five mathematical reasoning benchmarks and three LLM families, and when eight samples are already available, augmenting sample consensus with re-elicitation further improves macro AUROC by 4.3% and reduces AURC by 8.3%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Uncertainty quantification (UQ) in large language models (LLMs) is crucial for identifying when model outputs are likely to be incorrect, hallucinated, or unreliable. Existing UQ methods fall into categories such as passive single-trace methods, sampling-based methods, and active prefix-based methods, each with trade-offs in cost and accuracy. TrAC introduces a hybrid approach that combines active and passive signals from a single completed reasoning trace, aiming to improve efficiency without sacrificing performance.

## [DiffImaginE: Diffusion-Based Multimodal Entity Type Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03025) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DiffImaginE introduces a novel approach to multimodal named entity recognition (MNER) by formulating type verification as conditional latent diffusion inference, using denoising error as a surrogate for type-conditional negative log-likelihood. It replaces deterministic verifiers with a classifier-free-guided diffusion scorer trained with Min-SNR weighting, achieving consistent gains on Twitter-2015 and Twitter-2017. This work provides probabilistic semantics for entity type verification, addressing limitations of existing deterministic verifiers that compress diverse visual realisations into a single prototype. It could improve the accuracy and reliability of multimodal entity recognition systems, benefiting applications in social media analysis, information extraction, and question answering. The method retains a standard multimodal encoder stack and directly supervises per-type diffusion scores as classification logits, learning aggregation across noise levels and using antithetic sampling to reduce Monte Carlo comparison variance. Experiments on Twitter-2015 and Twitter-2017 show consistent gains over a matched deterministic ImaginE control, supported by ablations and paired significance tests.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal named entity recognition (MNER) determines whether a candidate span and entity-type hypothesis is supported by joint textual and visual evidence. Existing imagine-and-compare verifiers map each (span, type) pair to one predicted visual feature, compressing diverse visual realisations into a single prototype and providing a compatibility score without explicit probabilistic semantics. Conditional latent diffusion models perform diffusion in a lower-dimensional latent space, separating encoder-based representation learning from iterative, condition-guided denoising. The denoising error in such models can serve as a surrogate for negative log-likelihood, providing a principled way to rank competing hypotheses.

## [CastFSR: Fast-Slow-Reflect Agentic Framework for Context-Aware Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03031) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CastFSR, an agentic framework that formulates context-aware time series forecasting as a Fast-Slow-Reflect workflow, integrating fast data-driven priors, slow contextual reasoning, and reflective validation. It supports both training-free inference with off-the-shelf LLMs and efficient deployment via a two-stage SFT and reinforcement learning strategy. This framework addresses a clear gap in existing LLM-based forecasting methods by explicitly identifying relevant contexts, reasoning about their impacts, and validating forecasts against temporal and domain constraints. It has potential to improve forecasting accuracy in complex systems and demonstrates a meaningful advancement in applying LLMs to time series analysis. CastFSR consists of three stages: fast thinking profiles observations and selects lightweight forecasters to build a data-driven prior; slow deliberation retrieves contextual evidence and determines informative look-back windows; reflection iteratively refines forecasts for consistency. The framework can be deployed with compact LLMs through a two-stage SFT and reinforcement learning strategy, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Time series forecasting is crucial for decision-making in complex systems, where future dynamics are influenced by historical observations and evolving contextual features. Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled context-aware reasoning beyond numerical extrapolation, but existing methods often lack explicit mechanisms for context identification, impact reasoning, and forecast validation. Agentic frameworks, which use LLMs to orchestrate multiple steps, are emerging as a way to handle such complex tasks.

## [TARL: Transaction-Aware Reliable Ledgers for Executable Memory Management in Long-Term Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TARL introduces a memory state update framework that maps each statement to one of five executable actions (add, ignore, revise, reject, defer), moving beyond binary Write/Hold decisions. It also introduces TARL-Mem, a benchmark with fine-grained action labels and next-state targets, and demonstrates improvements in action prediction and state recovery across multiple evaluations. This work addresses a critical gap in long-term agent memory management by enabling more nuanced and reliable memory updates, which is essential for reducing memory pollution and cumulative corruption in AI systems. It could significantly improve the performance and reliability of LLM-based agents that rely on persistent memory. TARL identifies the affected memory, resolves its temporal scope, compares source reliability, and updates accepted, pending, and rejected ledgers. The framework is trained by comparing memory states produced by alternative update operations, encouraging the model to select the operation that leads to the correct result. Evaluations include in-domain, cross-source, temporal, counterfactual, and sequential scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term agents rely on persistent memory to retain knowledge across interactions, but a single update error can distort future retrieval and reasoning. Most existing systems reduce memory updating to a binary Write/Hold decision, which cannot distinguish between different types of updates. TARL addresses this by introducing a transaction-aware approach with five executable actions, similar to database transactions, to ensure reliable memory state updates.

## [ECHO: Local AI Health Assistant with Memory and Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06110) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ECHO is a locally-deployable agentic health assistant that integrates a ReAct-based chatbot with temporal memory, a two-stage safety layer, and a multimodal speech assessment module. It achieves a 94.9% tool-execution pass rate and 88.8% safety classification accuracy, running entirely on consumer hardware. ECHO addresses critical needs in chronic care management by providing a privacy-preserving, locally-run AI assistant that can operate without transmitting patient data to external services. Its combination of temporal memory and safety guardrails could set a new standard for trustworthy AI in healthcare, especially in regions with strict data regulations like GDPR and KVKK. The system uses LangGraph to orchestrate a ReAct loop with 17 clinical tools and a temporal knowledge graph for persistent memory. The safety layer combines a rule-based filter (under 1ms) with a signed graph neural network (GNN) using APPNP-style propagation, achieving 88.8% accuracy and 90.6% unsafe recall on a Turkish health dataset. The speech module fuses Whisper and BERT embeddings to estimate emotion, depression, and pain, with a mean macro F1 of 0.652.

Safety guardrails are essential in healthcare AI to prevent harmful outputs and ensure compliance with data protection regulations.

## [CIPO: Evidence-Oriented RL Reduces Confirmation Bias in Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06128) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Contextual Information Policy Optimization (CIPO), a reinforcement learning framework that explicitly aligns policy optimization with external evidence use in search agents. CIPO assigns dense, turn-level credit to reasoning actions influenced by retrieved information, combined with a global outcome reward to preserve answer correctness. This addresses a critical flaw in current search agents: confirmation bias, where agents rely on internal knowledge and use retrieval only to confirm pre-existing conclusions. By reducing prior-driven reasoning, CIPO could improve the reliability of LLM-based search and reasoning systems, which are increasingly used for knowledge-intensive tasks. CIPO requires neither human process annotations nor an additional reward model, making it practical to implement. Experiments on seven in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks show that CIPO reduces the prevalence of prior-driven reasoning and achieves excellent performance on most tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Search agents extend large language models (LLMs) by enabling them to acquire and use external evidence during multi-step reasoning. However, existing training methods often reward only final-answer correctness or intermediate progress, without directly assessing whether post-retrieval actions are grounded in the retrieved evidence. This misalignment encourages prior-driven reasoning, leading to confirmation bias and inefficient evidence use. CIPO addresses this by providing dense, turn-level rewards for evidence use, aligning policy optimization with external evidence.

TongGuOCR, a layout-aware and token-augmented multimodal large language model (MLLM), has been proposed for OCR of Chinese historical documents. It introduces a Layout-Aware Preprocessing module and a Token-Augmented Recognition module, achieving 93.76 AR on the M5HisDoc benchmark and reducing NED from 10.43 to 6.15 and RO-ED from 7.53 to 3.49. By outperforming existing OCR models and MLLMs, TongGuOCR could significantly advance digital humanities and archival research, enabling full-text retrieval and computational analysis of vast historical collections. The model uses character-level vocabulary expansion to give rare glyphs a direct one-token representation, and line-to-line transition modeling to inject discrete spatial displacement tokens for guiding reading order without precise coordinates. ## [StructReward: Efficient Dense Process Rewards for Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08326) ⭐️ 7.0/10

StructReward introduces a compute-efficient framework that provides dense step-level reward alignment for multimodal reasoning, using lightweight matching rules instead of costly annotations or external LLM judges. It combines aligned process rewards with final-answer consistency and output-validity rewards through a gated GRPO objective, and recycles policy rollouts for self-correction. This work addresses a significant gap in RLVR by providing dense process supervision without expensive annotations, potentially reducing computational overhead and improving self-correction in multimodal reasoning. It could enable more efficient and scalable reinforcement learning for multimodal models, benefiting researchers and practitioners in the field. The framework uses numerical, symbolic, and lexical matching rules to align generated reasoning steps with process-labeled reference steps, aggregating them into a dense process reward. It also uses a strong LLM to rewrite correct trajectories into reflection-oriented training instances, and recycles rollouts for response comparison and self-correction, all computed online without a learned verifier.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) typically uses binary rewards based on final-answer correctness, discarding intermediate step supervision. Process reward models offer finer-grained feedback but often require costly annotations or external LLM judges. StructReward aims to provide dense process rewards efficiently by using lightweight matching rules and recycling rollouts, reducing computational overhead.

## [ECAT: Adversarial Entropy Minimization for Android-to-HarmonyOS Migration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ECAT, a multi-agent framework that automates Android-to-HarmonyOS repository migration using adversarial entropy minimization, achieving 74.7% overall migration quality on the new A2H-RepoBench benchmark. This work addresses the challenging task of whole-repository migration, which is critical for the growing HarmonyOS ecosystem. It introduces a novel metric (Code Entropy) and a self-evolving memory mechanism, potentially advancing automated software migration and reducing manual effort. ECAT uses a generator-discriminator architecture where the discriminator computes Code Entropy and provides text gradients for file-level directives. The framework also distills successful trajectories into a memory tree for cross-repository knowledge transfer, and introduces A2H-RepoBench, the first real-world benchmark for Android-to-HarmonyOS migration.

LLM-based agents often struggle with long-horizon tasks, and entropy in software refers to the tendency of code to become chaotic over time. Adversarial entropy minimization is a technique borrowed from domain adaptation, where a discriminator guides a generator to reduce uncertainty.
