## [DNNs Generalize to Novel Orientations via Disseminated Invariance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.13445) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study reveals that Deep Neural Networks (DNNs) can generalize to objects in novel orientations by disseminating orientation-invariance learned from familiar objects, and this capability improves with more familiar objects but is limited to 2D rotations. This finding provides novel insights into how DNNs achieve generalization beyond their training distribution, linking to brain-like neural mechanisms, which could inform more robust and human-like AI systems in computer vision and cognitive modeling. The study identifies that dissemination is achieved via neurons tuned to common features between familiar and unfamiliar objects, and the generalization only works for 2D rotations of familiar orientations, not arbitrary 3D rotations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Deep Neural Networks (DNNs) are powerful at object recognition but often struggle with inputs outside their training distribution, such as objects in novel orientations. This paper explores how DNNs can generalize to such novel orientations, drawing parallels to the brain's ability to recognize objects from different viewpoints. The study uses computational experiments to uncover the underlying mechanisms, suggesting that orientation-invariance is not learned from scratch but disseminated from familiar objects.

## [Single-Qubit Quantum Neural Network Achieves One-Step Classification Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09486) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a resource-efficient Single-Qubit Quantum Neural Network (SQQNN) with a new data uploading technique, and for classification tasks, it proposes a novel training method inspired by polynomial regression that finds a global minimizer of the transformed least-squares objective in a single step. This work significantly accelerates the training of quantum neural networks by eliminating iterative optimization for classification, potentially making quantum machine learning more practical on near-term devices. It also demonstrates strong performance on benchmark datasets like Wisconsin Breast Cancer and MNIST, highlighting its versatility and scalability. The SQQNN uses parameterized single-qubit unitary operators and quantum measurements, and the authors prove that such networks can approximate any univariate function via a partial Fourier series, establishing exact correlations between trainable gate parameters and Fourier coefficients. The one-step training method for classification is inspired by polynomial regression and achieves global minimization of the transformed least-squares objective.

**Background**: Quantum neural networks (QNNs) are a class of quantum machine learning models that use parameterized quantum circuits to process data. Traditional training methods rely on iterative gradient-based optimization, which can be slow and prone to local minima. The single-qubit quantum neural network is a resource-efficient variant that uses only one qubit, making it suitable for noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ) devices. The new training method for classification leverages the structure of the least-squares objective to find a global minimizer analytically, avoiding iterative updates.

## [SynBoost: A Training-Free Framework to Accelerate Diffusion Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13058) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SynBoost is a new training-free acceleration framework for diffusion models that disentangles discretization and approximation errors, blending current noise estimation with a more accurate prediction from a larger timestep to reduce total sampling error. It integrates with existing samplers to improve speed and output quality, especially in extremely few-step regimes. This work addresses a known limitation in diffusion model sampling—the trade-off between speed and quality—by targeting both error sources simultaneously. It could enable faster inference for generative models without retraining, benefiting applications like real-time image generation and on-device deployment. SynBoost is validated on both unconditional and conditional generation tasks, covering pixel-space and latent-space DPMs. The framework is training-free and can be seamlessly integrated with existing samplers, showing substantial improvements in speed and output quality, particularly in extremely few-step regimes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate data by reversing a gradual noising process, but their iterative sampling is slow. Reducing sampling steps introduces discretization error, and existing high-order solvers have limited further optimization. SynBoost identifies an additional approximation error and proposes a dual-error disentanglement strategy to minimize total sampling error.

<a id="item-312"></a>
## [OpenDPDv2: Unified Framework for Efficient Neural Network DPD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06849) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OpenDPDv2 introduces an open-source end-to-end framework that unifies PA modeling, NN-DPD learning, and deployment-oriented optimization, featuring a new TRes-DeltaGRU architecture. On a 3.5 GHz GaN Doherty PA with a 200 MHz 256-QAM OFDM signal, it achieves -59.9 dBc ACPR and -42.1 dB EVM in FP32, and with 56% temporal sparsity and W12A12 quantization, it maintains -51.8 dBc ACPR and -35.2 dB EVM using only 450 active parameters, with a 4.5x reduction in forward-pass energy. This framework addresses the complexity bottleneck of NN-based DPD, making it more practical for real-world RF systems. By enabling aggressive sparsity and quantization without significant performance loss, it could accelerate the adoption of neural DPD in energy-constrained wireless transmitters, benefiting 5G/6G and wideband communication systems. The TRes-DeltaGRU architecture integrates a lightweight temporal residual path to maintain robustness under aggressive temporal sparsity, and supports joint optimization of sparsity and fixed-point quantization. The framework is built in PyTorch and includes code, datasets, and documentation publicly available on GitHub, with timing-accurate Gem5-based evaluation showing the energy reduction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Digital predistortion (DPD) is a technique used to linearize RF power amplifiers by pre-distorting the input signal to compensate for nonlinearities. Neural network-based DPD has shown superior performance but often increases computational complexity, making it challenging for real-time, energy-constrained applications. Temporal sparsity and quantization are techniques to reduce computation and memory footprint by skipping redundant operations and using lower-precision arithmetic, respectively.

This paper demonstrates that pivoting cube modular robots in 2D can be reconfigured effectively using only local sensing, with a neural network trained via reinforcement learning. The approach achieves near-optimal performance with nearest-neighbor interactions by using multiple information passing steps. This work challenges the assumption that global information is necessary for complex multi-agent reconfiguration, potentially enabling more scalable and distributed control in modular robotics. It also opens avenues for applying geometric deep learning to other space-relevant systems like CubeSat swarms. The study includes grid symmetries (rotation and mirroring) in the neural network architecture, which provides minor training benefits but reduces model size. The method is transferable to other systems with different action spaces, such as sliding cube robots and CubeSat swarms.

## [Token-Based Method Detects Spurious Correlations in Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04009) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a token-based diagnostic pipeline that applies leave-one-out token removal to detect spurious correlations in Vision Transformers. It quantifies model reliance on non-core visual cues using complementary measures at the token level, validated on ImageNet with both supervised and self-supervised models. Spurious correlations undermine model reliability and generalization, making their detection crucial for trustworthy AI. This work provides a novel interpretability tool for Vision Transformers, potentially improving model auditing and guiding more robust training methods. The method uses leave-one-out token removal to measure both aggregate and localized spurious effects. Experiments on ImageNet reveal that training methodology significantly impacts reliance on spurious correlations, and certain classes show strong reliance across models, often due to watermarks or background artifacts. A case study on invasive breast mass classification is also included.

## [HyWA: Hypernetwork-Based Personalized VAD Without Architecture Changes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12947) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HyWA introduces a hypernetwork-based weight adaptation method that converts a standard VAD into a personalized VAD (PVAD) while preserving the original architecture. It generates speaker-conditioned weights once at enrollment and requires no per-user optimization, reducing engineering costs. This approach addresses the practical problem of unwanted triggers in voice assistants by enabling target-speaker-only detection without architectural changes. It offers a deployment-friendly solution that can be applied to existing VAD models like FSMN and MarbleNet, potentially improving user experience and reducing false interruptions in full-duplex systems. HyWA was applied to pretrained Alibaba's FSMN and NVIDIA's MarbleNet VAD models, showing consistent improvements in target-speaker discrimination and false-positive suppression. In an integrated full-duplex barge-in pipeline, replacing generic VAD with HyWA-based PVAD reduced false-interruption detections from 88.9% to 9.9%.

**Background**: Voice activity detection (VAD) is an early gate in voice-assistant pipelines that detects speech from any speaker, leading to unwanted triggers from nearby conversations or assistant playback. Personalized VAD (PVAD) aims to detect only the enrolled target speaker's speech, but existing methods often require architectural changes to incorporate speaker information, increasing deployment costs. Hypernetworks are neural networks that generate weights for another network, enabling task-specific adaptation without altering the main model's architecture.

## [Hybrid Token Compression Boosts VLM Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.08240) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HTC-VLM introduces a hybrid visual token compression framework that combines continuous and discrete pathways, achieving 87.2% average performance retention across seven benchmarks with a single-token output budget, outperforming the leading continuous baseline at 81.0%. This work addresses a critical efficiency challenge in vision-language models by enabling extreme token compression without significant performance loss, which could reduce computational and memory costs for real-world deployments. It also introduces a novel hybrid approach that balances semantics and appearance, potentially influencing future research in efficient VLM design. The framework uses a continuous pathway to preserve ViT patch features and a discrete pathway with MGVQ quantization to provide semantic anchors, fused into a 580-token hybrid sequence and compressed into a single token using a disentanglement attention mask and a bottleneck. The paper also analyzes token-budget scaling, cross-architecture generalization, inference efficiency, robustness, and information-theoretic properties.

Existing compression methods face a trade-off: continuous compression can weaken high-level semantics, while discrete quantization may lose fine-grained appearance details. HTC-VLM aims to overcome this by disentangling semantics and appearance through two complementary pathways.

## [GraFine: Efficient Graph RAG with Retrieval-Time Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18579) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GraFine introduces a retrieval-time refinement method for Graph RAG that couples semantics-aware expansion (SPX) with graph-aware pruning (GSR), improving accuracy and efficiency. The paper also proposes Topological Recall (TR) as a new metric to quantify topological proximity between retrieved and oracle nodes. This work addresses critical blind spots in existing Graph RAG methods, namely semantically blind graph expansion and topology blind pruning, which often lead to suboptimal retrieval. By providing a more efficient and accurate retriever, GraFine can enhance the quality of RAG systems on text-rich knowledge graphs, benefiting applications like question answering and document analysis. GraFine alternates between two refinement stages: Semantic Proximity eXpansion (SPX) for adding semantically relevant nodes, and a Graph Smoothing Reranker (GSR) for pruning based on graph structure. Experiments on reference networks and text-rich knowledge graphs show that GraFine improves retrieval accuracy and generation quality while maintaining time-efficiency. The code is available on GitHub.

**Background**: Graph RAG enhances retrieval-augmented generation by leveraging knowledge graphs to provide structured context, improving over naive RAG that relies on plain text snippets. Corpus graphs are constructed from documents, where nodes represent text chunks or entities, and edges represent relationships. Retrieval-time refinement aims to improve the retrieval process without interleaving with generation, which is often slow.

The paper introduces ECIsem, a training-free diagnostic that ranks hard-negative sources for dense retrieval using frozen target-encoder embeddings and validity-weighted residual directions. It shows that a single lexical scalar, mean lexical residuality, can reproduce the transfer ordering across all tested blocks. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in dense retrieval: hard-negative source selection is typically deferred until after fine-tuning and evaluation. By providing a training-free diagnostic, ECIsem could save significant computational resources and enable more principled negative sampling, benefiting the broader information retrieval community. ECIsem is training-free but not label-free, requiring a query, a labeled positive, and an explicit candidate negative for each scored example. The log-determinant operates in its linear regime at the studied scale, reducing the score to the mean validity weight; however, constructed controls reveal an identifiability limit where a plausible false negative is non-inverted, near the positive, and lexically novel, which the gates reward.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Dense retrieval converts queries and documents into dense vectors and compares them using distance metrics, often relying on contrastive learning with hard negatives to improve performance. Hard-negative mining is crucial but typically requires fine-tuning and downstream evaluation to determine the best sources. ECIsem aims to rank these sources before fine-tuning using frozen embeddings, potentially streamlining the training pipeline.

<a id="item-320"></a>
## [XAI Correctness Does Not Always Improve Human Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25251) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A user study (N=200) manipulated explanation correctness at four levels (100%, 85%, 70%, 55%) in a synthetic time series classification task, finding that forward simulation accuracy dropped only at 70% and 55% relative to fully correct explanations, with no significant difference between 85% and 100%. This challenges the common assumption in XAI evaluation that higher functional correctness (faithfulness) directly translates to better human understanding. The findings highlight the need to validate computational metrics against human outcomes, impacting how XAI methods are assessed and designed. Correctness was defined against known ground truth, not estimated from a trained model. Even fully correct explanations did not guarantee understanding, as forward simulation accuracy was bimodal, with only a subset of participants well above chance. Lower correctness reduced the number of participants predicting accurately rather than lowering accuracy uniformly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Explainable AI (XAI) methods are often evaluated using functional correctness metrics like faithfulness or fidelity, which estimate how closely an explanation reflects the model's reasoning. Forward simulation is a human-grounded evaluation method where participants predict the model's decisions based on explanations, and their accuracy serves as a proxy for understanding. This study uses feature-attribution-style explanations in a synthetic task to isolate the effect of correctness.

## [BiScale-GTR: Fragment-Aware Graph Transformers for Molecular Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06336) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BiScale-GTR introduces a self-supervised molecular representation framework that uses context-grounded shared fragment tokens, constructed via graph Byte Pair Encoding (graph-BPE) with Weisfeiler-Lehman (WL)-based fragment identity, chemical validity filtering, and recursive out-of-vocabulary decomposition. The framework achieves strong performance on MoleculeNet, PharmaBench, and the Long Range Graph Benchmark. This work addresses a key challenge in fragment-based molecular learning: balancing fragment reusability with local atomic context sensitivity. It could advance drug discovery and cheminformatics by providing more interpretable and accurate molecular representations. The framework uses atom-level GNN representations grounded into shared fragment tokens via atom-to-fragment pooling and gated fusion, enabling context-dependent representations. A structure-aware fragment Transformer performs global reasoning over these tokens, capturing reusable substructure identity, local chemical context, and long-range dependencies.

## [RankFormer: Propose-then-Select Transformer for Multi-Agent Trajectory Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07126) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RankFormer introduces a pure Transformer-based model for multi-agent multimodal trajectory prediction, using two parallel decoders to generate trajectories and probabilities separately. It avoids graph structures and manual intention labels, following a propose-then-select strategy where spatial interactions are only used for probability estimation. This work addresses key limitations of existing trajectory prediction methods that rely on graph neural networks or manual intention labels, offering a simpler and more scalable alternative. It could improve autonomous driving and traffic safety systems by providing more robust multimodal predictions without extra annotation efforts. The model encodes historical trajectories and employs two parallel decoders on separate tracks for trajectory generation and probability estimation. It is evaluated on a highway dataset and a pedestrian dataset, showing solid performance, and the propose-then-select strategy reveals that agent-agent spatial interactions are only needed for probability estimation, not trajectory generation.

## [PinpointQA Benchmark Tests MLLMs' Small Object Spatial Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08991) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PinpointQA, a new benchmark built from ScanNet++ and ScanNet200, introduces 10,094 QA pairs across 1,024 indoor scenes to evaluate MLLMs' ability to localize small objects with spatial precision. It features four progressively challenging tasks, from target presence verification to structured spatial prediction. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating MLLMs for embodied AI, where precise localization of small objects is essential for real-world interaction. By isolating spatial grounding capabilities, it provides a diagnostic tool and training resource that could accelerate progress in robotics and indoor navigation. Ground-truth annotations are derived from aligned 3D geometry and instance-level annotations, while models only receive sampled RGB video frames. Evaluations show a consistent performance decline across task progression, with structured spatial prediction being particularly challenging, but supervised fine-tuning substantially improves performance.

## [FLiD: Lightweight Field-Localized Forgery Detection for ID Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09089) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FLiD is a new lightweight framework that localizes facial and textual regions in identity documents using a fine-tuned YOLO11 detector, then classifies field-level forgeries with a frozen MobileNetV3-Small backbone and a small 191K-parameter head. It achieves AUC scores of 0.834, 0.926, and 0.837 for face, text, and combined attacks, respectively, and reduces Equal Error Rate by 28–29 percentage points compared to a full-document baseline. This work addresses a specific security gap in eKYC systems, which are widely used by banks, fintech platforms, and telecom providers for remote identity verification. By focusing on localized forgeries in structured identity documents, FLiD offers a more efficient and accurate solution than general-purpose forgery detectors, potentially improving fraud prevention while being suitable for resource-constrained deployments. FLiD uses a fine-tuned YOLO11 detector to localize facial and textual regions, and a frozen MobileNetV3-Small backbone to extract compact representations. It requires 13x fewer trainable parameters and 21x fewer FLOPs per field (~8x per full multi-field document) compared to general-purpose detectors, making it practical for data- and resource-constrained KYC deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: eKYC (electronic Know Your Customer) is a digital identity verification process used by businesses to confirm a customer's identity remotely, often by comparing an uploaded identity document with a selfie or live facial capture. Identity documents have a structured layout with security-relevant information concentrated in specific fields, such as the face photo and textual details. Existing image-forgery detectors are designed for natural images and do not account for this structure, making them less effective for detecting localized manipulations in ID documents. YOLO11 is a state-of-the-art object detection model that can be fine-tuned for specific tasks like locating facial and textual regions.

A new preprint proposes using a reinforcement learning (RL) agent to decide when students should access generative AI, treating access timing as implicit scaffolding. In a controlled study with 105 higher education students, the RL-based timing improved post-test performance and metacognitive accuracy compared to unrestricted access. This research addresses the growing concern of over-reliance on generative AI in education, which can lead to metacognitive disengagement and reduced learning. By offering a scalable, theory-grounded strategy that works with off-the-shelf tools, it could influence how educators and designers implement AI access policies in learning environments. The RL agent's reward function is grounded in metacognitive theory, cognitive load theory, and productive failure. Exploratory comparisons with fully restricted access suggested that timed access may reduce task errors and time on task relative to complete withholding.

This paper proposes a stage-wise preference optimization framework that progressively constructs hallucination-focused preference pairs near known failure boundaries, using Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to reduce hallucination in vision-language models (VLMs). The framework emphasizes ambiguous spatial orientation, object relationships, OCR uncertainty, and adversarial false-premise training. Hallucinated negatives are generated through minimally perturbed yet visually inconsistent alternatives, enabling DPO to better separate grounded reasoning from plausible hallucination.

## [Matching Principle: When Training Penalties Cover Deployment Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22800) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the Matching Principle, a theoretical framework that determines when a training penalty covers deployment shift. It shows that isotropic penalties, such as Gaussian or Tikhonov regularization, can reduce deployment drift without requiring axis knowledge. This work provides a clear, testable hypothesis for improving model robustness against distribution shift, a critical issue in machine learning deployment. It offers practical guidance for regularization design, potentially influencing how models are trained for real-world applications. The paper proves ridge theorems for linear models and validates the principle across seven domains, showing that a named second-moment penalty outperforms unregularized training. It also demonstrates that matching beats even-spread, which beats wrong-axis penalties when axes are known, but a residual floor remains when axes are missed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Distribution shift occurs when the test data distribution differs from the training data, often degrading model performance. Regularization techniques like Tikhonov or Gaussian noise injection aim to improve generalization by penalizing large model parameters or encouraging smoothness. The Matching Principle formalizes how the training penalty should align with the deployment directions to minimize drift.

This paper introduces a task-aware flow-based generative framework that reformulates Flow Matching to learn optimal subsampling masks for compressed sensing, achieving state-of-the-art reconstruction performance on image classification, reconstruction, and MRI acceleration tasks. This work bridges generative modeling and compressed sensing, offering a unified, flexible approach to designing data- and task-driven sensing schemes. The method achieved a Peak Signal-to-Noise Ratio (PSNR) of 25.17 dB at a 5% subsampling rate on the CelebA dataset for image reconstruction, and 29.24 dB for 8x accelerated MRI reconstruction on the fastMRI dataset, with minimal computational overhead. The framework is a proof-of-concept, demonstrating feasibility but not yet a major breakthrough.

Flow matching is a recent generative modeling paradigm that trains continuous normalizing flows by regressing vector fields, offering a simulation-free alternative to diffusion models. This paper combines these concepts by using a flow model to learn optimal subsampling masks, which determine which measurements to acquire, thereby enhancing reconstruction performance.

This finding reveals a fundamental behavioral link between foundational LLMs and their fine-tuned descendants, enabling efficient transfer of truthfulness improvements. TruthProbe offers a lightweight, plug-and-play solution to reduce hallucination in both LLMs and MLLMs, which is crucial for reliable AI deployment. The inheritance of truthful heads is consistent with attention-head weight preservation, and these heads attend to query-relevant evidence. TruthProbe improves contextual truthfulness on HaluEval and reduces multimodal hallucination on POPE and CHAIR, with base-LLM Truth Scores transferring effectively to fine-tuned descendants.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are often fine-tuned into specialized multimodal LLMs (MLLMs), forming model lineages. Attention heads in transformers are known to capture specific functions; this paper quantifies head-level context-truthfulness scores to show that truthful heads are inherited across lineages. TruthProbe leverages this by reweighting head contributions based on Truth Scores, offering a training-free enhancement.

## [Uncertainty-Based Gating Reduces Reranking Costs Without Performance Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31087) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Training-Free Gated Reranking, which uses model uncertainty to decide when to rerank few-shot examples, reducing computational costs by up to 54% for NLU and 20% for MT while matching full reranking performance. This challenges the common assumption that reranking always improves performance, showing that it can degrade results and that targeted reranking based on uncertainty is more efficient. It offers a practical way to cut computational overhead in LLM applications like few-shot learning and retrieval-augmented generation. The method is training-free, requiring no additional model training or fine-tuning. Experiments were conducted across 8 LLMs, 7 NLU datasets, and 18 MT domain-direction combinations, with a calibrated operating point balancing cost and performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Few-shot learning often involves retrieving relevant examples and then reranking them to improve selection quality. Reranking is computationally expensive, and this paper shows it can sometimes hurt performance. Model uncertainty, a measure of how confident a model is in its predictions, can be used to identify when reranking is likely to help.

## [EventCoT: Event-Centric Video Chain-of-Thought for Temporal Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04872) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EventCoT introduces the first event-centric video chain-of-thought framework for reasoning temporal localization (RTL), converting videos into compact event tokens and grounding time intervals via embedding matching. It achieves state-of-the-art results on ActivityNet-RTL with fewer visual tokens and strong zero-shot performance on ReXTime. This work addresses the challenge of jointly performing high-level reasoning and precise temporal grounding in video understanding, which is crucial for applications like video question answering and surveillance. By reducing visual token usage, EventCoT improves efficiency and could enable more scalable multimodal reasoning systems. EventCoT first detects event boundaries from frame-wise features and extracts event features, then reasons within these events to generate answers. It uses embedding matching to align placeholder tokens with visual embeddings for time interval grounding, and the code will be released for research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reasoning temporal localization (RTL) requires a model to output an answer that includes the supporting time interval, combining reasoning and grounding in one response. Traditional methods often rely on dense visual tokens, which are computationally expensive. Event-centric tokenization condenses video into meaningful event units, improving efficiency and interpretability. Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting enhances reasoning by breaking down complex tasks into intermediate steps.

## [AI Anthropomorphism Critique: Institutional Bias and Neurodivergent Harm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00961) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.00961) challenges the dominant framing of AI anthropomorphism as user error, arguing it reflects institutional bias and disproportionately harms neurodivergent users. The paper was posted on August 2, 2026, and offers a critical perspective on how institutions adjudicate the legitimacy of human-AI interactions. This matters because it shifts the ethical debate on AI anthropomorphism from individual user error to systemic institutional power, potentially influencing how AI companies and regulators design safeguards. It highlights an underexplored harm to neurodivergent users, who may be pathologized for forming meaningful connections with AI, and calls for more equitable framing in AI research and policy. The paper argues that the anthropomorphism frame operates from institutional advantage, not earned epistemic authority, and reproduces itself through a self-validating evidentiary loop. It does not engage whether anthropomorphic interpretations are correct, but challenges whether governing bodies have met the conditions to determine them, and outlines methodological commitments for an equitable framing.

The paper introduces dots.tts.edit, a speech editing system built on the continuous autoregressive dots.tts model, which uses transcript-grounded XML-style tags to explicitly specify edit operations and their target regions. It also presents doteBench, a bilingual evaluation suite for measuring instruction following, local preservation, and audio quality across four editing controls. This work addresses the ambiguity of natural language instructions in speech editing by providing a precise, explicit interface, which is crucial for content creation tools. It demonstrates a practical application of continuous autoregressive models, potentially influencing future human-computer interaction and speech processing systems. The system supports four editing controls: lexical content, affective expression, pitch and speaking-rate delivery, and temporal phrasing, using text, emotion, prosody, and pause editing. Experiments show leading instruction following and local preservation across five editing categories, with audio quality comparable to existing open-source systems, and negligible differences from the base model in zero-shot TTS recognition error rate and speaker similarity.

The continuous autoregressive model, such as dots.tts, represents speech as continuous features, offering an efficient alternative to quantization-based models. XML-style tags provide a structured, externally inspectable way to specify edits on transcripts, avoiding explicit timestamp alignment.

## [Policy-Masked Private Experts: Auditable Capability Access Control in Sparse MoE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06690) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Policy-Masked Private Experts, a method that freezes a pretrained sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) model, trains a disjoint private expert branch, and controls its reachability during top-k routing to enforce auditable and reversible capability access control. Experiments on Qwen3-30B-A3B and DeepSeek-V2-Lite demonstrate zero unauthorized private execution across 64 adversarial scenarios and 96 deny/fail-closed events. This work addresses a novel systems question: whether trusted authorization can determine which newly trained parameters are reachable by the forward pass, offering a new layer of security for deployed MoE models. It provides a testable claim and empirical evidence that can influence how model access control is implemented, potentially enabling fine-grained, reversible control over model capabilities in production environments. The method freezes the base MoE model and trains a disjoint expert branch, selecting between public and private pools before top-k routing. In experiments, three Qwen BF16 seeds updated all 32 private experts while the public fingerprint remained unchanged; independent hooks exactly matched 11,616 routed private rows, and allow-deny-allow recovery was exact. The private branch improved exact tool use by 5.0 and 21.3 percentage points on two benchmarks, while a parameter-matched LoRA with a post-hoc request gate left 1,225 adapter calls under deny, whereas the disjoint expert branch left none.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of expert networks per input via top-k routing, enabling efficient scaling of model capacity. The trusted computing base (TCB) refers to all hardware, firmware, and software components critical to system security; this paper assumes a TCB to enforce access control. The method leverages the routing mechanism to control which experts are reachable, providing a way to separate execution control from task utility.

## [MaskFlow: A New Training Framework for Precise Regional Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06929) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MaskFlow introduces a novel training framework for regional image editing that incorporates masks into the probability path and flow-matching objective, along with a Soft-Poisson de-seaming module and a data synthesis pipeline to create the MEData dataset. This work addresses the challenging problem of precise localization, consistent background preservation, and seamless boundary transitions in regional image editing, which is crucial for applications like photo editing and graphic design. It demonstrates consistent improvements over competing methods, potentially advancing the state of the art in controllable image generation. The framework integrates masks into the probability path and flow-matching objective, coordinating generation inside the editable region with source preservation outside. The Soft-Poisson de-seaming module refines the predicted vector field during training and sampling to improve smooth integration, and the data synthesis pipeline constructs the MEData dataset for training and research.

**Background**: Regional image editing aims to modify specific areas of an image while preserving the rest. Flow matching is a generative modeling paradigm that learns a vector field to transform a simple distribution into a target data distribution, often used in diffusion models. The Soft-Poisson module likely draws on Poisson-based techniques for seamless blending, and the MEData dataset is designed to support mask-based editing tasks.

## [EliSeg: Verified Target Construction for Report-Grounded Abnormality Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07299) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EliSeg introduces a novel actor-verify-revise framework for report-grounded abnormality segmentation, which constructs segmentation targets directly from unfiltered radiology reports without predefined target identities or spatial prompts. The framework integrates grammar-constrained target construction with mask generation, and it outperforms existing methods on MIMIC-CXR-ILS and CheXlocalize datasets. This work addresses a significant underexplored problem in medical image segmentation: handling ambiguous and unfiltered radiology reports. By eliminating the need for hidden target oracles, EliSeg could enable more autonomous and clinically applicable AI systems that directly interpret reports to guide segmentation, potentially improving diagnostic workflows. EliSeg uses a grammar-constrained Actor to propose target slots and masks, an independent text-only Verifier to reconstruct the eligible finding inventory, and a Revision step that selectively re-executes the Actor when target structures disagree. Experiments on MIMIC-CXR-ILS show consistent improvements over direct segmentation and extract-then-segment cascades, with effective suppression of masks for ineligible mentions; transfer to CheXlocalize demonstrates generalizability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Radiology reports describe clinical observations but often contain ambiguous, negated, or irrelevant findings, making it challenging to directly use them for segmentation. Traditional segmentation methods typically require a predefined target identity or spatial prompt, which acts as a hidden oracle. EliSeg aims to remove this dependency by jointly constructing and verifying segmentation targets from the report text, leveraging grammar-constrained decoding and iterative verification-revision mechanisms.

## [ELBench: New Benchmark Evaluates Education-Facing LLMs Across Four Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09548) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ELBench is introduced as the first benchmark to evaluate education-facing large language models across four integrated requirements: general capability, safety and trustworthiness, basic education, and high-level cultivation. The benchmark evaluates nine models, including seven frontier general-purpose systems and two education-specialized variants, under a common protocol. This benchmark addresses a critical gap by evaluating education-facing LLMs as an integrated profile rather than in isolation, which is essential for real-world deployment in tutoring and teaching assistant roles. The findings reveal that safety is anti-correlated with practical teaching ability, and that education-specialized models do not lead in education modules, highlighting challenges for the AI-in-education community. The benchmark combines curated public sources with newly synthesized safety and cultivation data, and uses a task taxonomy with task-appropriate scoring. Module-level profiles with bootstrap confidence intervals show that the top six models are statistically indistinguishable on overall score, yet their module leaders differ substantially; safety is anti-correlated with practical teaching (r = -0.83).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly used in education as tutors, teaching assistants, and content generators, requiring accuracy, safety, instructional usefulness, and alignment with pedagogical goals simultaneously. Existing benchmarks evaluate these requirements in isolation, so none assesses education-facing suitability as an integrated profile. ELBench aims to fill this gap by providing a multi-dimensional evaluation framework.

## [Mass Vulnerability Scans Spoof AI Bot User-Agents](https://knownagents.com/insights) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Attackers are increasingly spoofing AI bot user-agents like ClaudeBot, GPTBot, and PerplexityBot to disguise mass vulnerability scans and other malicious traffic. This represents an evolution of existing junk traffic rather than a completely new threat. This matters because it complicates bot detection and mitigation for website operators, who must now verify the legitimacy of AI crawlers to avoid blocking legitimate ones. It also highlights the growing use of AI-related identifiers as a disguise, which could erode trust in AI bot traffic. User-agent strings can be easily spoofed, so verification via IP address ranges and reverse DNS checks is recommended. Many spoofed bots originate from VPS providers, and blocking these providers can eliminate a significant portion of fake bot traffic.

User-agent strings identify these bots, but they can be spoofed by malicious actors to bypass security measures. Mass vulnerability scanning is a common practice where attackers probe many websites for weaknesses, and spoofing AI bot user-agents is a tactic to avoid detection.

## [Neuroevolution Arena: Nested Ecological Evaluation of Neural Update Regimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10323) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Neuroevolution Arena, a GPU-accelerated spatial ecology for evaluating update-and-inheritance regimes across neural architectures, and proposes a nested evaluation protocol that separates training-run artifacts from evaluation contexts. It reveals discrepancies between training fitness and ecological outcomes across three regimes (EvoEvo, EvoRL, RLRL) and two architectures over 50,000 generations. This work addresses a critical gap in artificial-life research by showing that training performance does not necessarily predict ecological success, which has implications for designing robust neuroevolution systems. The proposed nested evaluation protocol offers a more rigorous methodology for comparing algorithms in competitive environments, potentially influencing future benchmarks in evolutionary computation and artificial life. The study uses three update-and-inheritance regimes (EvoEvo, EvoRL, RLRL) crossed with two neural architectures, running 50,000 generations with three independent runs per condition, resulting in 18 saved elite controllers. These are then evaluated in an aligned-run frozen-evaluation design with 198 computational jobs, averaging over three ecological contexts (two cooperation-permitting, one attack-permitting). The prespecified survival endpoint showed a complete floor effect, and six-way winners varied across artifacts and contexts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Neuroevolution is a form of artificial intelligence that uses evolutionary algorithms to generate neural networks, commonly applied in artificial life and evolutionary robotics. In competitive artificial-life systems, controllers trained under one evaluation may rank differently under ecological evaluation, where agents interact in a shared environment. The paper introduces a GPU-accelerated spatial ecology to study these discrepancies, building on concepts like ecological inheritance and niche construction from eco-evo-devo.

## [Hierarchical Compositionality for Assistive AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10330) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a novel AI agent architecture that embeds hierarchical compositionality and simple heuristics to resolve ambiguity in human-agent interactions, outperforming state-of-the-art data-driven baselines in experiments. This work challenges the prevailing reliance on LLMs for AI agents by reviving early AI principles, offering a more transparent and resource-efficient alternative. It could influence future agent design, especially in assistive applications where ambiguity resolution and user adaptation are critical. The architecture represents domain objects using primitive attributes from human-validated semantic feature norms, and builds a hierarchical combination of attributes and concepts from limited interaction history. It reasons with compositional knowledge, domain axioms, and models of semantic compatibility, session salience, and user-specific thematic preference, requesting clarification when needed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly used to assist humans, with LLMs considered state-of-the-art but suffering from opacity, high resource consumption, and arbitrary decisions in novel situations. Early AI pioneers emphasized compositional knowledge and heuristics, which modern methods underutilize. This paper explores applying those principles to disambiguation in human-agent interactions.

## [RL2C: Q-Learning Optimizes Laser Cutting Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10549) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces RL2C, a Q-learning-based algorithm with an epsilon-greedy policy, to dynamically optimize laser cutting parameters such as focal length and laser power. It reduces optimization steps by up to 12.5% and processing time by up to 81.8% compared to existing RL-based methods. This work demonstrates the practical potential of reinforcement learning in industrial automation, specifically for laser cutting of optical films. It can improve cut quality, reduce material wastage, and minimize manual intervention, benefiting manufacturers seeking efficient and precise cutting processes. RL2C incorporates a dynamic environment space adaptability mechanism to handle new states encountered during multi-batch learning. Experimental results show it requires fewer steps and less time to find optimal parameters, with up to 12.5% fewer optimization steps and 81.8% less processing time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is a machine learning paradigm where an agent learns to make decisions by interacting with an environment and receiving rewards. Q-learning is a model-free RL algorithm that learns the value of actions in states, and the epsilon-greedy policy balances exploration and exploitation by randomly choosing actions with probability epsilon. In laser cutting, parameters like focal length and laser power must be tuned for different film types, traditionally done by trial-and-error, which is slow and inaccurate.

## [Meta-Scientific Study of BRACIS Reveals LLM Growth and Community Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09964) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A meta-scientific study analyzed all 1,066 papers published at BRACIS from 2015 to 2025, revealing that LLM research grew from zero before 2020 to 19% of papers in 2024. The study also found an hourglass-shaped author community and highly concentrated citations. This study provides the first comprehensive meta-scientific analysis of Brazil's main AI conference, offering insights into publication trends and community dynamics that can inform research policy and funding. It highlights the rapid adoption of LLMs in a regional context and the challenges of open access. The study used DBLP metadata, 6,765 Google Scholar citations, and full texts of papers. It found that 80.5% of 2,623 authors appear in only one edition, while institutions return at nearly three times the author rate. The top 1% of papers carry 27% of citations, and artifact release rose from 8.9% in 2015 to 57.3% in 2023.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: BRACIS is the main Brazilian conference on AI, hosted by the Brazilian Computer Society since 2012. Meta-science is the systematic study of how scientific knowledge is generated, often using bibliometric data to analyze publication trends and community structures. The study's findings on LLM growth reflect a global trend, but also highlight regional specifics such as paywalled proceedings and low preprint rates.

## [Intuitionistic Fuzzy Deep RVFL Networks Boost Robustness Against Noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10007) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces IF-dRVFL and IF-edRVFL, which integrate intuitionistic fuzzy theory into deep Random Vector Functional Link networks to handle noise and outliers by assigning adaptive sample weights based on membership and non-membership degrees in kernel space. This addresses a key limitation of existing deep RVFL models that treat all samples uniformly, improving robustness for real-world noisy datasets. It offers a practical enhancement for classification tasks in fields like medical diagnosis or sensor data analysis. The models compute membership degrees based on distance to class centroids and non-membership degrees from local neighborhood heterogeneity. Experiments on UCI and KEEL benchmarks with Gaussian noise show superiority over existing fuzzy and non-fuzzy approaches; source code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Random Vector Functional Link (RVFL) networks are single hidden layer feedforward networks with randomly generated input-to-hidden weights and analytically computed output weights, offering fast training. Deep RVFL (dRVFL) stacks multiple layers, and ensemble deep RVFL (edRVFL) combines features from all layers. Intuitionistic fuzzy sets extend fuzzy sets by considering both membership and non-membership, providing a richer framework for handling uncertainty.

This study applies an ensemble-based Isolation Forest to GRACE satellite data to detect groundwater storage anomalies in Ghana from 2004 to 2024, identifying 12 anomalous months (5 deficits, 7 surpluses) and revealing spatial patterns with more deficits in the north and surpluses in the south. ## [Deterministic Guardrail Withholds Pair-Completing Chunks in Streaming LLM Output](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10279) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a deterministic guardrail for streaming LLM output that withholds the first chunk making both predicates of a paired lexical signature observable. Experiments across four signature families and eight chunk sizes showed streaming decisions matched the buffered scanner and withheld every pair-completing chunk. This addresses the release-timing problem in streaming LLM moderation, where complete-response moderation acts too late and repeated semantic classification is costly and unstable. It offers a deterministic, exact backstop for small fixed policies, complementing semantic moderation and potentially improving safety in real-time LLM applications. In a 512-trial strategy comparison, full-prefix scanning and complete buffering detected all configured pairs, a 512-character window detected 96/128, and chunk-local scanning detected 38/128. Fixed pairs flagged 0/338 human-derived safe responses and detected 0/394 jury-labelled unsafe responses, confirming narrow rather than general harm coverage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Streaming LLM output creates a release-timing problem: complete-response moderation acts after text has escaped, while repeated semantic classification of partial text is costly and unstable. This paper studies a narrow deterministic construction where each danger signature is a conjunction of two lexical predicates, and the guard withholds the first chunk that makes both predicates observable. This approach is an exact release-boundary backstop for a small fixed policy, not a substitute for semantic moderation.

## [FUSE: Frame-Unified Stress Estimation from Facial Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10442) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FUSE is a novel framework that processes entire facial video recordings as a single input by fusing all frames into a unified 2D representation, eliminating the need for temporal windowing. It achieves up to 69.44% test accuracy on a 58-subject stress dataset. This approach simplifies video-based stress detection by removing the need to choose window lengths and aggregation strategies, potentially improving efficiency and enabling full-recording analysis. It could influence future affective computing systems that monitor stress in real-world settings. The method folds the temporal dimension into the channel dimension and uses a unified asymmetric-attention architecture. At a temporal stride of t=1, it processes 3,600 frames (120 seconds at 30 fps) as one input, with computational costs ranging from 12.48 to 348.78 GFLOPs across stride configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional video-based stress detection divides recordings into short temporal windows before classification, requiring choices about window length, overlap, and aggregation. FUSE avoids this by fusing all frames into a single representation, allowing the model to analyze the entire recording at once. This is similar to techniques that fold temporal information into channel dimensions in other video processing tasks.

## [AI Policy Convergence and Divergence Across National and Regional Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11006) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper analyzes 74 national and 3 regional AI strategies from a global scan of 205 states, using a latent-inductive coding approach to examine policy design elements (goals, approaches, principles). It finds strong horizontal convergence on economic competitiveness, research support, and ethical AI use, but persistent divergence on human rights, participatory governance, and human-centric principles. This study provides a systematic evidence base for policymakers and researchers to understand common practices and regional variations in AI governance. It can inform future AI strategy development and updates, helping to identify emerging policy design norms and potential areas of tension. The analysis covers all 205 UN member and non-member states, coding strategies along three functional policy design elements. Results show the African Union (AU) has the highest vertical convergence, the EU aligns on regulatory and economic priorities but diverges on human-centric values, and the Nordic-Baltic Region shows mixed vertical convergence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Governments worldwide have published national and regional AI strategies in response to the rapid expansion of AI. This paper addresses that gap by coding and analyzing strategies using a latent-inductive approach.

The paper introduces CARD, a framework that uses controlled generation with non-verbatim guidance on reply structure, comment function, stance, tone, and conversational variation to simulate realistic credit card discussion threads on Reddit. It evaluates the generated threads against real discussions using lexical, semantic, behavioral, and structural metrics, showing that CARD matches real distributions better than simulation baselines across multiple LLMs. This work advances the simulation of online discussions, which is valuable for studying consumer behavior and financial communication. It also demonstrates that structured planning and targeted revision can improve the realism of generated text, potentially benefiting NLP research and applications in social media simulation. CARD consists of a planner that organizes controls, a writer that generates the discussion, and a calibration loop that updates comment populations to reduce differences between generated and real thread distributions. The framework is evaluated on real Reddit credit card discussions, demonstrating smaller effect sizes and distribution distances across metrics compared to baselines.

## [TransitReID: Occlusion-Resistant Passenger Re-ID for Transit OD Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11500) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TransitReID introduces an occlusion-resistant passenger re-identification framework that uses a variational autoencoder-guided attention mechanism and hierarchical matching to enable automated transit origin-destination (OD) data collection from onboard cameras. The framework achieves 88.3% R-1 accuracy on a new transit ReID dataset and sustains 80-90% OD estimation accuracy in simulations and real-world operations. This work addresses the high cost and device dependence of traditional OD data collection methods by repurposing existing onboard surveillance cameras, offering a scalable and privacy-preserving solution for transit agencies. It could significantly improve transit service optimization and passenger flow analysis, impacting urban planning and public transportation management. TransitReID comprises three key components: an occlusion- and viewpoint-robust ReID algorithm with VAE-guided region attention and selective feature pooling, a Hierarchical Storage and Dynamic Matching (HSDM) mechanism, and a multi-threaded edge implementation for near real-time processing. The system runs on NVIDIA Jetson edge devices, ensuring local data processing and privacy preservation, and a new dataset with over 17,000 images from real bus cameras was constructed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 12, 04:00

**Background**: Transit Origin-Destination (OD) data are essential for optimizing public transit services, but traditional collection methods like manual surveys, Bluetooth/WiFi tracking, and Automated Passenger Counters are often costly, device-dependent, or unable to support individual-level matching. Person re-identification (ReID) is a computer vision technique that identifies individuals across different cameras or time points, which can be applied to transit environments to track passengers. Variational autoencoders (VAEs) are generative models that learn latent representations, and when combined with attention mechanisms, they can help focus on relevant body regions despite occlusion.

This paper characterizes the revenue-optimal pricing mechanism for dynamic information services, showing that screening via service caps is optimal and rationalizes common flat-fee capped access models. This result provides a theoretical foundation for pricing strategies in AI service tiers, expert networks, and analyst retainers, potentially guiding providers toward optimal revenue extraction. It also extends mechanism design literature by showing that product distortion is unnecessary when screening via caps. The optimal mechanism deploys a single, undistorted information process and screens customers through a one-dimensional menu of service caps. The paper identifies a convexity-preservation property of urgency screening and provides conditions under which per-unit price declines with the cap.
